# Changelog

Release notes for tagged releases. Numbers are measured, not projected;
methodology for all Roast figures is in [docs/status/COUNTING.md](docs/status/COUNTING.md).

## v5.7.0 (2026-11-11) — a `Value` of 32 bytes, arrays of Ints and Nums kept as machine words, `--doc-links`, and Wi-Fi on the Uno R4

A quick release of everything on `main` since v5.6.3: 317 commits in five
days. A `Value` is 32 bytes where v5.6.3's is 80, with every reference count
kept. An untyped array of plain Ints or Nums keeps them as 8-byte words.
`rakupp --doc-links` links every construct of a program to its page on
docs.raku.org. `use Arduino::WiFi` puts an Uno R4 WiFi on a network. Natives
in multi dispatch are ranked as Rakudo ranks them. Most of the fixes come
from a review of src/, an audit of all 217 closed issues, and the items of
TODO-FOLLOW-UPS.md.

Roast: **all 1,425 files** of `spectest.data` and **all 219,395** of its tests
with skip and todo left out (221,034 of 221,034 counting them as passes), in
one run on 7ad1c88e, the tag less the release's own commits, which change
the version and the documents. The file list is v5.6.0's, archived as
v5.7.0.list. Roast is `cf28a323c`, three commits past v5.6.0's `da425eb92`:
it declares one test more, in `S13-overloading/operators.t` behind a
`#?rakudo skip`, and loosens five type checks in `S32-list/sort.t`. One run,
not three, so no union list is archived.

The local suite passes all 2,209 checks on 7ad1c88e, with the
`--target=js` checks skipped for want of node on this machine; CI's legs run
them. 7ad1c88e passed every t/race gate and perf-guard as it landed. Measured
on the Apple M4 that measured v5.5.0 and v5.6.0.

**Not run** for this release: the benchmarks, so BENCHMARKS.md's tables are
still v5.5.0's and the figures under Faster are the ones each commit
measured as it landed; perf-guard against a v5.6.3 build; the compiler and
slim differentials; a second toolchain; the installed-binary gate; the
module battery; Raku Koans; the adopters' suites; the conformance sweep.
The module battery, documentation-example and ecosystem figures in the
README and `docs/status/` are the older ones they name.

### New

- **`rakupp --doc-links prog.raku`: every construct of a program, linked to
  the page on docs.raku.org that documents it** (a516dd92, b4bf1dc9).
  Keywords, routines and methods, operators, types (native types too: `int`
  to its own page, `int64` or `size_t` to its table on the native-types
  page), special variables, quoting forms and regex constructs, as a list grouped by kind with the lines that use
  each; `--html` writes the program as one self-contained page with every
  construct a link, `--ansi` prints it in the terminal with clickable OSC 8
  links, `--json` gives each link's span and URL. `rakupp doc prog.raku` is
  the same list. Nothing runs and nothing is fetched (50 ms for the
  3,111-line tools/install.raku). The parser decides what a word cannot: a
  prefix from an infix `-`, an item from a list `=`, the program's own class
  from a built-in. A method links to its invocant's type when that is known
  without running the program — a literal, a declared type, a sigil, `T.new`,
  a chain with fixed result types (`$text.lc.comb(…).Bag` is a Bag) — and to
  the type up the chain that documents it (`.total` on a Bag is Baggy's);
  otherwise to the page listing every type's version, with the types named.
  The links come from a committed copy of the docs site's own search index
  (src/raku_docs_index_gen.cpp, 5,251 entries of 2026-11-07, regenerated by
  tools/gen-raku-docs-index.raku), because anchors cannot be derived from
  names; 383 anchors on 131 live pages were checked and all exist. The
  playground's output pane has a **Docs** tab on the same code
  (`rakupp_doc_links()` in Raku.js): the editor underlines what has a page,
  the construct under the cursor is named with its link, Cmd/Ctrl-click
  opens it, and `&docs=1` in a link opens the tab. The playground was
  exercised against the native binary; the wasm build was not run.
  t/regression/doc-links.raku pins 33 constructs to their label and URL and
  holds every link over examples/ to its source bytes.

- **`use Arduino::WiFi`: an Uno R4 WiFi joins a network** (a47fc18c,
  d3ca553d, 6c3e145e). The name and password are read by the build, from
  the environment or a file; the board answers its status and prints its
  address, on both simulated boards byte for byte and in a sketch
  arduino-cli builds for the R4 with no warning. `WiFi.begin` waits until
  the router has given the board an address (WiFiS3 answers WL_CONNECTED
  before that, so a `say WiFi.localIP` straight after printed 0.0.0.0), and
  `WiFi.getTime` answers the network's time as seconds since 1970, so a
  board has the time of day with no UDP; the simulators answer
  `RAKUPP_ARDUINO_TIME`, or this computer's clock. The gate's `--arduino15`
  builds the R4's own sketches, matrix and network, whole with its core.

### Faster

- **Less memory: a `Value` is 32 bytes, not 64** (2073eec8; VALUE32-PLAN, the
  four steps of 2026-11-10). Every reference count is kept. On 64-bit macOS
  and Linux:
  - the tag block is 4 bytes: a native width is held in bytes, in 4 bits;
  - an enum's name and type, a Junction's kind and a Blob's subtype live in
    the cold block, which copies share;
  - the payload and the cold block are reached by 32-bit handles into one
    reserved region, which the new `BodyPool` allocates from in 64 KB spans
    and gives back to the system as they empty;
  - a Str of up to 7 bytes lives in the value's 8-byte string slot, and a
    longer one is a handle to a shared body. A Str of 8 to 15 bytes costs an
    allocation where it cost none.

  Windows, a 32-bit target and an AddressSanitizer build keep pointers and a
  48-byte `Value` (`-DRAKUPP_BODY_HANDLES=0` chooses that anywhere).

  Peak memory, against the commit before:
  - a million Ints built by `.map`: 134.6 → 73.8 MB;
  - a million short Strs: 134.5 → 73.7 MB;
  - a million Rats: 211.4 → 150.0 MB;
  - 200,000 two-element arrays: 70.7 → 46.2 MB;
  - 200,000 two-attribute objects: 122.5 → 94.4 MB;
  - `hashfill`: 44.6 → 33.2 MB.

  A small program peaks about 0.25 MB higher: the region's first spans.

  Instructions retired over perf-guard's 27 kernels, 32 bytes against 48: 18
  within ±0.6%, the call and string kernels +1.1% to +1.8%, `junctionwide`
  +4.7%. The steps from 64 to 48 lowered them on most kernels (to −4.7%).
  Over the tools/bench programs, 21 of 23 are within ±1.7%.

  `t/run.raku` 2,174 of 2,174; Roast 1,425 of 1,425, and again with handles
  off; the ThreadSanitizer stress matrix 37 pass, 14 known-bad, none new.
  **Not run:** Linux, an ASan build, the module battery.

- **Less memory: a `Value` is 64 bytes, not 80** (02e52fa2; VALUE32-PLAN
  design C). A Str of up to 15 bytes now lives in the value's own 16-byte
  string slot, and a longer one is a pointer to a shared body. The slot held a
  32-byte `std::string` and a pointer before. Peak memory, measured against
  the commit before:
  - a million Ints built by `.map`: 164 → 134 MB;
  - a million short Strs: 164 → 134 MB;
  - a million Rats: 242 → 164 MB;
  - 200,000 two-element arrays: 82 → 72 MB;
  - `hashfill`: 52 → 45 MB.

  perf-guard is level (geomean −0.49% over 27 kernels; instructions −0.04%).
  Roast's allocations are within run-to-run noise. A string of 16 to 22 bytes
  now costs an allocation where it cost none: `textsplit`, which keeps 20,000
  of them, uses 26.3 MB where it used 23.6, and runs 9% faster.
  t/run.raku 1,992 of 1,992; Roast 1,425 of 1,425; Roast under ASan finds
  nothing new.
- **A string literal is built once** (5d52a248). Its value is made on the
  literal's first evaluation and copied after that, so a literal over 15 bytes
  no longer allocates each time it runs. Evaluating a 36-byte literal 200,000
  times takes 200,000 fewer mallocs and 12–19% fewer instructions in the
  interpreter. Loops the integer kernels compile are unchanged: their string
  lane still makes a new value per element stored. perf-guard is level, and
  `prepend` gets back the 2% of instructions the 64-byte `Value` cost it.
  `t/regression/str-literal-shared-value.raku` checks that a write through a
  copy never reaches the literal.
- **A long string stored many times is stored once** (f0376fa6).
  - The integer kernels used to build a new string for every element they
    stored. Now they share the one they read, from a variable, an element or
    a literal.
  - A double-quoted `"…"` with nothing interpolated is now the cached literal
    as well; until this change only `'…'` was.
  - Pushing a 36-byte literal 200,000 times takes 4,536 mallocs where it took
    404,533, 5.9 ms where it took 15.3, and 23 MB of memory where it took 48.
  - `"…" xx 200_000` takes 6.3 ms where it took 27.8.
  - perf-guard −1.32% (`prepend` −19.7%, `sharedappend` −19.0%).
- **`%h.elems` is the hash's size** (9b294b72). `.elems`, `.end` and `.Numeric`
  of a hash built a Pair for every entry to count them, one allocation each,
  on every call. `say %h.elems` of a 200,000-key hash makes 4,595 mallocs
  where it made 204,626. A Map, Set, Bag or Mix, a role mixed in, a subclass
  and an augmented `Hash.elems` answer as before.
  `t/regression/hash-elems-size.raku`.
- **An untyped Array of Nums keeps them as doubles** (a3246b12;
  docs/dev/experiments/SELFPACK-EXPERIMENT.md). JavaScriptCore keeps a JS
  array of doubles that way, and checks the kind once in compiled code
  (docs/dev/findings/JSC-VALUES-2026-11-07.md). Here:
  - `my @a = LIST` of 8 or more plain Nums stores their bits, in the packed
    body a `my num @a` has, and `my @v = @u` copies them.
  - The interpreter's loop kernels and `--exe -O`'s unboxed loop lanes read
    and store those words. Each array is checked once on entry, and a bail
    puts back the words a loop stored.
  - Storing anything that is not a plain Num, a bind, an `is rw` element and
    every path that knows nothing of words unpack the array into the Array it
    would have been.
  - `RAKUPP_NO_SELFPACK=1` turns it off.

  ns per operation for the perf review's untyped kernels, off → on:

  | kernel | interpreted | `--exe -O` |
  |---|--:|--:|
  | dot | 100 → 4.9 | 25.7 → 0.48 |
  | matmul | 216 → 9.3 | 34.8 → 1.10 |
  | stencil | 272 → 7.1 | 39.2 → 2.56 |

  Bun's top tier runs the same three at 0.69, 0.81 and 0.79 ns.
  - Peak memory is not lower: the words are built beside the Values.
  - The tree-walker alone, where no kernel takes the loop, runs matmul 16%
    slower.
  - `src/Codegen.cpp`'s ceiling is 10,620 lines, up from 10,500, for the
    lane code.

  t/run.raku passes 1,997 of 1,997, and 1,997 of 1,997 with
  `RAKUPP_SELFPACK_MIN=1`, which packs every Num array. `t/exe/fuzz.raku`
  passes 326 of 326, 70 of them new array programs held to the interpreter with
  packing off. `t/regression/selfpack-arrays.raku` agrees with Rakudo.
- **A loop in a top-level block knows the top level's types** (59764c5a).
  Under `--exe -O`, a loop in a bare block, or in a `do` or `try` block, at
  the top level now types the program's top-level variables as a loop at the
  top level does. `my $a = 3e0` is a Num there, so `$a / $b` is a float
  division and the loop takes the unboxed lane.
  - Before, a bare block refused the lane or kept an Int lane whose guard
    never held.
  - A `do`/`try` block lost its lane when it became a scope of its own
    (37935a34): `t/exe/fuzz.raku`'s 20 `div0` programs, whose loops sit in
    `(try { … })`, have it back (207 lanes of 326, from 187).
  - A block's own `my` of the name still shadows it, in that block and the
    blocks inside it.

  `t/regression/exe-lane-top-types-in-blocks.raku`.
- **A loop that stores into a big packed array no longer logs every store**
  (b9e35683). A loop kernel or `-O` lane that bails puts back the array
  elements it stored, so it logs what each store overwrote. The log used to
  switch to a copy of the arrays only once it held more entries than they hold
  words. Now it switches at a sixteenth, since copying a word costs a fraction
  of logging one.
  - FORTRAN-LESSONS' axpy, `c[i] = 2*a[i] + b[i]` over ten million doubles:
    26.4 → 13.8 ms compiled and 82.3 → 71.4 ms interpreted. gfortran takes
    2.55 ms.
  - A loop that stores a few elements still only logs.
  - docs/dev/findings/FORTRAN-PACKING-2026-11-07.md has the kernels against
    gfortran 16.2.

- **An untyped Array of plain Ints keeps them as 8-byte words** (6501ade2;
  docs/dev/experiments/SELFPACK-EXPERIMENT.md), in the interpreter, as one
  of Nums keeps doubles, and `my @a = LO .. HI` fills the words from the
  Range without building a Value. None of a `my int @a`'s rules shows: a
  Bool stays a Bool, a result past 64 bits is a big Int, a gap is holes,
  and each of those unpacks the array. Against 62474b1f, interpreted, least
  of 7 runs: a sieve to 2M 2,396M → 1,395M instructions and 197.8 → 107.2
  MB; `my @a = 1..2_000_000` summed by index 617M → 378M and 72.1 → 24.5
  MB. A million Ints through `.map`, `.sort` and `.grep` cost more, 3,974M
  → 4,075M and 192.5 → 207.8 MB: the array packs and unpacks. Compiled code
  packs Nums only. `RAKUPP_NO_SELFPACK_INT=1` turns the Int kind off alone.
  An Array also keeps its `.WHICH` when its storage changes form, which a
  Num array refilled with more elements did not.
  `t/regression/selfpack-int-arrays.raku`. No array packs itself while
  worker threads run (00b2cafa, 7ad1c88e, under Fixed: crashes).

- **A method nothing takes, `.throw` and `die` cost less** (104ce4d2,
  9debac5c). X::Method::NotFound is thrown after the dispatch chain has
  returned, and `.?`, `».?`, `.*` and a coercion's other routes take the
  miss back unthrown: `try { Int.no-such-method }` 1,583K → 602K
  instructions, `Int.?nope` 1,244K → 20K, a `Foo()` coercion through COERCE
  1,186K → 38K. `.throw` and the built-in `die` are raised where the frames
  are small: `.throw` 4.0M → 0.6M instructions, a `die` in a method 1.5M →
  0.96M; perf-guard's method kernels −0.5 to −0.8%. With 104ce4d2, a hyper
  call over an Array or a Hash stores a Nil answer as Any, as Rakudo's does.

- **A call inside a `--cnp` kernel passes a native as a native** (a7d16c43).
  The kernel boxed each argument plain, so `f($n % 3)` with `my int $n`
  took a multi's `Int` candidate from the tier-up on, where the interpreter
  and Rakudo take `int`, and missed the dispatch cache the interpreted
  iterations had filled: `multiwhere` under `--cnp` 288 → 188 ms
  (interpreted 191), `multiwhere-steady` 2,130 → 1,382 ms. And `+$n` of a
  native boxes, as Rakudo's does.

### Fixed: silent wrong answers

- **`%%` with a width pads its percent sign** (a8f46ba4): `sprintf("%5%|%-5%|")`
  was "%|%|", where Rakudo has "    %|%    |"; `%05%` zero-pads. Finding E8 of
  the review of src/. `t/regression/sprintf-percent-takes-width.raku`.
- **An `exit` in a regex's code block ends the program** (1fae9cc8): `"abc" ~~
  / a { exit 3 } b /; say "still running"` said still running and ended with
  0 — in a `{ }` block, a `<?{ }>` assertion, a `<{ }>` pattern and a grammar
  rule alike. Finding A3 of the review of src/.
  `t/regression/regex-block-exit-leaves.raku`.
- **`polymod` treats a divisor the same whatever the invocant** (9454d20f):
  `(2**70).polymod(0)` and `5.polymod(0 xx *)` are refused, where they were
  (2**70) and (); `(2**70).polymod(1, 10)` ends at the 1; `(2**100).polymod(
  2**70)` divides by 2**70, where it divided by the int64 maximum. Finding D5.
  `t/regression/polymod-divisor-rules.raku`.
- **`.pick(-1)` and `.combinations` over a Range of sizes** (6f4bee22):
  `(1..5000).pick(-1)` is empty, where it was all 5000; `(1,2,3).pick(-1)` is
  refused, where it was an empty Seq; `(1,2,3).combinations(
  -9223372036854775808..1)` is the 4 there are, where it did not come back.
  Findings D8 and D7. `t/regression/pick-negative-and-combinations-range.raku`.
- **`nqp::div_i` and `nqp::mod_i` of the int64 minimum by -1** (3178a8ca)
  answer the minimum and 0 on every machine, where x86-64 trapped. Finding E5.
  `t/regression/nqp-div-mod-by-minus-one.raku` (which an arm64 machine passed
  before too).
- **A string escape past the last codepoint is a compile error** (91f64325),
  as on Rakudo: `"\x[110000]"` was a Str of four bytes that are no UTF-8.
  Finding G8. `t/regression/string-escape-past-last-codepoint.raku`.
- **A `**` count that no native integer holds is a compile error, and so is
  `**^0`** (03b3879f): `/a ** 2..99999999999999999999999/` matched ｢aaaa｣,
  `/a ** 99999999999999999999/` nothing, and `**^0` was read as `*`. Finding
  F5. `t/regression/regex-quantifier-count-edges.raku`.
- **An error a user `infix:<OP>` raises reaches the caller of `OP=`**
  (31565ae4): `sub infix:<+>(A $a, $b) { die "boom" }; my $x = A.new; $x += 1`
  dropped the boom, ran the built-in `+` and left $x a Num. Only a call the
  operator declined falls back now. Finding A4 of the review of src/.
  `t/regression/op-assign-infix-error-propagates.raku`.
- **sprintf's integer directives at the edges** (a8ac64a6): `sprintf("%d",
  1e30)` is 1000000000000000019884624838656, where it was the int64 maximum
  (and `%x`, `%o`, `%b`, `%u` the same); an infinite or NaN argument is refused,
  where it printed that maximum and 0; `%c` refuses -1 and 0x110000, where each
  became four bytes that are no UTF-8. Findings E2 and E3.
  `t/regression/sprintf-integer-directive-edges.raku`.
- **`chrs` refuses what is no codepoint** (3d5ae806), as `chr` does: `chrs(65,
  -1)` and `(65, 0x110000).chrs` were Strs of invalid UTF-8. Finding E3.
  `t/regression/chrs-refuses-non-codepoints.raku`.
- **A string's `:N<…>` radix that no int holds is no number** (ab615543):
  `+":4294967298<11>"` was read in base 2 and answered 3. Finding B2.
  `t/regression/radix-prefix-too-long-is-no-number.raku`.
- **`--target=js`: a NUL before a digit, and a Num from 1e15** (44502bd4):
  `"a\x[0]1b".ords` was (97 1 98) and `say 1e15` 1e+15; both are the
  interpreter's answers now. Findings H2 and H3.
  `t/regression/js-nul-before-digit-and-num-boundary.raku`, which
  `t/js/run.raku` transpiles.
- **A `temp` or `let` of an enclosing block is restored when an inner block's
  LEAVE dies** (fbfa041a): `my $x = 1; try { { temp $x = 2; { LEAVE { die "L" };
  die "B" } } }; say $x` said 2 — the inner block left without closing its
  scope, and the enclosing one drained its restores against it. Finding A10 of
  the review of src/. `t/regression/temp-restored-when-leave-dies.raku`.
- **`$blob[0] += 1` is refused** (9d2cf3bf), as `=` is: the in-place lane for a
  Buf element took a Blob too, and changed it. Finding A9.
  `t/regression/blob-element-op-assign-refused.raku`.
- **A subrule's action method sees the span its `<(` or `)>` marks, once**
  (016f8d57): `token x { a <( b }` over "abc" called `method x` with "bc" from
  1 to 0, then again with the right match. Finding F1.
  `t/regression/subrule-action-sees-capture-span.raku`.
- **`.log(0)`, `.log(1)`, `.words(-1)`, `.lines(-1)` and a `.substr` length
  near the int64 maximum** (6d62482c): `5.log(0)` is -0 and `5.log(1)` a
  division by zero, where both took a base of 0 for none; a negative limit is
  no words or lines, where it was all of them; and `"abc".substr(1,
  9223372036854775807)` is "bc", where it was empty (Rakudo dies there, on the
  overflow this had). Findings D3, D4 and D1.
  `t/regression/method-argument-edges.raku`.
- **`[max]` and `[min]` over an Int Range that excludes the int64 minimum as
  its end** (96c81b9a) are -Inf and Inf, where the end wrapped and they were
  9223372036854775807 and the start. Finding B1.
  `t/regression/range-reduce-excludes-int-minimum.raku`.
- **A `use` that failed fails again** (7e9c0387): `try EVAL 'use No::Such';
  EVAL 'use No::Such; say "alive"'` printed alive — the name was entered as
  loaded before the search, and stayed. Rakudo dies both times. Finding C2 of
  the review of src/. `t/regression/failed-use-fails-again.raku`.
- **`-$obj` negates what the object's `Numeric` answered as what it is**
  (0c64680d): a `Numeric` of 2**100 gave -9223372036854775807, one of 1/3 a
  Num, one of 1+2i a real. Finding C3.
  `t/regression/prefix-minus-object-keeps-numeric-kind.raku`.
- **`return-rw` wrote into, or read from, the frame it had just left**
  (7a09c596). When a routine returns, its frame goes back to a pool and its
  variables are cleared. Two paths used one of those variables afterwards.
  - Assigning through an `is rw` call wrote the value into the returning
  routine's own copy of a sigilless parameter.
  `sub raw-id(\c) is rw { return-rw c }; sub set(\c, $v) { raw-id(c) = $v; c }`
  does that.
  - A `return-rw` of the routine's own local was read back only after the
  frame had been cleared.

  On most systems the freed memory still held the old bytes, so nothing
  showed. musl's allocator reuses it sooner:
  `t/regression/rw-sub-lvalue.raku` crashed with SIGSEGV on linux-aarch64-musl
  in CI. Both bugs are older than this release's other changes, and ASan
  found them only once it could see the engine's own pools free memory
  (f3096c7d: an ASan build now hands every slab and list block back to the
  allocator).
- **Right after `await start {…}`, a worker still exiting counted as running
  code** (578572c6). For as long as its thread took to tear down:
  - `my $b := $a` would not share `$a`'s cell, and `$b.VAR.default` died;
  - a callee did not see a `my $*X` declared later in its caller's frame.

  Both depended on timing, which is why CI's Linux legs failed
  `scalar-alias-keeps-type.raku` or `dynamic-var-memo.raku` now and then.
  The checks that ask whether another thread can be running code now count
  worker bodies, which end when the body returns.
  `t/regression/worker-teardown-is-not-a-running-body.raku` makes the
  teardown slow on purpose (`RAKUPP_WORKER_EXIT_DELAY`).
- **Under `--exe`, a `do { … }` or `try { … }` block in an expression was not
  a scope of its own** (37935a34), as a bare block is. Two things came of it:
  - At the top level, a `my` inside one that shared a top-level variable's
    name assigned that variable. `my $j = 7; my $r = do { my $j = 100; $j }`
    left `$j` at 100 compiled, and at 7 interpreted.
  - A C-style `loop (my $a = 0; …)` inside one did not build, with or without
    `-O`: the generated C++ assigned `v_sa` and never declared it.

  A top-level `do` block that declares a dynamic (`my $*x`) now runs bundled
  with the interpreter, as a bare block there already did. Compiled natively,
  it died with `Undefined routine` when the block also declared the sub that
  read the dynamic. `t/regression/exe-do-try-block-scope.raku`.
- **A `for` loop over an array that another thread was pushing to could crash**
  (9b7a9824). Before looping, `for` checks the list for a literal `IterationEnd`.
  It read the array without the lock `push` takes, so a push that moved the
  buffer left the check reading freed memory. The values a loop sees while
  another thread grows its array are still unspecified, but the runtime no
  longer crashes. It crashed now and then on linux-aarch64-musl in
  `t/stress/ub-iterate-during-push.raku`, and an ASan build with the pools off
  reported it in 1 of 12 runs; it now runs 40 of 40 clean.

- **A `constant` naming a type is that type in a signature** (28257097;
  issue #220). `my constant Alias = Foo::Bar; sub g(Alias $x) { }` gave a
  parameter whose `.type` was a type named Alias, `=== Foo::Bar` False,
  and a signature rendered `:(Alias $x)`; Rakudo resolves the constant at
  compile time and answers Foo::Bar. The binder and the return check
  already resolved the name per call; `.type`, the parameter's and the
  signature's rendering, `--> Alias`, `.returns` and `.signature.returns`
  now resolve it in the routine's closure too, so a role's method in `class
  RI does R[Int]` has an `Int $x`. Found beside it, the binder dropped a
  parameterization: `constant AI = Array[Int]; sub f(AI $x)` took an untyped
  Array, and `multi m(AI $x)` won over `multi m(@x)` for one; both refuse
  it now, as Rakudo does. t/regression/constant-type-alias-in-signature.raku
  agrees with Rakudo 2026.09, interpreted, under `--jit`, `--cnp` and
  `--exe`.

- **`Array.WHICH` and `List.WHICH` are identity, as `===` is** (266d37c6,
  92561fe1; issue #221). They were the type and the elements joined by a space:
  `@a.push(3)` moved `@a.WHICH`, two arrays holding 1, 2 had one WHICH and
  were one element of a Set or a Bag, and `[1,2]`, `["1","2"]` and
  `["1 2"]` were all `Array|1 2`. An Array, a List or a Seq now identifies by
  its payload, which every copy shares and a push or a refill leaves in
  place; a typed one is `Array[Int]|…`, `Empty` is still one object, and a
  Capture and a Junction are still values. An identity is a serial now, not
  an address, which the next body took once a temporary was gone: `(1,2).WHICH
  eq ('1','2').WHICH` was True, as were two temporary objects, Hashes, Bufs
  and Instants. A Set of 200,000 objects takes 0.058 s where it took 0.050,
  and each live body asked for its WHICH holds about 42 bytes more. Found beside it, `(elem)`
  flattened the list it searched: `@b (elem) (@b.item,)` was False and
  `2 ∈ (1, (2, 3))` True. `t/regression/issue-221-array-list-which-identity.raku`.
- **`Positional[Int] ~~ Positional[Mu]` is True** (28824bb7; issue #222).
  Two type objects of a built-in parameterized role matched only with
  identical arguments, which is right for a class (`Array[Int] !~~
  Array[Numeric]`) and wrong for a role: Rakudo asks each argument on the
  right to accept the one on the left. A `:D` argument on the right takes
  only itself, since the left one is a type object; a nested
  parameterization asks again a level down. PDF::COS::Tie's
  `$att.type !~~ Positional[Mu]` warned four times on `use PDF::Content`.
  `t/regression/issue-222-parameterized-role-arguments-smartmatch.raku`, 52
  rows against Rakudo 2026.09.
- **A `--> Int:D` after a parameter is checked** (32b1ea01). Only a `-->`
  that opened the signature kept its smiley, so `sub j($p --> Int:D) { Int }`
  returned the type object Rakudo refuses, and the signature read
  `($p --> Int)`. `t/regression/signature-text-names-types-as-raku.raku`.
- **A sub beside a routine its scope imports is a redeclaration**
  (eb574770; issue #230). `unit module Mine; use Other; sub run-word {…}`,
  where Other exports a `run-word`, compiled and kept the local sub; Rakudo
  refuses it with "Redeclaration of symbol '&run-word'.", and in the
  reporter's 2,000-line module calls reached the imported one, so the output
  changed with nothing said. Every import road stepped aside for the local
  name, and a `sub EXPORT`'s map was bound over it. Now it is
  X::Redeclaration in either order: for `sub`, `my sub` and `our sub`; in a
  module, the mainline, a block, a routine body, or a class or package body;
  after `import`; for a `sub EXPORT`; and in `--aot` and `--exe` binaries.
  The error names the `use`'s file and line. A multi beside an imported sub,
  or a proto written after the import of a group, is `Redeclaration of
  routine 'f'. Did you mean to declare a multi-sub?`, as on Rakudo; a proto
  written before it takes the imported candidates (f395b6eb: eb574770 had
  refused it too, and Roast's S11-modules/import-multi.t with it). Two
  multis still merge, a local sub still completes an imported stub, and a
  selective export the `use` did not ask for, `need`, `use M ()` and a `use`
  in another scope still clash with nothing. It is raised when the `use` runs, so there is no
  `===SORRY!===` header, and a `use` in a routine body reports it at the
  routine's first call. `t/regression/import-over-local-routine.raku`, 38
  checks against Rakudo 2026.09.
- **A colonpair in a `«…»` or `qww` word list keeps its value** (5f6d78e4;
  issue #231). `« :x[1, 2] »` was the two words ":x[1," and "2]", where
  Rakudo has `:x([1, 2])`: the word ended at the first blank, so the pair
  never formed. PDF::Content's `my Array enum ColorName « :Aqua[0, 1, 1] … »`
  died on a type check, so `use PDF::Content` failed on its first line; an
  untyped enum or a hash took the fragments for its names. In every
  quote-protected list (`« »`, `<< >>`, `qww`, `qqww`, `Qww`, `q:ww`, `Q:ww`
  and a `%h«…»` subscript) a colonpair is now code, as Rakudo lexes it. A
  value group glued to the key, `(…)`, `[…]`, `{…}`, `<…>`, `<<…>>` or `«…»`,
  belongs to the pair, blanks and quotes and nested brackets included, and is
  code even in `qww`, which leaves bare words alone. A pair may start mid-word
  (`a:b` is ("a", :b)), pairs may chain (`:x:y`), and the next character after
  the value starts a new word (`:x[1,2]y` is (:x([1, 2]), "y")). A lone pair
  is that Pair (`qww<:x>` is `:x`). `qw`, `q:w`, `qq:w` and `<…>` still split
  at blanks. The pair is read by the ordinary colonpair parser, which brought
  four fixes to every colonpair: `:x<1/2>` is a RatStr and `:x<1+2i>` a
  ComplexStr (both were plain numbers); `:x<<a>>` is "a" (it was "<a>");
  `:x«a :b»` holds a Pair and splits what it interpolates; and `:!x[1]` and
  `:42x<a>` are Rakudo's compile errors, where they indexed the pair at run
  time. `t/regression/wordquote-colonpair-bracket.raku`, 124 checks against
  Rakudo 2026.09.
- **`--> Buf` refuses a Blob, `--> Hash` a Map and `--> Rat` a FatRat**
  (56a7962d), as Rakudo's return check does, interpreted and compiled. The full
  check took each sibling for the type; the compiled fast check
  (rtTypedFast's Rat) took a FatRat too, and so `my Rat $r = FatRat.new(1, 2)`
  stored. A `Buf` or `Hash` parameter still takes a Blob or a Map (left open).
  `t/regression/return-type-sibling-refused.raku`.
- **A `rule { … }` or `token { … }` kept in a variable, or passed to a
  routine, matches as one** (c2ec4411): `my $r = rule { a b }; 'a b' ~~ $r` was
  Nil and 'ab' matched, in both engines; a written value lost its kind on
  the way into the variable or the argument list.
  `t/regression/anon-rule-value-keeps-sigspace.raku`.
- **`%h{@b.item} = 1` stores one key** (6e8cdfbe), as the read and `%h{$x} = 1`
  did: the assignment's slice branch took any list a method answered for
  many keys. `t/regression/itemized-list-hash-key-assign.raku`.
- **`run` and `shell` refuse a named argument they do not take** (db0fdbca):
  `run(…, :ENV(%env))` ran the child with this process's environment, and a
  whole FatRoast campaign ran against the wrong directory unnoticed. It is
  X::Multi::NoMatch with Rakudo's candidate, as on Rakudo; `run(:timeout)`,
  rakupp's own, stays, and Proc::Async's `.start` is lenient in both
  engines. `t/regression/run-refuses-unknown-named.raku`.
- **`* ~~ D` curries for a user class with its own ACCEPTS** (b9c337a0), as `* ~~
  Int` does: it asked D.ACCEPTS(*) and answered a Bool, so `.map(* ~~ D)`
  died. `t/regression/whatever-curry-past-user-accepts.raku`.
- **A ternary statement's regex branch matches `$_`** (5d7e8be9): `False ?? /q/
  !! /c/;` left `$/` untouched in the interpreter, where Rakudo and `--exe`
  match. `t/regression/sunk-ternary-regex-matches.raku`.
- **A `let` is undone when a LEAVE dies while its routine unwinds** (2a32bb59),
  for a sub and a method; it kept the new value.
  `t/regression/let-undone-when-leave-dies.raku`.
- **A class's candidate overrides a role's only when the required named
  arguments match too** (370e0b4d): `m($a)` in the class replaced the role's
  `m($a, :$cast!)`. `t/regression/role-candidate-required-named-key.raku`.
- **An unpassed optional parameter typed by a subset is checked** (7c69a48c): it
  holds the subset's nominal type object, which the `where` must accept,
  with Rakudo's message; it bound the subset's own type object unchecked.
  `CN ~~ CN` is True. `t/regression/optional-subset-param-default.raku`.
- **A native return type is checked** (393aa64e), interpreted and compiled:
  `sub f(--> num) { "s" }` answered "s", where Rakudo cannot unbox it; a
  NumStr answers its Num and a Bool its Int.
  `t/regression/native-return-type-checked.raku`.
- **A numeric operator on a Whatever value dies** (1f5ed33a), "Cannot resolve
  caller Numeric(Whatever:D: )", as on Rakudo: `my $w = *; $w + 1` was 1 and
  `-$w` -0. `t/regression/whatever-value-not-numeric.raku`.
- **A Pair passed to `run` as a positional is an argument** (04db6160), "a\t1";
  it was read as an adverb and dropped.
  `t/regression/run-positional-pair-argument.raku`.
- **`%h{(1..2).item} = 1` stores one key** (2cbd6521), as `%h{$(1..2)}` does.
  `t/regression/itemized-range-hash-key.raku`.
- **`--exe`: `constant @L = (2, 4)` is a List** (84afd3c1), where it said Array
  and printed `[2 4]`. `t/regression/list-constant-native.raku`, built
  natively by t/run.raku.
- **`exit` runs no LEAVE phaser** (50f0b0bd), interpreted or compiled: `for ^2 ->
  $i { LEAVE { say "leave$i" }; exit 0 if $i == 0 }` printed leave0, where
  Rakudo runs the END phasers and ends. `t/regression/exit-runs-no-leave.raku`.
- **A typed `$` parameter's `.VAR` is its value** (019f922c): `sub t(Channel $in)
  { $in.VAR.^name }` said Scalar, Rakudo Channel; an Iterable is still
  itemized. `t/regression/typed-param-var-is-value.raku`.
- **A Whatever held in a variable stays a Pair's key** (ae41bc63): `($w => 1).raku`
  was `"*" => 1`. `t/regression/whatever-value-pair-key.raku`.
- **A Pod block's `.Str` and `.raku` are Rakudo's** (7279b2ad), `Pod::Block::Named<…>`
  and the constructor that builds it, where both were its text; `V<…>` is
  text run into its neighbours. `t/regression/pod-block-str-and-raku.raku`.
- **A supply whose own code dies runs its CLOSE phasers before the tapper's
  quit** (e9bb987d), in every whenever arm, and a `done` in it before the tapper's
  done; a source's quit passing through still reaches the tapper first.
  `t/regression/supply-close-before-quit.raku`.
- **Code cued on the scheduler that dies is reported and ends the process**
  (d3ab58de), status 1, as Rakudo's scheduler does; it was silent and the
  program ran on. `t/regression/scheduler-cue-die-reported.raku`.
- **A phaser written last makes the value Nil** (592afa33): `sub f { 42; LEAVE { }
  }` answered 42 and `(1, 2).map({ $_ * 2; LEAVE { } })` (2 4).
  `t/regression/phaser-written-last-is-nil.raku`.
- **A native int operator wraps with a folded literal** (e82801e8), interpreted
  and compiled: `$x * 2**62` on a native 3 answered 13835058055282163712, where
  Rakudo folds `2**62` and wraps. `t/regression/native-int-folded-literal.raku`.
- **A value-parameterized role matches its type** (1c681ec5): `R2[1] ~~ R2[Int]`,
  a class doing R2[1], and `.does(R2[1])` were False.
  `t/regression/value-role-argument-accepts.raku`.
- **`callsame` from a role's method stops at a role it does** (d6c9e30c), as
  Rakudo's: it called the composed-away method. `t/regression/role-callsame-skips-composed-role.raku`.
- **`.^find_method` and `.can` on Promise, Channel, Supply, Lock, Semaphore,
  Thread and the code types answer from Rakudo's table** (cd191fb1): any name was
  found; `Cool.can('chars')` was empty. `t/regression/find-method-from-table.raku`.
- **An itemized Range is one element** (21111219): `my @a = (1..2).item`, `[$(1..2)]`
  and `.flat` spread it, and a destructuring spread an inner Range.
  `t/regression/itemized-range-stays-one.raku`.
- **A `fail` in a `start` block breaks its promise** (fa0cc7aa); it was kept with the
  Failure. `t/regression/fail-in-start-breaks-promise.raku`.
- **A sunk `start` that dies is reported and ends the process** (bc6faf15), status 1,
  as Rakudo's; it was silent. `t/regression/sunk-start-reported.raku`.
- **A tapper that dies on an interval's or a Channel's value goes to the
  scheduler** (ea460d2a), reported with status 1 or to `uncaught_handler`; it quit
  the supply. `t/regression/tapper-death-to-scheduler.raku`.
- **A `$var` in a `regex`, `token` or `rule` value matches its value** (c7c4e564):
  `my $w = "b"; "ab" ~~ regex { a $w }` was Nil, and so was a token a sub
  returned over its parameter. `t/regression/regex-value-variable-atom.raku`.
- **An anonymous `rule` or `token` keeps its adverbs in `<$r>` and `/ $r /`**
  (853b606b): `"x a b" ~~ /<$r>/` over `rule { a b }` matched nothing; its `.raku`
  is `rule { a b }`. `t/regression/anon-rule-interpolated-keeps-kind.raku`.
- **A FALLBACK does not take a method Any has** (34e48087): `W.new.first` and
  `.elems` answered "fb:first" and "fb:elems"; `.throw` and its kin go to
  the FALLBACK of an object that is no Exception.
  `t/regression/fallback-after-any-methods.raku`.
- **A FIRST or INIT written last is the block's value** (8e5b2dc0), on every pass:
  `(1, 2, 3).map({ $_; FIRST { 7 } })` was (1 2 3); a FIRST runs before an
  ENTER in a called block. `t/regression/first-init-written-last-value.raku`.
- **A `fail` in a block written at the mainline throws** (8f0b138e), where it
  returned a Failure from the routine that ran the block.
  `t/regression/fail-in-mainline-block-throws.raku`.
- **Closing a supply block's tap calls no `done`** (e1bd580c), as Rakudo's; the
  inner taps the close ended called it. `t/regression/closed-tap-calls-no-done.raku`.
- **`~` of two buffers of one type keeps it** (a72b133c): utf8 ~ utf8 was a Buf,
  Blob ~ Blob a Buf, and a utf16 beside another buffer a Str or an error;
  any other pair is a plain Buf, and `~=` answers the same.
  `t/regression/buffer-concat-keeps-type.raku`.
- **A program's `multi` on a built-in prefix joins the built-in** (25f02957):
  `multi prefix:<->(Str $x)` was never asked for `-"a"`, which died, and
  `prefix:<->(3)` reached it alone; `&postfix:<++>` and its kin were Any.
  `t/regression/user-multi-on-builtin-prefix.raku`.
- **Two imports of one name in a block keep the last** (b8d8799d), as the
  mainline did: `{ use A; use B; f() }` called A's.
  `t/regression/block-import-last-wins.raku`.
- **A block's or an EVAL's `constant` does not shadow a CORE term outside it**
  (1c8c779c): after `{ constant Less = 42 }`, `Less` was 42 everywhere.
  `t/regression/block-constant-not-over-core-term.raku`.
- **An INIT in a routine runs once, at program start** (a5cb94fa), though it reads
  a mainline variable with an initializer: `my $n = 0; sub g { INIT { $n++ } }`
  ran it on each call. `t/regression/init-in-routine-runs-once.raku`.
- **A built-in that takes a slurpy or Mu takes a Junction whole** (3f7f2856):
  `@b.append(1|2)` appended 1 and 2, `Set.new(1|2)` was a junction of Sets.
  `t/regression/builtin-takes-junction-whole.raku`.
- **`.map` binds an `is rw` parameter to the element** (0579b6c2): `@a.map(&inc)`
  and `@a.map(&postfix:<++>)` left @a as it was.
  `t/regression/map-rw-param-binds-element.raku`.
- **A Nil through an untyped element's alias resets it to Any** (649cb2b1):
  `my $e := @p[0]; $e = Nil` left Nil in @p, and a Hash's value the same.
  `t/regression/element-alias-nil-resets.raku`.
- **A list assignment into a typed variable declared before checks it** (73940d6e):
  `my Int $a; ($a, $z) = "la", 2` stored "la", and a `:D`, a `where` and a
  coercion were passed over too. `t/regression/list-assign-checks-typed-scalar.raku`.
- **`.first`'s `$_` is the Array's element** (ba6f7edf): `@a.first({ $_ = 9; True })`
  wrote nothing and answered 1. `t/regression/first-topic-aliases-element.raku`.
- **A typed element written through `.hyper.map`, `.hyper.grep` or a `.kv`
  loop answers to its type** (4c47943e), and a typed Hash's value under `.kv`:
  each stored "x" into a `my Int @a`. `t/regression/typed-element-alias-guards.raku`.
- **`.polymod` takes a Junction divisor whole, and a Real stops at 1** (fd4495e3):
  `1.polymod(2 & 3)` is `(all(1, 1) 0)` where it threaded the call, and
  `2.5.polymod(1, 2)` is `(2.5)` where it was `(0.5 0 1)`.
  `t/regression/polymod-junction-and-real-steps.raku`.
- **An element type with a smiley is checked whole** (3341d028): `my Int:D @d;
  @d.push(Int)` stored the type object, and an `Array[Str:D]`'s element
  took one or a Nil; a refusal says "expected Int:D".
  `t/regression/smiley-element-type-checked.raku`.
- **`%h.values.map` binds an `is rw` parameter to the hash's value** (07e940ce):
  `-> $v is rw { $v++ }`, `&inc` and `-> \v` left %h as it was.
  `t/regression/hash-values-map-rw-param.raku`.
- **`andthen`'s `$_` answers to a typed variable's type** (43dbcefb): `my Int $x;
  $x andthen $_ = "s"` stored the Str, as `with` once did.
  `t/regression/andthen-topic-typed-variable.raku`.
- **A role a role does is asked about a multi beside an only** (57dad570): `role
  R does S` bringing an only `m` beside S's multi `m` was taken, and two
  parameterizations are named `(A, A)`. `t/regression/role-clash-through-role.raku`.
- **A Date or DateTime field that is no number is refused** (e8a8ba53):
  `Date.new(2015, "x", 1)` was month 0, an hour "x" midnight, and a Str
  timezone beside a timestamp a number. `t/regression/temporal-str-field-refused.raku`.
- **An `exit` on a worker thread runs the END phasers** (e205f9ae), and so does an
  unhandled error in cued code; `start { exit 3 }` ended the process with
  none run. `t/regression/worker-exit-runs-end.raku`.
- **An assignment through a list literal's item answers to the variable's
  type** (d5186165): `my Int $a; ($a, 2)[0] = "l"` stored the Str, and a Nil
  reset it to Any, not its default. `t/regression/list-literal-item-typed-assign.raku`.
- **A `where` clause is asked of a store through an alias** (5932b773): `my $w :=
  $wh`, `with $x { $_ = … }` and an `is rw` parameter stored past it.
  `t/regression/where-alias-checked.raku`.
- **`for $=pod` takes one block at a time** (3d4a3a91), where it made one pass
  with the whole list. `t/regression/for-over-pod-variable.raku`.
- **`=finish` is no block of `$=pod`** (57cc8291): one pod block and a `=finish`
  section made two. `t/regression/pod-finish-not-a-block.raku`.
- **A `flatmap` block that answers a `gather` spreads its elements** (0d87a53e;
  issue #235). `(1, 2).flatmap({ gather { take $_ } })` was `()`: flatmap read
  the result's element buffer, which a gather nothing has pulled from yet
  leaves empty. Getopt::Long makes a flag's receivers with `gather for` and
  collects every option's with `.flatmap`, so it refused each declared flag
  ("Unknown option --version") and its own test suite died on its first line.
  `t/regression/flatmap-of-gather.raku`.
- **`flatmap` is `map` then `.flat`** (5a0e9868), as Rakudo defines it. It
  spread each result one level its own way: a nested list stayed nested
  (`(1, 2).flatmap({ ($_, ($_,)) })` was `(1, (1,), 2, (2,))`), an itemized
  list, an Array's element and an Array inside an Array were spread where
  `.flat` keeps them whole, and a Hash stayed one element where `.flat` gives
  its pairs. `t/regression/flatmap-is-map-then-flat.raku`.
- **A `#=` after a routine's or package's closing brace documents it** (983b9524):
  `~&f.WHY` of `#| a` / `sub f {` / `}` / `#= b` was "a", not "a\nb". A
  declarator block's `.raku` is its constructor, naming its declarand, and a
  `#|{ … }` folds its lines. `t/regression/declarator-doc-after-body.raku`.
- **The JavaScript runtime's `substr` reads a start and a length as Rakudo
  does** (b07f37e2): `"abc".substr(-2)` answered "bc", and `"abcd".substr(1, *-1)`
  "bcd". `t/regression/substr-start-and-length.raku`.
- **`.flat` keeps an itemized Hash whole** (689728bf): `my $h = %(b => 2); ($h,
  1).flat` was (:b(2), 1), where Rakudo keeps `${:b(2)}`; a Hash a list holds
  bare still spreads. `t/regression/flat-keeps-itemized-hash.raku`.
- **The JavaScript runtime's Set iterates as its Pairs** (8f55e40a), as a Bag does:
  `set(3).map({ .key })` was (Any,), and `for set(3)` saw 3. Its `.flat`
  takes `:hammer`. `t/regression/set-iterates-pairs.raku`.
- **A compiled program steps a Buf's byte as the interpreter does** (9b0073c8)
  when the subscript counts from the end or the Buf is an element:
  `$b[*-1]--` died "Index out of range", and `@a[0][0]++` made the Buf an
  Array. `t/regression/exe-buf-element-step.raku`.
- **A class's `does W['B']` composes the candidate the arguments fit**
  (c0aa3e0e). With `role W['A']` beside `role W['B']` the class got W['A']
  and none of W['B']'s methods: a literal parameter took any value of its
  type, and the earliest candidate won (a run-time `but W['B']` was right).
  The choice is Rakudo's multi-dispatch over the candidates' signatures now:
  the narrowest types (an untyped role parameter is Mu), then a literal,
  `where` or captured type that accepted, in declaration order, then the one
  taking exactly that many arguments; none that fits, or two still tied, is
  X::Role::Parametric::NoSuchCandidate, where `does W['C']` and
  `role R[$x]` beside `role R[$y]` were taken silently.
  `t/regression/role-group-candidate-by-value.raku`.
- **An `@` parameter that is `is copy` refuses what its sigil refuses** (63b92af7):
  `sub c(@a is copy) { }; c(1)` made a one-element Array of the 1.
  `t/regression/copy-array-param-refuses-scalar.raku`.
- **A compiled routine binds an `@` or `%` parameter as the interpreter
  does** (ae3321f2), with or without -O: `sub u(@a)` given an Int held the Int,
  an unpassed `@a?` held Any, and an `is copy` one shared the caller's
  Array, its push reaching the caller. `t/regression/exe-sigil-param-binding.raku`.

- **Natives in multi dispatch are ranked as Rakudo ranks them** (52dc8d0c,
  a1706cb7, 3be0a0dd). A native parameter takes a native of its own family
  and never one of another (a `my uint` is no `int`, a `my int` no `uint`);
  a native argument takes its family's candidate over every boxed one,
  whatever `where` or smiley that has; a boxed value takes any boxed
  candidate over a native one, and a lone `multi z(int $x)` is
  X::Multi::NoMatch for a boxed 3. Before, `multi e(int)` lost `e($n)` to
  `multi e(Int $x where * > 0)`, and a boxed 3 took `multi f(int $x)` over
  `multi f($x)`. A native array's element handed to a routine is the native
  it holds: `f(@a[$i])` of a `my int @a`, and what `.map`, `.grep` and
  `.first` give their block. Compiled dispatch under `--exe` guards a
  native parameter the same way, where it sent a `my int` to `multi a(Int
  $x)`. Probed against Rakudo 2026.09 with each native kind.

- **A `*@` slurpy flattens a Hash into its Pairs** (b245ef4e), on every
  backend, and a compiled one flattens at all: `sub f(*@a) {…}; f(%h)` bound
  one Hash where Rakudo binds its Pairs, as did `+@a`'s single-argument
  rule and the implicit `@_`. Found by showcase/ledger, with three more:
  `.nude`, `.numerator` and `.denominator` belong to a Rat only; `$*USAGE`
  lists every alias, in Rakudo's order; and `--lint` reads a class past its
  forward declaration.

- **A compiled loop binds a sub-signature's named parameters** (2a6d5752;
  issue #162): `for (b => 2,) -> (:$key, :$value) { … }` said `Nil Nil`
  under `--exe`, with and without `-O`, where the interpreter and Rakudo
  say `"b" 2`. The compiled loop took every name by position; it binds as
  the interpreter's binder does now, through one shared function.

### Fixed: crashes

- **`.base`'s digit count and `.indent`'s amount are capped** (33adda5f) at
  what a Str has graphemes, as Rakudo caps them and in its words:
  `255.base(16, 99999999999)` and `"abc".indent(99999999999)` filled memory
  until the process was killed. Finding D2 of the review of src/.
  `t/regression/base-indent-count-is-capped.raku`.
- **`to-json` of a structure that contains itself is refused** (853fc96e), and
  one nested past the 20000 levels `from-json` reads back: `my @a; @a[0] = @a;
  to-json(@a)` wrote brackets until it was killed (JSON::Fast under Rakudo
  does the same). Finding E1. `t/regression/to-json-of-a-cycle-is-refused.raku`.
- **Ref, PayRef and PRef read the source's pointer before releasing their own**
  (7e251a18) in a copy-assignment: the source may live in what the target alone
  owned, and was read after that was freed. Hidden by the slab pool's free
  list; no test shows it without the pools off. Finding I3.
- **A `<{ code }>` pattern that calls a rule of its grammar parses a second
  time** (9c5c4d82): `grammar G { token TOP { <{ '<foo>' }> }; token foo { a } };
  G.parse("a"); G.parse("a")` died with a segfault at the second parse — the
  pattern's regex is compiled once and kept, and its `<foo>` remembered where
  the first parse's matcher, destroyed since, had found foo. Finding F2 of the
  review. `t/regression/dynamic-subrule-second-parse.raku`.
- **`+<` refuses a count past 2**31** (c6791c72), as Rakudo does and in its
  words ("Error in mp_mul_2d: Value out of range"): `1 +< 9223372036854775807`
  ran, taking memory, until it was killed. Finding A8.
  `t/regression/shift-left-count-is-bounded.raku`.
- **A shaped parameter's dimension past an int64 compiles** (60e9df1f):
  `sub f(@a[99999999999999999999]) {}` ended with "Internal error: stoll: out
  of range". The dimension is the literal's value now, not its spelling, so
  `@a[0x10]` is 16, where it was 0, and `@a[1_0]` 10, where it was 1. Finding
  G2. `t/regression/shaped-param-dimension-is-its-value.raku`.
- **An array element written after code that grew the array is still the
  element** (d389b329): `my @a = 1,2; @a[0] += do { @a.push($_) for ^100000; 5 }`
  left @a[0] at 1, where Rakudo has 6, and the process died at exit — the store
  went through a pointer into the block the pushes had freed. The same for
  `||=` on a computed index, `(temp @a[0]) +=`, a `given` or `with` block that
  assigns its topic (a segfault), and `temp`/`let` of an element with an
  assignment (a segfault at the restore). Findings A1, A2 and C1 of the review;
  20 shapes compared with Rakudo 2026.09, Roast 1,425 files and `t/run.raku`
  2,150 checks pass. `t/regression/element-target-survives-array-growth.raku`.
- **A sub-signature on a routine's or a pointy block's parameter no longer
  stops `--exe`** (9102ff01): `sub f(($a, $b))` and `.map(-> ($k, @n) { … })`
  were C++ that clang refused, and no binary was written; the program is
  bundled with the interpreter now. `t/regression/exe-sub-signature-param.raku`.

- **A compiled program's `start` blocks have a `$/` of their own**
  (86a5058f): `my $t = start { for ^10000 { 'a1' ~~ /\d/ } }; for ^10000 {
  'b2' ~~ /\d/ }; await $t` died with SIGSEGV in every run of its `--exe`
  binary, with and without `-O`; interpreted it was fine. A compiled
  closure makes no scope when it is called, so a worker's matches wrote
  `$/` and `$0` into the scope that spawned it while the main thread wrote
  the same slot. Every worker starts in a routine frame of its own now.
  Found by the audit of issue #209, whose program (three workers and the
  main thread walking one directory through one sub) died 20 runs in 20
  and passes 20 in 20. `t/regression/threads-own-match-var-native.raku`,
  `t/race/match-var-threads.raku`.

- **A shared Array refilled from worker threads** (00b2cafa, 7ad1c88e).
  Eight `start` workers each refilling one array with `@shared = ^20` died
  in about 125 runs of 200 (SIGSEGV, SIGTRAP, SIGABRT) once an Int array
  packed itself (6501ade2), and the same program refilling Nums 94 runs in
  100 before that, since a3246b12; a `my int @shared` filled
  before the workers started died every run, where Rakudo 2026.09 prints
  `bad: 0`. No Array packs itself or fills its words from a Range while
  workers are live, and a refill writes back the variable's own body and
  its own `of` block. `t/race/array-refill-shared.raku` refills an untyped
  Int, an untyped Num, a `my int @` and a `my num @` array: 0 deaths in
  1,000 runs.

- **An assignment's result is copied under the slot's lock** (295617a3).
  The store had let its stripe go by then, so beside a second thread's
  store the copy counted a body already freed. Under TSan
  t/stress's private-eval reported it 1 run in 8 and once died in the
  allocator; 62 runs after show neither. A Str of 8 to 15 bytes has a body
  since the 32-byte Value, which is how a plain `$v = "o-" ~ $n` came to
  allocate there; an Array's body was open to the same before.

- **`$b[*-1]` of a Buf under `--exe`** (8913094c; issue #163): `my $b =
  Buf.new(1, 2, 3); say $b[*-1]` died "Index out of range. Is: -1" in a
  compiled program, for a Blob too and for a store `$b[*-1] = 30`, where
  the interpreter and Rakudo answer 3. A `*-2 .. *-1` slice still bundles
  the interpreter.

- **`@!xs = …` in a method compiles under `--exe` again** (5913bf30), as do
  `@.xs = …`, `@!xs .= sort`, a dynamic `@*d = …` and a native `$*n = …`:
  the generated C++ named each through a local that does not exist, since
  500c29e2. `t/regression/attribute-array-assign-native.raku`.

- **`--exe` builds a sub that reads `@_` or `%_`** (fe5426ca). Its C++ named
  `@_` without declaring it, and the build failed outright instead of
  falling back to bundling. Such a sub has the `*@_` and `*%_` they mean
  now, natively, and `.signature` says `:(*@_)`. The interpreter's `@_` is
  lazy when an argument is, as Rakudo's is.

### Behaves like Rakudo

- **Two PDF::Content declarations compile** (1a3f36d1; issues #223 and
  #224), so `use PDF::Content`, and PDF::Lite above it, gets past them. A
  named sub nested in a method takes a `$!attr` parameter
  (`sub STORE($, $!text) { … }`, handed to a Proxy), binding into the
  method's `self` as an anonymous `sub ($!x)` there does; a sub at the top
  level or in a class body is still refused. `my subset S of ::?ROLE:D is
  export(:S) where { … }` reads the enclosing type, where the `::?ROLE:D`
  became a statement of its own and `is export(…)` a call to a routine
  named `export`. `t/regression/issue-223-224-pdf-content-declarations.raku`.
- **A class's FALLBACK answers `.name` again** (e7bbb97c; issue #225, a
  regression since 5.0.0). The built-in arm that makes `42.name`
  X::Method::NotFound threw before the unknown-name path, where FALLBACK has
  run since 0cc5b2bd moved it there; on a user class the arm now hands the
  name on to `handles *`, FALLBACK and `.^add_fallback`.
  `t/regression/issue-225-fallback-gets-name.raku`.
- **A signature names NativeCall's types as their `.raku` does** (32b1ea01;
  issue #220's follow-up): `sub f(Pointer $x)` is
  `:(NativeCall::Types::Pointer $x)`, as on Rakudo, while the type itself
  is unchanged. A coercion renders its target's smiley first,
  `Str:D(Int) $y` (it was `Str(Int):D`), and `--> Str()` as `--> Str(Any)`.
- **A program ends at once past a worker blocked in a read** (17d28f81;
  issue #229). `exit`, or the mainline's end, while a `start` waited on
  `$*IN.read`, `.get`, `.slurp` or an `is native` read(2) that nothing would
  satisfy took 2.2 s: drainWorkers waited out its whole 2 s grace for a
  worker that could not see the shutdown flag, then abandoned it anyway. A
  worker parked in such a call is now counted, and the drain stops waiting
  once every live worker is one: 0.25 s, where Rakudo 2026.09 takes
  0.32-0.44 s. `t/regression/issue-229-exit-past-blocked-read.raku`.
- **A type object where a needle goes is X::Multi::NoMatch** (c73d0b8d):
  `.index(Str)`, `.indices`, `.rindex`, `.contains`, `.starts-with` and
  `.ends-with` died X::TypeCheck::Binding::Parameter about a `$needle`
  parameter. The capture in every X::Multi::NoMatch message now names the
  invocant's definedness (`m(A:D: Str:D)`, where it said `m(A: Str:D)`) and
  a Bool argument as its adverb (`:i`, not `:i(Bool)`).
  `t/regression/needle-type-object-no-match.raku`.
- **A qualified enum's type object gists by its short name** (ced6a8ef),
  `(Idx)` for `B::Idx`, as a class's does.
- **`now` is an exact Rat of nanoseconds** (4c49bc3e), as Rakudo's is, so its
  `.raku` reads back `eqv`: it was a Num, `Instant.from-posix(1793331244.935708e0)`.
  A binary can embed `constant T = now` now. `t/regression/now-is-exact-rat.raku`.
- **`Str.new(1)` dies "Default constructor for 'Str' only takes named
  arguments"** (122eae41), as do Any, Cool, Match, Exception, Capture and
  Signature with a positional, and `"x".new(:value<a>)` is "a".
  `t/regression/default-constructor-positional.raku`.
- **`~` and `sprintf('%s')` of a built-in exception made with `.new` are its
  message** (46c4e957), as `.Str` was: `~X::AdHoc.new(:payload<boom>)` was "" with
  a warning. One with no message says "Something went wrong in (E)".
  `t/regression/builtin-exception-stringifies.raku`.
- **`for Empty -> $x { }` runs** (e00e905c): `Empty` is a term before a block or
  a pointy, as `rand` and `now` are.
- **`$b[1]++` steps a Buf's element** (5ae92da8), wrapping at its width, where it
  died "Index out of range"; one past the end grows the Buf, and a Blob's
  element is refused as Rakudo refuses it, compiled too.
- **An infix on a Whatever value is applied to it** (2d078a02): `my $w = *; $w ~
  "a"` is "*a" and `5 ~~ $w` True, where both curried; only a written `*`
  curries, as for methods. A `.&` call on a written star builds its own
  WhateverCode now (`*.uc.&{ $_ ~ 'one' }`), where it ran the block on the
  star and leaned on that curry. `t/regression/whatever-value-infix-does-not-curry.raku`.
- **Mixing two list-associative operators is refused** (a7739c52): `1 or 0 xor 1`,
  `1 || 0 ^^ 1`, `1 and 0 andthen 1` say "Only identical operators may be
  list associative…", as Rakudo does; `1 min 2 max 3` has Rakudo's message.
- **A signature returns the type of a written return value** (8fbd9e08):
  `sub f(Int $x --> Nil)`'s `.signature.returns` is Nil (it was Mu), `-->
  True` Bool, `--> 42` Int, and `&f.signature ~~ :(Int)` is False.
- **`loop(…)` calls a `&loop` parameter** (6497dca5), as it called a `sub loop`;
  without one, `loop(…) { … }` is refused, as Rakudo does.
- **`=for para` and `=begin para` are a Pod::Block::Named called "para"**
  (a3f00087) holding the Para, as `=para` was, and `--doc=Text` renders a `para`
  block as its paragraphs, not under its name.
- **A Vow's `.keep` and `.break` take their value** (b4cb4a06): `$vow.keep` kept
  the promise with True, where Rakudo dies "Too few positionals passed".
- **`.slurp` of a directory dies "Tried to open directory <path>"** (23822660),
  X::AdHoc as on Rakudo, and `.lines` of one is X::IO::Directory, where it
  answered no lines. `t/regression/directory-slurp-and-lines.raku`.
- **A subset declared in a package is named for it** (5f78f1f3), `my` or not:
  `K::S`, where it said `S`. `t/regression/subset-named-for-package.raku`.
- **A subset's unknown trait is refused** (f59ff84c), X::Inheritance::UnknownParent
  (when the declaration runs; Rakudo refuses it at compile time), and
  `SubsetHOW.new_type(:name<User>)` on a taken name answers `User`, not
  `User+1`. `t/regression/subset-unknown-trait-and-new-type-name.raku`.
- **`.AST.DEPARSE` of a `start` block leaves out its implicit `$/` and `$!`**
  (88751a81), which grew by a pair each round.
  `t/regression/rakuast-start-block-deparse.raku`.
- **Three package gaps** (85a90672): `GLOBAL::.keys` lists the package-scoped
  names (it was empty), a second `module A`, a `module` before a class and a
  `package` after a role are redeclarations, and `P.new` on a package says
  "You cannot create an instance of this type (P)". `t/regression/package-gaps.raku`.
- **A variable inside a string is reported on its own line** (728cf9cb): past
  line 200 it was line 1, and `$?LINE` after a newline in a string, or in a
  heredoc, was a line off. `t/regression/interpolated-variable-line.raku`.
- **`--doc` renders the Pod and runs nothing past INIT** (07dc2ee9), as Rakudo's
  does: it ran the program first. A `DOC` phaser runs only under `--doc`,
  `DOC BEGIN` included. `t/regression/doc-renders-without-running.raku`.
- **X::Str::Numeric and X::OutOfRange made with `.new` have Rakudo's
  messages** (0f3b9feb): the first was (Any), the second wrote its range `1 2`.
  `t/regression/exception-new-messages.raku`.
- **`use Test; sub ok(…)` is a redeclaration** (483370b7), "Redeclaration of
  symbol '&ok'.", and every routine declared twice gets Rakudo's one
  wording, "… Did you mean to declare a multi-sub?".
  `t/regression/test-routine-redeclaration.raku`.
- **An undeclared type-like name before a statement's block is refused at
  compile time** (422df3d1), "Function 'Foo' needs parens to avoid gobbling
  block", for every xblock (`if`, `for`, `when`, …), not only in an EVAL.
  `t/regression/gobbled-block-refused-at-compile-time.raku`.
- **`.AST.DEPARSE` writes `start 5` as written** (a63dab44), not as a block.
  `t/regression/rakuast-start-expression-deparse.raku`.
- **An unknown parent is reported in Rakudo's words** (53837aa5), "'C' cannot
  inherit from 'nosuch' because it is unknown.", and a role's unknown `is`
  is one too, where it was dropped. `t/regression/unknown-parent-message.raku`.
- **`substr`'s out-of-range messages are Rakudo's** (8660ca95): a start past the
  end hints "use *-10", a negative length is the "Number of characters
  argument". `t/regression/substr-out-of-range-hint.raku`.
- **`is hidden-from-backtrace` hides the routine's frame** (fa865d47), which was
  printed; `.backtrace.list` keeps it with `.is-hidden` True.
  `t/regression/hidden-from-backtrace-frames.raku`.
- **A built-in operator called by its name is the operator** (75326b8b):
  `prefix:<->(3)`, `postfix:<++>($y)` (which steps $y),
  `postcircumfix:<[ ]>(…)`, `infix:«<=»(1, 2)` died or did not parse.
  `t/regression/builtin-operator-called-by-name.raku`.
- **A type argument keeps its smiley** (a797a3c6): `Positional[Int:D].^name` said
  `Positional[Int,D]`, and `R[Int:D] ~~ R[Int]` is True.
  `t/regression/type-argument-smiley-name.raku`.
- **A parse error shows its line and a `^` under `-e`, `-c` and `--lint`**
  (6c0c4fa1), as a file run did, and most errors name a column.
  `t/regression/parse-error-excerpt-everywhere.raku`.
- **A runtime error in a string's `{…}` names the block's line** (e89a0e1e), where it
  named the statement's first. `t/regression/interpolated-block-error-line.raku`.
- **A `Buf` parameter refuses a Blob, a `Hash` one or variable a Map** (79b70d34),
  and `.Buf`/`.Blob` coerce a buffer (a utf8 had no `.Buf`).
  `t/regression/buf-param-refuses-blob.raku`.
- **A binding failure says Rakudo's whole message** (0cee5c8f): "Earlier failure:"
  and the Failure's `.mess` before a type check that refused one, and the
  typed-array hint for a Hash, a Map or a Pair given to an `@` parameter.
  `t/regression/binding-failure-priors-and-hint.raku`.
- **A parameter refuses what its sigil or type refuses** (0ca4968e): `sub f(%h is
  rw)` at compile time, a named `&c` given an Int, `Int %h` given an untyped
  hash, `given 5 -> @a`; `%h is copy` is a plain Hash, and `my %h := (a =>
  1)` binds the Pair. `t/regression/params-refuse-what-sigil-refuses.raku`.
- **A call with a named argument and the wrong positionals is refused in the
  run's words** (cae1cb63), "Too few positionals passed; …", where it said
  Rakudo's compile-time "will never work".
  `t/regression/arity-with-named-arg-at-run-time.raku`.
- **A compile-time X::AdHoc does X::Comp** (c2dcfeb9), `X::AdHoc+{X::Comp}`, and
  `class GLOBAL {}` is refused as every pseudo-package is.
  `t/regression/compile-adhoc-does-x-comp.raku`.
- **A package declaration is a term an infix may follow** (01673b79): `class A {}
  === A` and `class D {} ~~ D` were refused.
  `t/regression/package-declaration-is-term.raku`.
- **Two roles' clashes are refused in Rakudo's words** (846ae003): the roles last
  first, a multi by its signature (X::Role::Unresolved::Multi), and an only
  method beside a multi, which was taken. `t/regression/role-conflict-message.raku`.
- **X::Cannot::Map's message ends with its suggestion** (1695a062).
  `t/regression/cannot-map-message-suggestion.raku`.
- **A misspelled type's suggestions take in the native types and `Inf`** (75213a10):
  `Strr` suggests str too. `t/regression/type-suggestions-native-and-terms.raku`.
- **A plain object answers Any's list methods** (586a7f14): `P.new.max`, `.Seq`,
  `.is-lazy`, `.AT-POS`, `.classify` … died "No such method"; `.invert` says
  "… in invert". `t/regression/plain-object-any-list-methods.raku`.
- **A Failure answers `.backtrace` and `.mess`** (1c32f010), and an exception never
  thrown has no backtrace; "Earlier failure:" is the Failure's `.mess`.
  `t/regression/failure-mess-and-backtrace.raku`.
- **What dies at BEGIN time is Rakudo's X::Comp::BeginTime** (d9fb551b), worded as
  Rakudo's, an EVAL's statement-form BEGIN and CHECK and a dying constant
  included. `t/regression/begin-time-failure-wrapped.raku`.
- **An undeclared variable gets Rakudo 2026.09's suggestions or hint** (2108a08b),
  "Perhaps you forgot a 'sub' …", at compile time too.
  `t/regression/undeclared-variable-hint.raku`.
- **Date and DateTime strings take any decimal digit** (de67ca59), and
  X::Temporal::OutOfRange carries `.got` Int and `.comment`; a timezone's
  minutes are "minutes of timezone". `t/regression/temporal-string-digits-and-ranges.raku`.
- **A `:D` or `:U` refusal is worded as Rakudo's** (868dbe53): "… but got Int
  (Int) (perhaps Nil was assigned to a :D which had no default?)", where it
  said "but got Nil" or "but got Int". `t/regression/smiley-refusal-wording.raku`.
- **A typed Array's `.raku` writes an undefined element as its type** (564eec50):
  `Array[Cool].new(Cool, 1)`, where it was `Array[Cool].new(Int, 1)`.
  `t/regression/typed-array-raku-undefined-element.raku`.
- **A `:D` or `:U` element type stays on the container** (a776b42a): `my Int:D @d`
  is an Array[Int:D] and `my Int:D %h` a Hash[Int:D], where both were typed
  Int, and `@d[0] = Nil` is refused. `t/regression/smiley-element-type-kept.raku`.
- **Any's mutators and `.sum` on a plain object are Rakudo's X::Multi::NoMatch**
  (a343a825): "Cannot resolve caller push(P:D: Int:D); …", where they died "No
  such method". `t/regression/plain-object-any-mutators.raku`.
- **An OP= refused into a typed element names the container** (e56816cc): `@a[0]
  ~= "x"` says "of @a", where it said "of @a[0]"; `//=` keeps the element.
  `t/regression/op-assign-element-refusal-name.raku`.
- **`.^roles` lists a class's roles last-declared first, a value argument by
  its type** (0d6168fb): `R2[Int]` for `does R2[1]`, and `R2[1].^roles` is
  empty. `t/regression/roles-order-and-value-arguments.raku`.
- **A class or role refused for an unknown parent leaves nothing behind**
  (cd1e7aa9): `class C {}` after it was a redeclaration.
  `t/regression/refused-parent-leaves-no-type.raku`.
- **`.polymod` answers a Seq** (add45aeb): `(1, 1, 1).Seq`, where it was a List.
  `t/regression/polymod-answers-seq.raku`.
- **An undeclared variable in a declaration's initializer is refused at
  compile time** (024a0905): `my $y = "a $x b"` ran what came before it.
  `t/regression/undeclared-in-declaration-initializer.raku`.
- **`.AST.DEPARSE` writes `{ }`, `-> { }` and `sub { }` as Rakudo does** (f371f8b2):
  an empty body was "{\n}", and a pointy block with no parameters a plain
  one. `t/regression/rakuast-empty-and-pointy-blocks.raku`.
- **`.AST.DEPARSE` keeps `is export`, `is DEPRECATED`, `is default` and
  `handles`** (e4ec869c), each dropped before.
  `t/regression/rakuast-declaration-traits.raku`.
- **A multi method's refusal lists the candidates' signatures** (07dd7b4a), as a
  multi sub's did: `(A $:: Int, *%_)`, a role's named for its class.
  `t/regression/multi-method-nomatch-signatures.raku`.
- **An explicit `::?CLASS` invocant is typed by the class** (793c6d61):
  `(E:D $:: $y, *%_)`, where it was `Mu:D`. `t/regression/class-invocant-typed-by-class.raku`.
- **A typed `where` variable refuses what its type refuses as its subset**
  (edc36182), "expected <anon>": `my Int $x where * > 0; $x = "s"` died
  converting the Str. `t/regression/typed-where-variable-refusal.raku`.
- **`q;…;`, `q,…,` and `q.….` are quotes** (c1c8743d), as Rakudo reads them, where
  `say q;abc;` called a routine `q`; a sigilless term named q is still the
  term. `t/regression/q-quote-semicolon-comma-dot.raku`.
- **An undeclared variable's suggestions take in the routines in scope** (628ad9cb),
  scored as Rakudo's: `$prnt` suggests `&print`, `$frob` beside `sub frob`
  `&frob`. `t/regression/undeclared-variable-routine-suggestions.raku`.
- **A routine named by its `&` and declared nowhere is refused at compile
  time** (49808203), as a call to it is: `say "hi"; say &nope` printed hi first.
  `t/regression/undeclared-routine-reference.raku`.
- **An EVAL's undeclared routines are reported in Rakudo's words** (25975633),
  "Undeclared routine:\n    prnt used at line 1. …", every name at once.
  `t/regression/eval-undeclared-routine-report.raku`.
- **The JavaScript runtime has `flatmap`** (2867e40e), `map` then `.flat` and as
  lazy as the map, where it died "No such method 'flatmap'"; its `.flat`
  spreads a Hash a list holds bare. `t/regression/flatmap-spreads-lazily.raku`.
- **A sub-signature's arity refusal is Rakudo's** (f907b840): "Too many positionals
  passed to 'f'; expected 2 arguments but got 3 in sub-signature", "or
  parameter @x", "1 or 2 arguments", and the phrase again for each nesting
  level. `t/regression/sub-signature-arity-message.raku`.
- **A role with a Method::Also `is also` method composes again** (6004f84a):
  every class doing one died "Dynamic variable $*TYPE-ENV not found" since
  0b29ef00. rakupp called the role metaobject's `specialize_with` hook on
  its own, where Rakudo calls it from the metaobject's `specialize`, which
  Method::Also overrides to declare that variable; a metaobject that
  overrides `specialize` too is not called there now (rakupp runs no user
  `specialize`; the aliases are its own). The suite's Method::Also case was
  skipped wherever the module was absent, the suite's machines included;
  `t/regression/role-how-specialize-wraps-specialize-with.raku` is that
  shape with no module. Reported by Offizin in pull request #234.
- **`role W { }; role W['X'] does W { }` declares a candidate of W's group**
  (f11a9f04) over the W declared before it, where it was refused "Role 'W'
  cannot inherit from / compose itself", and a run-time `$obj does W['X']`
  gets the bare W's methods and attributes. PDF::Content declares its
  XObject['Form'] so and mixes it in at run time.
  `t/regression/role-group-candidate-does-bare-role.raku`. Reported by
  Offizin in pull request #232.
- **A method's `handles` delegation implements a role's stub** (88f81c1f), as
  an attribute's did: `class C does R { method Pages handles <page> { … } }`
  died "Method 'page' must be implemented by C", whether the delegation was
  the class's own, inherited or a role's; PDF::Lite answers
  PDF::Content::API that way. A role's method delegation in a later `does`
  slot, and any delegation from a second parent (`class D is B is A`), is
  dispatched too, where it was "No such method".
  `t/regression/handles-delegation-satisfies-and-dispatches.raku`. Reported
  in part by Offizin in pull request #233.
- **A class's own method named as a composed role's Method::Also alias is
  X::Method::Duplicate** (655d13dd), "Package 'WithOwn' already has a method
  'zed' …", as on Rakudo; the class's method won.
  `t/regression/method-also-role-composition-2026-09-02.raku`, whose other
  non-Rakudo expectation, calling a proto through one of two aliases, it no
  longer asks (Rakudo answers "Ambiguous call" there, from Method::Also).
- **A heredoc's body is no code** (f929935a): one carrying a program's `sub q($x)
  { … }` made a later `q[…]` in the file a call, "Undefined routine 'q'".
  `t/regression/heredoc-sub-text-not-declared.raku`.

### Platforms and packaging

- **`raku++` is a command of its own in every distribution** (e1245a6c,
  6785ecda). The product is Raku++; the command was only `rakupp`.
  `rakupp` is still the one engine binary, and `raku++` is a symlink to it
  or nothing, never a copy or a hard link. `cmake --install` now puts
  `bin/raku++`, a relative link, beside the binary, so every POSIX archive,
  the `.deb` (`/usr/bin/raku++`), the Docker image, Nix, Guix and a source
  install have it, and `raku++ --exe` compiles as `rakupp --exe` does.
  Nothing else claims the name, so unlike `raku` it is not a question. The
  Windows zips carry none, a zip storing no links: `install-windows.ps1`
  (with mklink), the wizard (with CreateSymbolicLinkW) and `rakupp upgrade`
  make the symlink, which Windows allows an ordinary user only with
  Developer Mode on; without it, or on a filesystem with no symlinks, the
  install goes ahead without `raku++` and says how to get it. `install.sh`,
  the Dockerfile and `rakupp upgrade` make the link when an older archive
  has none. Found on the way: `rakupp upgrade` run as `raku` on Windows
  looked in the zip for a `bin\raku.exe` it never had, and remade the
  `raku` name with `cp -p`, which plain Windows cannot run; it looks for
  `rakupp.exe` and uses the engine's own hard link and copy now.
  t/unix/install.sh passes 47/47 here against a tarball packed from the
  build; `rakupp upgrade` was run end to end against local archives, with
  and without a working `ln`. The PowerShell scripts, the wizard, the
  `.deb` and the image were not run here; CI's legs run each. The Homebrew
  tap's prebuilt formula installs `bin/rakupp` alone and is unchanged.

### Tools

- **The LSP's JSON reader checks the word of a literal** (6cb23670): `t`, `f`
  and `n` each skipped four or five characters whatever followed. Finding I6
  of the review of src/; checked by hand, no test.
- **A JIT kernel that failed to compile is seen as one** (cfa535d7): the
  command's status was that of the `rm -f` it ended in, always 0. Finding H6;
  no test (nothing here makes a compile fail).
- **`rakupp install` cleans up without `rm`** (20f0691d; issue #228). Its
  three scratch directories were removed with `rm -rf`, so on Windows every
  distribution printed "'rm' is not recognized" and the last cleanup died
  in an END with X::Proc::Unsuccessful, though the installs succeeded. They
  are removed in Raku now: a symlink unlinked and never followed, a
  read-only file made writable first. Found beside it, the directory an
  install from a URL unpacks into was never removed, on any platform; it
  goes at the end too. Not run on Windows.

- **Regression tests for fourteen closed issues that no test named**
  (ca60fcc7). An audit of all 217 closed GitHub issues found 171 with a
  test naming them, 21 that are questions, install reports or platform
  notes, and 25 code issues with none. Seven of the 25 had a test of the
  same behaviour under another name; #162 and #163 still failed under
  `--exe` and the #209 audit found the `start`-block crash, all three
  fixed above; the rest have tests now, each matching Rakudo 2026.09.
- **Gate 6b runs koans.app's Raku koans** (44459155):
  `tools/koans-gate.raku`, natively and on a Raku.js bundle.
- **`tools/run-bench.raku` times a slow lane once and kills a lane that
  does not finish** (bff5b5f6).

- Found with 31565ae4, the same before it: `OP=` falls back to the built-in

  $z -= 1` makes $z a Num, where Rakudo dies with
  X::TypeCheck::Binding::Parameter (an only-sub shadows the built-in).
- Found with a8ac64a6, the same before it: `sprintf("%c", 0xD800)` encodes the
  surrogate, where Rakudo refuses it.
- Found with 1fae9cc8, the same before it: a `last` or `next` in a regex's
  code block is swallowed where the `exit` was — `my $n = 0; for 1..3 { "abc"
  ~~ / a { $n++; last } b / }; say $n` says 3, where Rakudo has 1. And the
  Supply taps of BuiltinsSupply.cpp still run inside 42 catches of everything,
  which take a `die` and an `exit` in a tap (finding K4.1, not run).
- From the review of src/, probed against Rakudo 2026.09 and left as they
  were:
  - a Pair that holds an Array has its payload's ADDRESS for a WHICH, which the
    next such Pair gets — `(^5).map({ (a => [$_]).WHICH }).unique.elems` is 1,
    where Rakudo has 5 (finding I2). A serial per Pair is not the cure: tried,
    it failed S32-list/pick.t, S32-list/roll.t and the `Pair.freeze` group of
    6.c/APPENDICES/A03-older-specs/01-misc.t, because a Pair taken from a hash
    entry is identified by the entry's value container, which two takes share.
    The serial has to be that container's;
  - `(k => $o) === (k => $o)` for an object $o is True, where Rakudo has False;
  - a `die` in a Supplier's tap is dropped — `$s.Supply.tap({ die "boom" });
    try { $s.emit(1) }` leaves $! empty, where Rakudo raises it at the emit —
    and a `die` in a Supply's `.map` ends the program, where Rakudo hands it to
    the tap's `quit` (finding K4.1; an `exit` in a tap does end the program).

## v5.6.3 (2026-11-06) — Arduino sketches the Arduino IDE compiles, leaner objects, hashes and Rats, and the program's own operator in a metaop and in `~`

A technical release, cut from `main` mostly so that a released binary
writes Arduino sketches the Arduino IDE compiles. Every sketch v5.6.0
through v5.6.2 writes stops the IDE with `'T' does not name a type`, on
every board, so until now a board meant building rakupp from `main`, or
putting the sketch in a `.cpp` file beside an empty `.ino`. It also carries
everything else that landed on `main` since v5.6.2, below: it is not a
fix-only patch from a release branch.

Roast was not re-run, and its figures are v5.6.0's: 1,425 of 1,425 files and
219,395 of 219,395 tests on Roast `da425eb92`. The local suite passes all
1,965 checks on 94ee8944, and `t/arduino/run.raku --arduino15` all 44,
building every sketch but the two LED-matrix ones for a Due through the
IDE's own .ino step. With that binary, arduino-cli 1.5.1 builds
examples/arduino's blink and fade for an Uno and an Uno R4 WiFi, and its
raku-icon and t/arduino's fib for the R4, with no warning from the sketch.
No performance gate was run: the benchmarks, perf-guard and the other gates
were not re-run, and the figures under Faster are the ones each commit
measured as it landed, not measured again at the tag.

### Platforms and packaging

- **A `--target=arduino` sketch compiles in the Arduino IDE** (ab86fea9).
  Every sketch failed there, blink included, on every board: the IDE writes
  a prototype for each file-scope function of a .ino and dropped the
  `template<...>` of the runtime's helpers, so `static T rk_flash(const T*
  p);` stopped the build. The helpers sit in a namespace now, which the IDE
  leaves alone. Every t/arduino case builds with arduino-cli 1.5.1 for an
  Uno, an Uno R4 WiFi and a Due, with no warning from the sketch; the gate's
  `--arduino15` check now goes through the IDE's own .ino step when
  arduino-cli is found, and uses the Due's own gcc 4.8.3 where a newer one
  is installed beside it.

- **An Arduino sketch carries only the helpers its program calls**
  (73559a27). Every sketch had the whole board runtime above `setup()`, 95
  lines of it, whether the program called one helper or none; now it has
  the ones its code names and the ones those call, read off the helpers
  themselves. Blink, which calls none, is its includes, `setup()` and
  `loop()`: 21 lines where it was 116, as the IDE's own Blink is; the other
  t/arduino sketches lose 10 to 61 lines each. The firmware is the same
  byte for byte (the compiler had dropped the unused helpers already), and
  every case still builds with arduino-cli for an Uno, an Uno R4 WiFi and a
  Due.

- **A walkthrough for Raku on an Arduino** (2fa4629a),
  [examples/arduino/](examples/arduino/README.md): from a blinking LED on
  the simulated board, through a button and a sine table computed while
  building, to the Uno R4 WiFi's LED matrix, with a program for each step,
  each built for an Uno, an Uno R4 WiFi and a Due. And t/arduino gains a
