  second LED-matrix case (64114798), Fibonacci numbers in a 3×5 font whose
  frames the board composes as it runs, scrolling up to F(92). The
  walkthrough's `fade.raku` stopped the showcase grammar of Raku at
  `(pi * 2)`-shaped code, which read `pi` as a call on `* 2`; a term word
  never starts a call there now (94ee8944), and every examples/ file parses
  again.

### Faster

- **Objects and hashes take less memory** (a90fb35e). Every hash carried
  48 bytes for an aliasing generation and object keys almost none has, and
  every object 24 for a boxed value and a monitor's lock; both are behind
  one pointer now. An entry's dead flag moved into its hash (120 → 112
  bytes), and an object's attribute table holds exactly its attributes where
  it rounded up to a power of two. 200k two-attribute objects 609 → 512 bytes
  each (Rakudo 494); five attributes 1,444 → 1,009; two-key hashes 769 →
  663 (Rakudo 746). t/regression/hash-exact-first-chunk.raku agrees with
  Rakudo 2026.09; Roast 1,425 / 1,425.

- **A Rat takes 163 bytes, not 437** (300832c7). Its numerator and
  denominator were two separately allocated BigInts reached through the
  200-byte block every Value with a cold field carries; they are one 80-byte
  body of their own now, shared by every copy of the Rat, with the FatRat
  flag in it. 1M kept Rats: 437 → 163 bytes an element (Rakudo 211), one
  allocation each where there were three; that block is 168 bytes, not 200,
  for every other value. Rat arithmetic costs what it did (perf-guard's
  `rats` kernel unchanged), and keeping 300k Rats from a `.map` takes 24%
  fewer instructions. t/regression/rat-body-shared-and-written.raku agrees
  with Rakudo 2026.09; Roast 1,425 / 1,425.

- **A native array built by a loop keeps its elements as machine words**
  (fb43011a). Only `my int @a = ^N` and other list assignments made the
  packed form; a `my int @a` (or `num`) declared empty and grown by `push`
  or by index stayed 80-byte Values. It is packed from its declaration now,
  and a store at or past its end grows it. 1M pushed Ints: 87 → 24 MB peak,
  7.44G → 2.60G instructions; 1M stored by index 6.99G → 1.75G; the
  pushed row of tools/run-memory.raku 83 → 17 bytes an element, against
  Rakudo's 63. t/regression/native-array-grown-stays-packed.raku agrees
  with Rakudo 2026.09, interpreted, compiled and under `--cnp`.

- **A multi whose candidates all decline goes straight to the built-in**
  (5ebbe282; issue #47). Math::NumberTheory adds six `infix:<gcd>`
  candidates, none for two Ints, and every `$_ gcd $n` in its euler-phi
  scored all six before the built-in answered. The multi-dispatch cache now
  remembers "no candidate takes this shape", and takes candidates typed
  `Rat`, `FatRat`, `Complex` or `Bool`. euler-phi over 1 .. 3000 3.37 → 1.39 s
  (Rakudo 1.54); the issue #47 driver in run-bench 4.11 → 2.21 s interpreted
  and 3.23 → 1.29 s under `--exe`, against Rakudo's 3.33 s.
  t/regression/dispatch-cache-builtin-answers.raku agrees with Rakudo
  2026.09; perf-guard's new `multidecline` kernel 170 → 83 ms.

- **A `multi infix:<+>` over the program's own class no longer slows every
  Int `+` in the program** (00734769). Its candidate is noted when the sub
  is hoisted, above the class it names, so the class could still have been
  a subset of Int, and every core-operand `+` went through the candidates'
  scoring: 3.8× on a `$t = $t + $_` loop, 2.3× in `[+]`. The operator is
  now decided again once the class or subset is declared. Retired
  instructions, before / after / with no such multi: the loop over 5M Ints
  31.4G / 8.17G / 8.20G, `[+]` 21.0G / 8.87G / 8.89G; perf-guard's new
  `userplus` kernel 259 → 73 ms. A subset of Int a hoisted candidate names
  still keeps the operator armed (t/regression/user-infix-filter-after-
  declarations.raku, green on Rakudo 2026.09).

### Fixed: silent wrong answers

- **A reduce or a zip meets the program's own candidate for a built-in
  operator** (c43f88fa). With `multi infix:<lcm>(Rat:D, Int:D)` declared,
  `2.0 lcm 6` took the candidate but `[lcm] 2.0, 6` was the built-in's 6,
  and so were `[gcd]`, `[\lcm]`, `[+]`, `[max]`, `Z+` and `X+` over any
  candidate the built-in could also answer for, a `where` candidate over two
  Ints among them. Each step of a metaop now does what the operator written
  out does. Math::NumberTheory's `carmichael-lambda(360)`, which ends in
  `[lcm]` over a Rat, is 12.0 as on Rakudo, not 12.
  t/regression/metaop-core-operands-user-infix.raku agrees with Rakudo
  2026.09, interpreted and compiled; `[+]` over Ints keeps its fold, also in
  a program whose `+` candidates take its own classes.

- **`~` written out takes a user candidate over an object or an enum**
  (c77f05e7). With `multi infix:<~>(Rt $a, Int $b) { "rt-cat" }` declared,
  `Rt.new ~ 1` was `Rt<…>1`, and a `(Color, Str)` candidate left `Red ~
  "x"` as "Redx": the arm for `~` concatenated an object through its Str
  before anything looked, where `**`, `+`, `[~]`, `Z~` and `~=` took the
  candidate. Where none takes the operands, they concatenate as before.
  t/regression/cat-user-candidate-object-operand.raku agrees with Rakudo
  2026.09.

### Left open

- Found with a90fb35e, the same before it: a class deriving Int loses its
  value in arithmetic — `class D is Int { method double { self * 2 } }`
  gives `D.new(21).double` 0 and `D.new(3) + 4` 4, where Rakudo 2026.09
  gives 42 and 7 (TODO.md, Correctness).

- Found with 5ebbe282, the same before it: a user candidate exactly as
  narrow as a core one is taken, where Rakudo calls the two ambiguous —
  `multi infix:<gcd>(Int:D $a, Int:D $b) { "mine" }; 4 gcd 6` is "mine",
  and Rakudo 2026.09 dies "Ambiguous call to 'infix:<gcd>(Int, Int)'"
  (TODO.md, Correctness).

## v5.6.2 (2026-11-05) — v5.6.1, correctly named: `is copy` of a constant, `--cnp` and a method that changes its invocant, and the Raku.js archives

A patch release on v5.6.0, and v5.6.1's source with the version added. The
v5.6.1 tag was cut on 52f31000, before `project(RakuPP VERSION …)` and the
three package files moved from 5.6.0, so every v5.6.1 binary reports 5.6.0,
its Python wheels are `rakulang-5.6.0-…` (which the PyPI workflow refuses for
the tag v5.6.1), and its Raku.js engine would announce 5.6.0 in the
playground: the v3.20.0 mistake again. A published tag is never moved, so
v5.6.2 is that release built from the commit that names it (5fe10487).
Install this one; everything in v5.6.1 is in it.

v5.6.1's tag run left out two more things: its linux-aarch64 job failed the
example and showcase suite, which the same commit's `main` run passed, so the
release has no glibc aarch64 Linux archive and no aarch64 wheel, and the
Docker image, which waits for that job, was not built. PyPI still serves
5.5.0, because v5.6.0's wheels were never uploaded.

Roast was not re-run, and its figures are v5.6.0's: 1,425 of 1,425 files and
219,395 of 219,395 tests on Roast `da425eb92`. The local suite passes all
1,958 checks on 5fe10487. The benchmarks, perf-guard and the other gates
were not re-run.

- **The Raku.js archives are built again** (52f31000). v5.6.0's wasm job
  failed its Node smoke test: the grammar case wrote its two tokens with no
  `;` between them, which Rakudo and Raku++ both refuse. So v5.6.0 has no
  `rakujs-*.zip`, no Excel add-in and no Google Sheets download, and
  raku.online's playground stayed on v5.5.0's engine. The case now has the `;`.

### Fixed: silent wrong answers

- **Under `--cnp`, a method that changes its invocant changes the loop's
  variable** (b1b35cd7; issue #219). A kernel ran a method call with the
  invocant copied into a scratch scope, and a Buf's `push`, `append`,
  `prepend`, `unshift`, `pop`, `shift`, `splice` and `reallocate` write
  through the method's lvalue — the copy — so every write the kernel made
  was lost: `while $k >= 0 { $b.push($k); $k = $k - 1 }` kept the one push
  made before the loop tiered up. A loop variable invocant is now the
  variable itself, and a `my` of the loop's own is written back from its
  copy. t/cnp/cases/mutating-invocant.raku agrees with Rakudo 2026.09,
  interpreted and under `--cnp`, each of its twelve loops in a kernel.

### Fixed: crashes

- **An `is copy` parameter given a constant is writable, in every shape**
  (39f73f24; issue #218). `my constant X = 500; sub f($n is copy) { $n =
  $n - 1 }; f(X)` died "Cannot assign to a readonly variable or a value":
  a constant's value is readonly, as a sigilless `\y` parameter's is, and
  the mark came along into the copy. The simple path `is copy` joined in
  5321f41c kept it, b7c47a57's native wrap put it back (`int32 $n is copy
  = X`), and a named parameter, a default and a slurpy (`*@`, `+@`, `**@`,
  `*%`) never cleared it, so a sub, a method, a multi, a pointy block and a
  `for` loop's all died, and `sub g(\y) { f(y) }` on any argument.
  t/regression/is-copy-of-a-readonly-argument.raku agrees with Rakudo
  2026.09, interpreted and under `--exe`.

### Left open

- Found with 39f73f24, the same before it: a slurpy hash's values are
  writable, so `sub f(*%h) { %h<a> = 1 }; f(:a(5))` lives where Rakudo dies
  "Cannot assign to an immutable value"; and a constant handed to a slurpy
  that is not `is copy` dies "Cannot assign to a readonly variable or a
  value" when its element is assigned, where Rakudo, and rakupp for a
  literal, say "Cannot assign to an immutable value".
- Found with b1b35cd7, the same before it: under `--cnp`, a sub that
  changes a Buf argument (`sub f($x) { $x.push(9) }` called as `f($b)` in
  a compiled loop) still loses the write, through the scratch argument its
  parameter links back to; interpreted, and in Rakudo, `$b` takes it. And,
  interpreted, `my $d = $b; $d.push(1)` leaves `$b` as it was, where a
  Rakudo Buf is one object both names share.
- Why v5.6.1's linux-aarch64 example suite failed is not known: its log
  needs a GitHub sign-in, and the same job passed on `main` at the same
  commit.

## v5.6.0 (2026-11-04) — Arduino sketches, `--detect-races`, programs run from a URL, and native types at the untyped code's speed

Roast: **all 1,425 files** of `spectest.data` and **all 219,395** of its tests
with skip and todo left out (221,033 of 221,033 counting them as passes), on
Roast `da425eb92` — the same Roast and the same file list as v5.5.0, archived
as v5.6.0.list. Two of the three runs gave 1,425; the third lost
`S17-promise/nonblocking-await.t` to the crash it has had since v5.0.1 at
least (below, under Left open), and the union of the three is the same 1,425. The 188
tests more than v5.5.0's 219,207 are all `S32-str/indices.t`'s, and they mean
**v5.5.0's 1,425 files were 1,424**: v5.5.0 died at that file's line 29, and
the harness of the time scored it `[PASS] 18/18` (316e5855, below). It runs
206 tests now; Rakudo runs 294, because the file tries its `:ignoremark` cases
only on MoarVM.

The other gates, on the tag's own build unless named. The local suite passes
all 1,957 checks, and perf-guard against a v5.5.0 build is under Slower,
below. On d252f389, the tag less the version bump, the emit wait of 1979aacc
and 50a0d8a3, and one test's timing (d9d21499): the compiler agrees with the
interpreter on every `-O` kernel; the slim size checks pass, at budgets raised
to 13.0 MB for `--slim=-all` and 13.25 MB for a bare `--slim` (624b1f5d: the
`-all` hello went from 12,762,920 bytes at v5.5.0 to 13,419,640, +5.15%,
spread over the cycle's work and led by InterpreterBinding.cpp and
InterpreterCalls.cpp); t/aot passes 18 of 18; the installed-binary gate passes
its 13 offline checks; and Raku Koans fails the three koans it has failed since
v5.3.0, whose solutions Rakudo refuses too. On 3a9c1b6f: `--cpp` output fails
to compile for 57 accepted programs, 55 of them at v5.5.0 too and the other 2
new test files that reach older gaps; the slim differential finds 1,290 of 1,324
programs identical slim and full, and no slim cut that changes one (of the
rest, 1 prints a timing, 20 do not compile, 11 differ from themselves run to
run, and 2 need a server or the network); and the adopters' suites are where
v5.5.0 leaves them on this machine (iz4's t/07-install fails under both). CI
is green at the tag on every job, the GCC and MinGW builds among them. Not
run: the module battery, the DBIish leg of the installed-binary gate and the
conformance sweep. The module battery, documentation-example, `-O` harness and
ecosystem figures in the README and `docs/status/` are v5.5.0's or older, as
labelled, and BENCHMARKS.md was not re-measured.

**Measured on the M4 again.** The machine of record, an Apple M3, is still out
of reach, so the gates ran on the Apple M4 that measured v5.5.0, against a
v5.5.0 build on the same machine.

### Faster

From docs/dev/findings/FLOAT-HEAVY-ISSUES-2026-10-22.md, issues 9, 11, 16, 18,
19 and 20. Figures are ns per call of that file's probe scripts, best of 3,
against a build of v5.5.0 in the same sitting, `--exe -O` unless they say
interpreted.

- **A lexical `my sub` is called directly** (d7c6d9d5). Every call, its own
  recursive ones included, found `&name` in the runtime env and went through
  callCallable; now a call site in its scope reaches the body through a weak
  handle, and takes the old path once that scope has gone. A recursion of
  2**10 leaves 210.6 → 42.5 µs (a top-level sub: 39.9).
- **Native types compile at the untyped code's speed** (d7c6d9d5). `+ - * /` on
  natives have a helper per operator instead of rtNativeArith's dispatch on
  the operator's text, a typed parameter that already conforms is not moved
  or looked up, and a native store compares no type names: `(num $a, num $b
  --> num)` 29.1 → 10.0 (untyped 9.7), `(Num $a, Num $b --> Num)` 22.3 →
  9.7, `num < num` 19.5 → 6.2, `(int $m --> int)` 10.6 → 5.5, a `num` float
  loop 28.5 → 9.5 (untyped 8.7).
- **`.elems`, `.defined` and a one-argument `.push` are answered at the call
  site** (d7c6d9d5), by the same tests methodCall's own first arms make:
  `@a.elems` 24.9 → 8.0, `$x.defined` 9.7 → 6.4, `my @a`, three `.push` and
  return 89.8 → 54.1.
- **A sub with a plain `@` or `%` parameter takes the direct call**
  (d7c6d9d5): `sub (@x)` 16.8 → 3.9 (`sub ($x)`: 3.8). Summing 10 Nums with
  `for @a -> $x` 168.6 → 148.8, with `while` and `@a[$i]` 170.6 → 155.2.
- **Prefix `-` has a Num and machine-Int arm in both engines** (d7c6d9d5): `-$x`
  12.9 → 5.2 compiled (`0e0 - $x`: 6.5), 143.5 → 128.6 interpreted (126.9).
- **A top-level `constant` holding a machine Int is a literal to the int
  kernels** (d7c6d9d5), compiled and interpreted: two constant references in a
  sub 9.2 → 1.5 compiled, 213.9 → 5.7 interpreted (two literals: 1.7 / 5.5).
- **A sub typed `num`, `int`, `Num`, `Int` or `Str` takes the interpreter's
  lean call path** (3e97932e; issue 17), when each argument already is of its
  parameter's type, and an argument that is not goes the general way. Against
  d7c6d9d5, interpreted: `(num $a, num $b --> num)` 387.1 → 152.6 (untyped
  144.8), `(Num $a, Num $b --> Num)` 360.8 → 148.3, `num < num` 245.2 → 143.1.
- **A loop kernel takes `int` and `num` variables** (3e97932e; issue 17), at
  full width: it bails where a result leaves int64, and the native path then
  wraps. Interpreted, the issue-15 float loops 1,024 → 292 and 2,332 → 581
  (untyped 282 and 407), and perf-guard's strscan, which counts in a `my
  int`, 96.4 → 6.0 ms.
- **`for ^$n -> int $i` and `-> Int $i` run as `-> $i` does** (07775af3;
  FORTRAN-LESSONS-2026-10-15 item 1a). A typed loop variable bound each
  iteration through the general binder and reached no kernel; a lone `int`,
  `int64` or `Int` parameter over an integer Range now takes the counted
  path, the loop kernels and the `--cnp` site, keeping what the binding
  gave it (read-only, and a native `int` wraps). Against a87a6b73, best of
  3: `$s = $s + $i` over 10M, interpreted, 1.211 → 0.018 s with `-> int
  $i` (untyped 0.017); a hash-increment body no kernel takes, 1M, 0.217 →
  0.023 s (untyped 0.020); the findings file's `--cnp` dot loop over 10M
  `num`s 1.860 → 0.144 s (untyped 0.143).
- **`--exe -O` gives `for ^$n -> int $i` and `-> Int $i` the unboxed loop
  lane** (c95d9332), as it gives `-> $i`; the typed loop ran boxed through
  the typed binder. `$s = $s + $i` in a sub over 10M, against a87a6b73, best
  of 3: 60.6 → 2.2 ms (untyped 2.2).
- **`--exe -O` gives a `my int` / `my num` declared in a loop header the
  unboxed loop lane** (d0ba733b; FORTRAN-LESSONS-2026-10-15 item 1b). `loop
  (my int $i = 0; …)` ran boxed, because the name outlives the loop and the
  lane's store-back lost the native's width; it now rebuilds the native, so
  `$i` after the loop still wraps. The findings file's `sub f(int $nn -->
  num)` over 10M, against a87a6b73, best of 3: 240 → 5.1 ms (untyped 5.1).
- **`--exe -O` gives a `my num` the loop writes, and a `my num` in a sub, a
  float lane slot** (b81021ba; PERF-REVIEW-2026-11-02 §6 item 1). The lane
  typed a slot by what reached it inside the loop, so `$g = -$g` left a
  `my num $g` an Int slot whose guard failed on every entry, and the loop
  ran boxed. A slot now takes the type the parser resolved its declaration
  to. The review's numloop-n over 200M, against fe9237a8, best of 3: 7.82 →
  0.162 s (C at `-O2`: 0.158 s; untyped 0.169 s).
- **`--exe` compiles a routine with a `:D`/`:U`, `where` or subset variable,
  each store asking the check inline** (762ce0e2). A routine declaring a
  `:D`/`:U` or `where` variable ran interpreted, and a subset's store ran
  its `where` through the interpreter. The parser now gives each use its
  declaration's smiley and `where`, and a compiled store asks the `where` (a
  WhateverCode's body or a block's one expression inline, anything else
  smartmatched), the smiley and the type, in the interpreter's order. A
  `where` that reads another variable, and a write that would skip the check
  (a list assignment, a binding, a `cas` or `⚛=`, an `is rw` write-back),
  still run interpreted. 3M iterations of `$s += 3` in a sub, against
  576cfe1b, best of 3: `my Int:D $s` 0.34 → 0.008 s,
  `my Int $s where * < 10**12` 1.19 → 0.065 s, a subset's 0.97 → 0.057 s
  (Rakudo 0.77, 0.87, 0.88 s). t/regression/exe-constrained-var-native.raku
  agrees with Rakudo 2026.09, whole and bundled.
- **A packed `int` / `num` array keeps its words under a computed key**
  (5e5d80ec). `@a[$i + 1]`, read or stored, unpacked the array for good in
  the interpreter, so a `--cnp` loop entered after it read boxed Values. The
  key is still evaluated once.
- **`--cnp` reads and writes a packed array's word with no call** (4405241a;
  FORTRAN-LESSONS-2026-10-15 item 2), its fixed facts checked once as the
  register is loaded. Against a87a6b73, best of 3, 10M iterations: the
  findings file's dot loop 140.9 → 58.6 ms, one read 80.7 → 45.7 ms (no
  read: 42.2), axpy 178.5 → 64.5 ms, a three-point stencil over 1M 103.8 →
  33.9 ms, and its flat 200×200 matmul 419.0 → 60.4 ms (with 5e5d80ec).
- **`my num @c = @b` copies a packed array's words** (7fcca8a8) and leaves
  @b packed; it read @b's Values, which unpacked it for good. Against
  a87a6b73, best of 3, 10M elements under `--cnp`: the findings file's
  axpy-loop, which starts that way, 403.3 → 64.7 ms, and reading @b after the
  copy 258.6 → 44.9 ms.

The next nine entries measure `--exe -O` against the build before each commit,
best of 5 interleaved, on one program of three routines (a recursion of
about 500 calls over four native parameters updating file-scope tables at
its leaves, a loop over one array, and an integer routine), its arrays
plain before 500c29e2 and native from it, unless they say otherwise.

- **A bundled program's routine that steps or stores into an outer variable
  stays native** (42f79e98). `$n++`, `$n = $n + 1` and the like under -O, and
  any store into a native outer, named a C++ local a routine of a bundled
  program has not got, and the routine ran interpreted; the recursion ran
  2.3× faster.
- **Native `%`, `div`, and a `num` stored into an `int`, are inline in
  compiled code** (42f79e98): `%` and `div` built a string from the operator
  on every call, and the store took the general conversion. An Int or Str
  subscript key makes no call. A routine of `int` locals doing integer
  arithmetic on a `num` argument: 2,659 → 1,459 instructions a call.
- **A compiled native store of a native `+ - *` skips the store's checks**
  (b9108e1d) when the value can only be a fresh number of the target's kind:
  that routine 1,459 → 1,317 instructions a call (untyped 1,294).
- **A sub whose parameters are all full-width `int` / `num` compiles to a
  plain C++ function over int64_t and double** (0b9950b1), beside its Value
  function; a call of another such sub is a direct C++ call. A statement
  whose native operation could answer other than a number keeps the boxed
  form. The recursion 110.3 → 59.8 µs a call (plain `--exe` 220.5 → 64.0),
  the integer routine 134.5 → 75.0 ns; untyped code retires the same
  instructions.
- **`my num @a` / `my int @a` compile under `--exe`** (500c29e2): a typed `@`
  declaration bundled the whole program with the interpreter. A full-width
  native array is built and filled as the interpreter does (one routine for
  both engines now), keeps its words, and an element read or store uses them
  while the array is packed. The recursion over native arrays 371.7 → 33.3 µs
  a call, the loop 6.64 → 1.07 µs, the integer routine 580.8 → 72.0 ns; the
  binary 29.91G → 3.06G instructions. A `num @x` parameter still bundles.
  Every compiled store into an element (`=`, `op=`, `++`) converts, wraps or
  refuses as the interpreter's does (80ea9030): until then one the word could
  not take went to the element's place, which took a Str, a Rat, a Failure or
  an Int too wide, kept a Bool, and did not wrap a step.
- **A native routine reads and writes a native array's element as its word**
  (1b182b8e), and a `/` with a Num on either side divides as a double, behind
  the runtime tests that make that the answer (the array packed, the index in
  range, a divisor not zero); otherwise the statement runs boxed, as before.
  A routine ending in a loop gets its native function. The recursion 33.5 →
  14.9 µs a call, the loop 1.10 → 0.64 µs; the binary 3.06G → 1.48G
  instructions. The same program over plain arrays, and untyped, retire the
  same instructions as before.
- **A native routine's read of a file-scope native and its element tests are
  inline, and it makes no `$_` it does not read** (828e52d5): the read's
  conversion sat in the same function as its test, which clang called out of
  line, and every call built and destroyed the routine's own `$_`. The binary
  1.500G → 1.225G instructions.
- **A native loop checks its arrays' facts once, on entry** (4d1c7cc3): what
  an element's test asked at every access (packed, of its kind, no default,
  no worker running) is asked as the loop starts and again after anything
  boxed or a native call runs in it, and an element's test is its index
  against the length kept. While a worker runs, the per-access tests answer,
  as before. The recursion 12,892 → 8,604 ns a call, the binary 1.215G →
  0.851G instructions.
- **A top-level `loop` or `while` over native arrays, or calling a native
  routine, compiles as a native routine's loop does** (d972abe7): native
  locals for its header's `my int`, the facts on entry, direct calls. A
  statement in it that runs boxed may declare nothing, and a program with a
  CATCH, LEAVE or END at its top level keeps its loops as they were. The
  loop 589 → 55.2 ns a call, the integer routine 75.0 → 14.6 ns (over plain
  arrays 58.0 → 14.2), the binary 0.853G → 0.767G instructions.
- **`next`, `last`, `redo` and `return` written as an operand set the flag
  the statement form sets** (48b76d56; issue #195): `my $x = f($i) // next`,
  `f($i) or next`, `my $x = f($i) // return 0`, a ternary branch. Each threw
  a C++ exception through the interpreter's frames, which macOS's unwinder
  makes 30-65 µs a jump. The walk that decides now goes on past a call on
  the left, and the general assignment path stores nothing once the flag is
  up. µs per skipped iteration, the issue's script, interpreted, against
  4083b26b: `// next` 67.4 → 0.7, `or next` 33.7 → 0.4, `// return` 62.9 →
  0.8 (`next without`, the statement form, 0.9). That script's `--exe`
  fell back to the interpreter, so it gained the same. Still thrown: a block
  inside the statement (`try { … } // next`), a compound or list
  assignment and a call argument; compiled code, until aa0f3618 below.
- **`--exe` compiles `next`, `last` and `return` written as an operand to
  the statement form's own jump** (aa0f3618; issue #195): `my $x = f($i) //
  next` is `$tmp = f($i); if (!rtIsDefined($tmp)) continue;` and then the
  store, `f($i) or return 0` a C++ `return`, a ternary branch an `if`. A
  `next` there was an exception (rtThrowNext), and a `return` was refused,
  which sent the whole program to `--aot` and kept a module's routine
  interpreted. µs per skipped iteration, the issue's script, compiled, plain
  and `-O`: `// next` 0.2 / 0.1, `or next` 0.1 / 0.1, `// return` 0.2 / 0.1
  (`next without` 0.2 / 0.1). Before, that script bundled, and `// next`
  compiled on its own cost ~3. A `redo` keeps the exception.
- **`read-uint64` and the other `read-*` methods of a Buf copy nothing**
  (18ef8f73; issue #178). Each call copied the whole buffer when another Value
  shared it: on an 8 KB, 800 KB and 8 MB Buf a call cost 0.38, 11.35 and
  116.33 µs, and costs 0.31, 0.29 and 0.27 µs now (Rakudo 2026.09: 0.29,
  0.66, 0.22; the issue's script, interpreted).
- **Passing a Blob or a Buf to a routine costs nothing** (55565059; issue
  #177). The snapshot taken to copy a write to a Buf or Blob parameter back
  to the caller was compared with it byte by byte when the call returned:
  1.2 ms a call for 16 MB. A Blob, which is immutable, is not snapshotted,
  and a Buf the callee did not write is told by its body's pointer. ms per
  call, 1 MB / 16 MB: a Blob 0.046 / 1.199 → 0.001 / 0.000, a Buf 0.050 /
  1.229 → 0.000 / 0.000 (Rakudo 2026.09: 0.000). `subbuf-rw` refuses a Blob,
  as in Rakudo. t/regression/blob-param-costs-nothing.raku agrees with
  Rakudo 2026.09.
- **`.read($n)` reads $n bytes of the file** (ad2bba29; issue #176). The
  first `.read` on a handle read the whole file into it, and `.seek` and
  `.eof` did too; a handle opened to read a regular file reads with pread at
  its cursor now. The first `.read(8)` of 64 MB / 256 MB: 224.3 / 903.7 ms →
  0.5 / 0.2 (Rakudo 2026.09: 0.5 / 0.1); each later one 0.001 ms, before and
  after. A read sees bytes appended since the one before, as in Rakudo.
  Write and update handles and Windows keep the copy.
  t/regression/handle-read-reads-what-it-asks.raku agrees with Rakudo
  2026.09.
- **A non-ASCII string's grapheme table and `.chars` take one walk over its
  bytes** (308aecdf; issue #179). The table `.index` and `.substr` read was
  built by decoding the string, indexing its codepoints and mapping cluster
  starts back to bytes, 17 ms a MB, and `.chars` decoded it too; one pass
  over the UTF-8 does each now, and a pair of codepoints below U+0300 skips
  the property lookups. 16 MB with an é every eight characters: `.chars` 226
  → 13 ms, the first `.index($n, $pos)` 271 → 25 ms. The issue's script on 4
  MB with é: 0.136 → 0.013 ms a call (Rakudo 2026.09: 0.001). 800 random
  strings over every cluster rule split the same before and after.
  t/regression/grapheme-table-one-walk.raku agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **Mathematica::Serializer's encoders run faster than Rakudo's** (608774f1,
  5321f41c; issue #217), where they took twice its time. `when Pair` / `$x ~~
  Int` against a core type keeps its answer per value kind, type and symbol
  generation (never for a name a subset or class of the program's has
  taken), and so does `.isa(Whatever)`; `--> Str` and the other core return
  types accept a plain value without resolving the name; a bare core type
  name answers from its node, `WhateverCode` too while no scope binds such a
  name; `Buf.new` and a `.subst` that finds nothing are answered before the
  method chain; a plain method call skips evalMethodCallExpr's arms; and an
  `Int:D` or `is copy` parameter binds on the simple path. The encoders over
  the R datasets' metadata, s per encode: WL 0.169 → 0.079, WXF 0.416 →
  0.209 (Rakudo 2026.09: 0.089, 0.232). A nine-arm `given` of type `when`s
  1,463 → ~330 ns, `5.isa(WhateverCode)` 332 → 70, `Buf.new(1, 2)` 435 →
  167, `'abc'.subst(:g, '"', '\\"')` 341 → 109, `sub (Int:D $v is copy -->
  Blob)` 511 → 411. t/regression/core-type-shortcuts.raku and
  t/regression/method-call-lean-path.raku agree with Rakudo 2026.09.

- **`EXPR.return` as a whole statement is `return EXPR`** (d54cc3ba): about
  1 µs a call instead of about 160 µs, the cost of unwinding an exception out
  of a method call. Cro picks every message body's parser with `.return if
  .is-applicable($message)`. t/regression/method-return-as-statement.raku
  agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **A channel's send and close wake its parallel-mode readers** (c91a94fb)
  instead of their next 5 ms look: a value from `$channel.Supply` arrives in
  about 0.6 ms on an idle machine (4 to 6 ms before). Cro::WebSocket keeps a
  connection's close promise one such hop after it answers the close frame.
- **A promise's continuation runs on a pooled worker** (a11aa7ab), as
  Rakudo's do: a `.then`, or a `whenever` over a promise that settles later,
  started a thread each. 2000 `whenever $socket.write(…) {}`, Cro's TCP
  replier's shape, take 19 to 27 ms (50 to 72 before), and 2000 `.then`s 14
  ms (32). Such a whenever's delivery into a supply block busy on another
  thread queues behind that body instead of holding its worker.
  t/regression/promise-continuations-reuse-threads.raku agrees with Rakudo
  2026.09.

### Slower

Against a v5.5.0 build on the same M4, five interleaved rounds of perf-guard
at the tag put no kernel more than 5% behind on the gate's measure, each
kernel's best run. The furthest are `subcall` +4.5% (median +2.7%),
`sharedappend` +3.8% (+4.3%) and `strpass` +3.1% (+2.4%). `asg`, which
7a419af4 made 13% slower with not an instruction of its loop changed, by
moving the int kernels' code along, is level again since d252f389: timed
alone, 15 runs each, its median is v5.5.0's within 0.1%. Ahead of v5.5.0:
`exprnext` −98.8% (`next` and `return` written as an operand, 48b76d56),
`strscan` −93.6% (a loop kernel's `int` variables, 3e97932e), `substrloop`
−8.8%, `objnew` −7.8% and `mainnext` −5.0%.

`junctionwide` is accepted 4–5% slower, inside gate 3's 5% on the minimum.
At the tag it is +2.0% (median −0.5%), but the gate's sittings on d252f389
put it at the edge, and at a hundred times the kernel's size, interleaved,
the best of nine was 1,687 ms against 1,617 (+4.3%) and the median +5.9%.
The build before d252f389 (3a9c1b6f) is +4.9%, with the junction code at
the same address in both, so it came earlier in the cycle; it is not
bisected. At the gate's own size its runs span 7.8%, so `perf-guard --check`
calls it inconclusive rather than slower.

The perf-guard baseline is still the one recorded on the M3 for v5.2.0, so
the comparison above is an A/B on one machine, as at v5.5.0.

### New

- **`--target=arduino`: a Raku program as an Arduino sketch** (56309f17,
  ARDUINO-PLAN.md phase A0). The mainline before the top-level `loop { }` is
  `setup()`, one iteration of that loop one call of `loop()`, and
  `use Arduino` names the core's API; the sketch is C++11 that names no
  runtime, and a construct outside the board's subset is refused with its
  line. Every `constant` and top-level `BEGIN` runs while building, so
  `(^32).map: { … sin … }` reaches the board as 32 bytes in flash. A program
  runs with no board: `rakupp prog.raku` on a simulated board built into the
  engine (Serial on stdout, one line per pin change on stderr, real or
  virtual time, fixed inputs), and `--target=arduino --sim` compiles the
  sketch against the same board in C++. t/arduino/run.raku holds seven
  programs to their goldens and each sketch to the interpreter byte for byte,
  and eleven refusals to their messages (27 checks, ~5 s); with
  `--arduino15=DIR` every sketch also compiles for an Arduino Due under
  `-Wall -Werror` — blink is 102 bytes of code. Not yet tried on an AVR board
  or real hardware. See docs/guide/ARDUINO.md.
- **`use Arduino::LEDMatrix`: the Uno R4 WiFi's 12×8 LED matrix** (34d05b96).
  `frame` packs a picture — eight rows of twelve, `#` lit and `.` dark — into
  the three 32-bit words the core's Arduino_LED_Matrix library takes, while
  building, so the board gets twelve bytes; Arduino's tutorial heart packs to
  the tutorial's own words, `3184a444 44042081 100a0040`. `LEDMatrix.begin`,
  `.show(@frame)` and `.clear` become the library's calls, and both simulated
  boards draw each new picture on stderr. A compile-time error now stops an
  Arduino build with its message. None of the backend reaches an `--exe`
  binary whose program does not `use Arduino`: fibonacci's has no trace of it
  at `--slim=safe` or `auto`, which t/run.raku now checks. Not yet compiled
  against the real R4 core.
- **A program can be run from a URL** (636f69d0): `rakupp
  https://github.com/antononcube/Raku-Mathematica-Serializer/blob/main/examples/Serializer-profiling.raku`.
  curl fetches it, as it fetches for `rakupp install`, so the engine still has
  no HTTP or TLS code; the arguments after the URL reach `@*ARGS`, and
  `$?FILE`, `$*PROGRAM-NAME` and backtraces name the URL. A GitHub file page
  is read as the file (`/blob/` → `/raw/`, GitHub's Raw button); any other web
  page is refused rather than parsed, and so is plain `http://`, and a
  redirect down to it. `-c`, `--lint` and `--exe` take a URL too (`--exe`
  names the binary after the file); `--watch` refuses one. The missing-module
  hint names the URL, and both commands it offers work: `rakupp install URL`
  reads the program at the URL and installs what it uses, and `rakupp --auto
  URL` installs all of it on the first miss, in one installer run. Checked
  offline with `file://` URLs and a stand-in curl: 8 checks in t/run.raku, 5
  in t/deps/run.raku. That gate had been dying at its RAKUPP_OPT check, which
  passed a List to a `%env` parameter (Rakudo refuses that too); it now runs
  to the end.
- **The subset metamodel** (0abc0b39; issue #77): `Metamodel::SubsetHOW
  .new_type(:name, :refinee, :refinement)`, also asked of a metaclass made
  from SubsetHOW; `.^refinee` and `.^refinement`; a `.HOW` of each subset's
  own, so what a role mixes into it sticks and `S.^m` reaches the methods
  it brings; and a subset's user traits, which went to no `trait_mod:<is>`
  before. Red's sub-models (`User.^submodel(…)`, `subset Administrator is
  sub-model of User where …`) stand on all of it. The module cache is
  format v56 (SubsetDecl.traits). t/regression/subset-metamodel-for-sub-models.raku
  agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **The NQP exception ops and `nqp::where`** (aa309270; issue #77):
  `nqp::newexception` with `setmessage`/`setextype`/`setpayload` and their
  `get` forms, `nqp::throw` — a CONTROL_WARN one is a resumable warning
  that reaches CONTROL with its payload — `nqp::where`, and the
  `nqp::const::CONTROL_*` values. Red raises its "pending Promise" warning
  through them and compares shared query ASTs by `where`.
  t/regression/nqp-exception-ops-and-where.raku agrees with Rakudo 2026.09,
  and as `--exe`.
- **`rakupp install` tests a suite that stops on `$*VM.name` once more as
  `moar`** (38fe9757), and installs it if that passes, saying so.
  LibraryMake, whose own test dies *Unknown VM; don't know how to build*
  on `cpp`, installs without `RAKUPP_VM_NAME=moar`, so `rakupp install cro`
  gets past it. Programs, and a suite failing for any other reason, still
  see `cpp`. docs/guide/faq/unknown-vm.md says what changed.

- **`--detect-races` reports data races between threads** (12af490d;
  docs/dev/plans/RACE-DETECTOR-PLAN.md). `rakupp --detect-races prog.raku`,
  or `RAKUPP_RACE=1`, watches scalar variables, array elements and lengths,
  hash entries and keys, and attributes. It reports each pair of accesses
  from two threads, at least one a write, that no `await`, `Lock`,
  `Channel`, `Supply` or `⚛` orders. Each report goes to stderr once, when
  the program ends, with both sites, the threads, a backtrace of the later
  access and every name the location was reached by; a run that raced
  exits 66. It is a FastTrack happens-before detector, so it also reports
  races that gave the right answer. The flag is refused with the compilers,
  `--target=js`, `--jit` and `--cnp`, and it turns the loop kernels off.
  Detector off, the hooks cost within about ±0.7% instructions and cycles
  on the benchmarks measured. t/race-detect/ is its gate (134 checks at 20
  runs each). tools/race-sweep.raku runs files plain and under it: Roast
  S17 and four distributions reported 11 races, each one in the test, none
  the detector's own mistake
  (docs/dev/findings/RACE-SWEEP-2026-10-25.md). guide/CLI.md, "Finding data
  races", and faq/threads.md have the user's side.
- **The Jupyter kernel reads Jupyter::Kernel's cell magics** (5d0e7ce9;
  issue #203), so that kernel's notebooks run unchanged. `#% html`,
  `#% markdown` (`md`), `#% latex`, `#% latex(ENV)` and `#% js` publish the
  cell's value under that MIME type. `#% > TYPE` holds what the cell prints
  and publishes it as one display when the cell ends, in order with any
  `jupyter-display` call. `#% javascript` sends the cell to the frontend
  unevaluated. `%% bash` runs it in bash with its output streaming, and a
  non-zero exit fails the cell. `%% run FILE` evaluates FILE's code, then
  the cell's, as one cell. `%% always prepend|append|show|clear` wraps every
  Raku cell. A `#%` line that is not a magic stays a comment, and an unknown
  `%%` line is an error that lists the magics. tools/jupyter-smoke.raku has
  22 new checks, 17 of which fail on the previous binary.
  guide/integrations/JUPYTER.md, "Cell magics", has the user's side.
- **A cell value that begins with `<svg` renders as an image, and one that
  begins with `<img` as HTML** (170802d4; issue #203). This is
  Jupyter::Kernel's one guess about a value no magic typed. Only the first
  four characters are read, case aside, so any other value is text as
  before, and a cell with a type magic is never guessed. Five gate checks.
- **`--jupyter-install` warns when it replaces another kernel** (cb7933f3;
  issue #204). `raku` is Jupyter::Kernel's kernelspec name too, so the
  default install takes the Rakudo kernel's place, and it used to do so
  silently. The warning goes to stderr, under `-q` too, names the program
  the old kernel.json launched, and offers `--name=rakupp` to keep both; a
  reinstall over the same binary stays quiet. JUPYTER.md, "It replaces
  another Raku kernel", also gives
  `raku-jupyter-kernel --generate-config --force` to hand the name back.
- **The Wolfram binding prints what Raku prints, in a notebook too, and
  `RakuEval` follows the REPL's rule** (fb9009bc; issue #56).
  `RakuEval["say 3 ** 2"]` wrote 9 to the kernel's stdout, which a notebook
  never shows, and returned True. Raku's output is now captured with
  `rk_set_output` and a `ForeignCallback` and printed by the kernel when
  the call returns, in the order written: print cells in a notebook,
  stderr in red, and the same UTF-8 text on stdout and stderr in a script.
  Code that wrote to stdout returns Null, as `Print` does; `RakuCall` keeps
  its value. An eval that prints nothing measured 49 µs against 44 µs, and
  printing costs about 14 µs a write (20,000 `say`s: 0.28 s against
  0.02 s). bindings/wolfram/tests/output.wls is a new Wolfram leg of
  tools/bindings-smoke.raku, which the old package fails. The RakuppLink
  paclet carries the fix from version 1.0.3 (2026-10-31).
- **The bindings' guides give three ways to get librakupp, and the loaders'
  not-found errors print the commands** (62e16b9e). A release archive
  carries the library (every one but OpenBSD's), a fresh checkout builds it
  as before, and beside a static development `build/` it is built alone:
  `cmake --build build-shared -j --target rakupp_shared` in a
  `-DRAKUPP_BUILD_SHARED=ON` directory of its own, named with `RAKUPP_LIB`.
  The Python, JS and Wolfram errors print those commands;
  bindings/README.md, section 1, has the rest.
- **`.DEPARSE("RU")` renders the tree in Russian** (3431c375), and in any
  language whose L10N dist is installed: `Q[my $x = 1; say $x].AST.DEPARSE("RU")`
  is `мой $x = 1;` and `скажи $x`. It ignored its arguments and rendered
  English. The spellings are the dist's `RakuAST::Deparse::L10N::RU` role,
  whose `xsyn` method is asked at every keyword with the category Rakudo's
  deparser asks it with, so `.DEPARSE` takes a role of the user's own too.
  A language that is not installed is X::CompUnit::UnsatisfiedDependency, as
  upstream; a role that highlights (`hsyn`) is X::NYI, where Rakudo colours
  the output. L10N::BG, LV, RU and UK 0.0.2 now pass their test 15 outright,
  which their suites ran under a `todo` here. Which keyword is asked, where,
  and under which category is tools/rakuast-deparse-l10n-spec.raku, 23 cases,
  and t/regression/rakuast-deparse-l10n.raku holds it to Rakudo 2026.09
  (docs/dev/findings/rakuast/deparse-l10n-2026.09.tsv); it also renders a
  fixture language by name and reads it back with `.AST("ZZ")` as the same
  program.
- **The Python binding takes a module's name with dots, and walks a long
  name a part at a time** (992c75cf, 3072c3a5). `raku.use("JSON.Fast")`
  loads JSON::Fast, adverbs and import arguments included
  (`"JSON.Fast:ver<0.19+>"`); the name stopped at the dot, and
  `raku.use("Outer.Inner")` died "No such method 'Inner' for invocant of
  type 'Any'". After `raku.use("Outer::Inner")`,
  `raku.main.Outer.Inner.hi()` calls `Outer::Inner::hi`, through the
  package Outer that the engine now declares (see Behaves like Rakudo). A
  name missing from `raku.main` raises "Raku MAIN has no …", where the name
  was blank. bindings/python/tests has two new tests, and the README's
  modules section shows both.

### Fixed: silent wrong answers

- **A routine compiled in a bundled program judges a store into a native
  array's element as the interpreter does** (166334a9). Compiled on its own,
  with no view of the program's declarations, it wrote the element's place:
  a Str, a Rat or a Failure into an `int` or `num` array, and an Int into a
  `num` one, were stored, `@w[$i] += 2.5` left 2.5, a Bool stayed one, `++`
  at int64's top and an `int8` past its range did not wrap. Each module's
  routines now know its native `@` names, of any width, and a store into one
  asks the array whether it is native before taking the native path.
- **A compiled store into a native array's element through a parameter, a
  block's parameter, a `$` variable or an attribute is judged too**
  (29b10450): `sub f(@a) { @a[0] = 2.5 }` given an `int` array stored 2.5,
  and so did `$x[0] = 2.5` with `$x` holding one. Such a store asks the array
  at run time; one into an array declared plain never asks, and programs
  storing only into those compile to the same C++ as before.
- **A compiled `op=`, `++` or int lane into a typed scalar is checked as `=`
  is** (6617859d). Only `=` checked the declaration: `my Small $x = 5; $x +=
  100` held 105 under `--exe` and `--exe -O`, where Rakudo dies with
  X::TypeCheck::Assignment, `my Int $i = 1; $i += 1.5` held 2.5 and `$i ~=
  "a"` "1a", and a UInt's `--` went below 0. Under `-O` the int lanes did the
  same: `my Num $g = 5e0; $g = $one` held 1, and a loop's store-back wrote
  past a subset (`while $y < 300 { $y += 100 }` on a `my Small $y` ended at
  305). A subset bundles the program, and a bundled program's routines get
  lanes without `-O`, so plain `--exe` lost the check too. Each of those
  stores now goes through the check, and a loop lane refuses a slot whose
  type checks more than its guard does (a subset, UInt, a class), so that
  loop runs boxed. Untyped, `my Int`, `my Num` and `my Str` slots keep their
  lanes, and an untyped store compiles to the same C++ as before.
- **A variable's `where` is checked in a routine, in a compiled routine and
  from the parse cache** (d23c2ed8). An untyped `my $x where * < 10` in a sub
  or an inline block took any value interpreted (`$x = $one + 100` held
  101). The parse cache did not write a variable's `where`, and a bundled
  program's routines are compiled from it, so a compiled routine never saw a
  local's: `my Int $x where * < 10 = 5; $x += 100` held 105 under `--exe`
  and `--exe -O`, where Rakudo dies with X::TypeCheck::Assignment, and a
  module read from the parse cache took any value from its second run on.
  Nor did a compiled routine see an outer one's in an untyped `op=` or `++`,
  or in a loop lane, untyped or `my Int`. The cache now writes the `where`
  (version 58), a routine that declares one runs interpreted, and a compiled
  routine checks every store into an outer variable declared with one and
  gives it no lane; an outer variable without one keeps its lane.
- **A variable's `:D` and `:U` are checked from the parse cache, in a compiled
  routine and in an interpreted `op=`, `++` or `cas`** (0d9ea1ca). The parse
  cache did not write a variable's smiley, and a bundled program's routines
  are compiled from it: `my Int:D $x = 5; $x = $int` held Int under `--exe`
  and `--exe -O`, where Rakudo dies with X::TypeCheck::Assignment, and `my
  Int:U $x; $x = $five` held 5. A compiled routine's store into an outer `my
  Int:D $c` took Int too, and a Nil reset it to Int. Interpreted, `=` checked
  the smiley, but `cas`, `⚛=`, `op=`, `//=` and `++` did not: `my Int:U $x;
  $x++` held 1. A module read from the parse cache lost five more of a
  declaration's fields: a Rat was coerced into a `my Int(Str) $x`, `(state @s)
  = 1, 2` was assigned on the first call only, a `my constant` became a
  package symbol, `my ::foo $x` held Any and a bare `my S $s` of a subset with
  no type object to start from held one. The cache writes all six (version
  59), a routine that declares a smiley runs interpreted, and each of those
  stores checks it.
- **Compiled `try` consumes a live Failure** (42f79e98), as interpreted: a block
  that returned one handed the Failure back and left `$!` unset; now `try`
  answers Nil, sets `$!` and marks the Failure handled.
- **A Failure already handled passes through `try`** (42f79e98), as its value
  with `$!` Nil, as in Rakudo 2026.09, in both engines; it was consumed like a
  live one.
- **Compiled `-$x` on 0e0 is -0** (d7c6d9d5), as interpreted and in Rakudo; it
  was `0 - $x`, which is +0. An object's `-$o` asks its `.Numeric`, not its
  `.Bridge`, as the interpreter does.
- **A List kept from a literal is written through in compiled code**
  (d7c6d9d5). `f(($a, 2))` with `sub f($x) { $x[0] = 5 }` writes $a. Three ways
  it did not: bundled for it, the program's routines were still native, and
  stored into the List's item instead of through it (a second write then died
  "Cannot modify an immutable List"); a native program handing such a List to
  a module's routine built it of values (the call now bundles the program);
  and a module routine keeping one did the same (it stays interpreted).
- **`fail` in a compiled routine returns its Failure** (d7c6d9d5). The builtin
  decides by the interpreter's routine frame, which compiled code never has,
  so every compiled sub that failed died with the exception: `q(-1) //
  "failed"` printed it and exited 1, with or without -O. In a block closure
  it is aimed at the routine's frame, as `return` is, and while a compiled
  `try` runs it still dies, so `(try q(-1)) // "caught: $!"` catches it.
- **`Num.Rat($epsilon)` walks as Rakudo's does** (d7c6d9d5): `1e-300.Rat(0)` was
  0/1 (Rakudo: 1/10**300), a fractional part below 1e-18 stopped a convergent
  early, and b/d past 2**53 was rounded twice. Where Rakudo's walk never ends
  (`7.01492021304424e-06.Rat(0)` grows Ints until memory runs out) this one
  answers the double's exact value. `.Rat(0)` is not the exact value in
  either engine: `0.1e0.Rat(0)` is 1/10, as in Rakudo, which the findings
  file had wrong. 13,222 (input, epsilon) pairs agree with Rakudo 2026.09.

- **A `$` store in compiled code resets on Nil and checks a declared type**
  (3e97932e), as `=` does interpreted: `my $r = try die "x"` left Nil (Any),
  `my @a = Nil` was `[]` (`[Any]`), `my Int $x; $x = "a"` stored the Str and
  `$x = Nil` the Nil. In a bundle, a module-mode routine's store into one of
  the interpreter's variables asks its declaration.
- **`-O` answers as plain `--exe` and the interpreter do** (3e97932e): `~` and
  an interpolation join two Strs NFC (`"e" ~ "\x[301]"` was two code points);
  `my ($a, $b) = $j` iterates the List in `$j` (it bound it to `$a`) and a
  Hash gives its pairs; `!=` between an unsigned native and a negative signed
  one is False, as in Rakudo; `my $bound := $q` is no longer compiled as a
  copy (it bundles).
- **Instant and Duration arithmetic answer Rakudo's types** (3e97932e):
  `-$duration` and `abs $duration` are Durations (were Rats), `0 - $duration`
  and `7 % $duration` Nums (were Durations), `-$instant` a Num; compiled, no
  operator kept the tag (`$d - 0` was a Rat).
- **`.push($s)` and `.unshift($s)` slip a Slip held in `$s`** (3e97932e), as
  Rakudo does; the `unshift` sub is the method (it answered the new length,
  slipped nothing and checked no element type).
- **`my ($k, $v) = %h` takes the pairs** (3e97932e); `$k` held the Hash.
- **`fail` in a block that another routine runs returns from the routine it
  was written in** (3e97932e), as a `return` does, in both engines: `sub q {
  ap({ fail "z" }); … }` returned the Failure from `ap`, and `q` sank it and
  died.
- **`f(($a, 2))` writes `$a` from a sub's second call on** (3e97932e): the
  interpreter's lean call path dropped what keeps the List's containers, and
  `$x[0] = 5` died "Cannot modify an immutable List".

- **A compiled routine applies its `--> T`** (e30e2827): `sub r(--> Int) {
  "x" }` answered "x" with or without -O, `--> Str()` did not coerce and
  `--> Int:D` passed Int. Every way out is checked, in top-level, lexical and
  anonymous subs, multi candidates, methods and pointy blocks, as the
  interpreter checks it; a value of a core type passes inline, at no cost
  measurable against the unchecked call (`--> Num` -O: 10.6 → 10.3 ns).
- **A write through a kept List into a typed variable is checked**
  (e30e2827), in both engines: `my Int $a; sub f($x) { $x[0] = "s" };
  f(($a, 2))` stored the Str, and a Nil there stayed Nil instead of
  resetting to the default.

- **A compiled loop's Num division by zero fails** (e24f05f1), as interpreted
  and in Rakudo, with X::Numeric::DivideByZero. The unboxed loop lane, on with
  `-O` and without, divided doubles and answered Inf: `while … { $t = 1e0 /
  $x }` with `$x` 0e0 returned Inf, and `$s = $s + 1e0 / $x` ran on where it
  dies. The lane bails on a zero divisor now, -0e0 and an Int 0 included.
- **A compiled loop of Nums alone leaves its lane correctly** (e24f05f1). A
  lane with no Int slot had no landing for a bail, so a Num `**` that
  underflows ended the loop with the values it held at that point: `$t = $x
  ** ($k + 1e0)` with `$x` 1e-200 answered 1e-200, where Rakudo gives an
  X::Numeric::Underflow Failure.
- **A `<name>` that calls a `my token` matches in .comb, .split and
  .contains** (8644846a). Compiled code declares its tokens where .comb did
  not look, so with -O and without `'abq xyzq aq'.comb(/ <?after <two>>
  'q' /)` (`my token two { 'ab' | 'xyz' }`) answered three q's and
  `.comb(/<two>/)` twelve empty strings. .split and .contains missed one in
  both engines: `'zzz'.contains(/<two>/)` was True, `'zz'.split(/<two>/)`
  cut between every character. Rakudo 2026.09 agrees but on the
  lookbehinds, where it matches the subrule against the reversed text.
- **A compiled regex literal is the Regex where its value is taken**
  (74806a6d), natively with -O and without, as interpreted: a Pair's key
  (`'a b c'.trans(/\s/ => '_')` changed nothing), a ternary's branch
  (`.grep(True ?? /c/ !! /a/)` kept everything, `return True ?? /b/ !! /q/`
  was Nil) and `/x/ xx 2` (two Nils); `m//` is the match (`my $m = m/c/`
  stored the Regex). A regex asked for its truth in a compiled loop matches
  the loop's topic: `for <xa yb ab> { say $_ if /a/ && /b/ }` printed xa and
  yb, judged against the program's `$_`.
- **A `for` over a Range with a BigInt end walks its real integers**
  (e614993a), interpreted and natively with -O and without. The counted loops
  walked the saturated int64 fields beside the carried ends: `for 2**64 ..
  2**64 + 2 -> $i` ran once, at 9223372036854775807 (Rakudo: three times from
  2**64), `9223372036854775806 .. 9223372036854775808` stopped one short, and
  `-2**64 - 1 .. -2**64` ran once at int64's min. `2**64 .. *` carried no
  start at all, so its .raku said 9223372036854775807..Inf; it walks lazily
  now, until its `last`.
- **`++` and `--` in a `--cnp` kernel store .succ and .pred** (de2e08d4), as
  interpreted. Anything but a machine number was stepped by `+ 1`: `my $b =
  False; $b++` counted to 3 where the interpreter and Rakudo answer True,
  `$s++` on "a" died "Cannot convert string to number", and `@flags[$i]++`
  made a Bool element an Int. A FatRat whose parts fit two words ran on the
  Rat lane, so `$fr * 3 + 0` answered a Rat; it stays boxed now.
- **A subscript's key is evaluated once** (769ed705), as in Rakudo. A key
  that was no machine Int was evaluated by evalIndex's fast arm and again by
  the general path: with `my @a = 10, 20, 30`, `@a[$i++ + 0.5]` read 20 and
  left $i at 2 (Rakudo: 10, and 1), `@a[($i++, 1)]` read (20 20), and
  `@a[key()]` called key() twice.
- **A Failure used as a subscript throws what it carries** (85d56001), as in
  Rakudo, where it indexed as the 2 its hash holds: with `my $f = 1 div 0`,
  `@a[$f] = 5` stored into @a[2], `@a[$f]:exists` was True and `%h{$f} = 5`
  made a key of the Failure's fields. Slices, junctions, `++`, `:delete`,
  native arrays and the AT-POS / *-KEY methods throw too, interpreted,
  compiled and in a `--cnp` kernel.
- **A store into a native array's element converts or refuses as Rakudo's
  does** (960e8aed). An `int` element held True, 1.5 or 2**70 as it came, an
  int8 one 300 and a uint8 one -1, `@a.push(1)` put an Int into a num array,
  and `@a[0] += 1.5` left 3.5. True is 1 now, 300 into an int8 is 44, and a
  Rat dies with the X::TypeCheck or unbox message Rakudo gives for that store
  (`=`, `+=`, a slice, push, append, splice). A store answers what it was
  handed, `(@u8[$i] = -1)` is -1, and a slice an `array[int]` of those. 103
  probes of the forms agree with Rakudo 2026.09. Bools keep a packed array
  packed: `@a[$i] = $i %% 3` under `--cnp` takes 8.1 ms over 1M, from 30.9
  on a87a6b73 (best of 3).
- **A store through an alias of a native array's element converts or
  refuses as Rakudo's does** (273863ac). `my $x := @n[1]; $x = 1.5`, `for @n
  { $_ = 1.5 }`, `given @n[0] { … }`, an `is rw` or `\a` parameter, a `.kv`
  value, `(@n[0], @n[1]) = …` and `@a[0] := @n[0]` took what they were
  given, so an int8 held 300 and an int array 1.5, and `@n[0] ||= Nil`
  stored Nil. True is 1 now, 300 into an int8 is 44, and 1.5, "7", Nil or a
  big Int dies as an unbox, as `@n[0] += 1.5` already did. A native
  variable refuses a Str as an unbox too, and `$i ||= Nil`, `$i .= Str` and
  the Any a `$` holds for Nil are refused, as Rakudo refuses them. Through
  an alias of any array's element, `$x ||= 5` now stores. An interpreted rw
  loop over a native array pays a few nanoseconds an iteration for it
  (`for @ints { }` over 2M: 69.4 → 76.9 ms against 74895cce).
- **C's `cond ? a : b` is a compile error, as in Rakudo** (93f9a4af). A lone
  `?` ended the expression and `? a` began a new statement, so
  `my $s = $n > 2 ? "many" : "few"` set `$s` to True; other shapes died of
  "Malformed radix number" or "expected )". It is now Rakudo's X::Obsolete,
  "Unsupported use of ? and : for the ternary conditional operator".
- **A store reaches the container an element is bound to** (84add3b1).
  After `@b[1] := my $c`, `@b[1] = 9` left $c as (Any): the bind looked $c up
  before the declaration made it. `$x := my $c` was the same, and
  `@b[1] := my $c = 4` died on the store. A slice (`@b[1, 2] = …`,
  `%h<a b> = …`) or list assignment (`($c, @b[1]) = …`) replaced a bound
  element instead of storing into it, and `.VAR` of one was its value's type.
  22 shapes agree with Rakudo 2026.09; a list assignment into a Proxy now
  runs its STORE.
- **A routine named after a block prefix gets its arguments** (08a17d21).
  `sub start($x) { $x + 1 }; say start(5)` said 1: the prefix handed the sub
  its thunk as a Block, and `start(2, 3)` died. `do(5)`, `try(5)`,
  `quietly(5)` and `once(5)` never called the routine, and `gather(5)` was
  empty. Glued to its paren, such a name is now the routine's call (`react`
  and `supply` too), while `start { … }` and `start 5` stay the prefix. A
  routine declared below its call counts, which also fixes `say repeat(5);
  sub repeat($x) {…}`, a parse error before.

- **A package's `our` variable reads through its Stash** (5d31d4a2; issue
  #202). `Circle::{'$pi'}` answered (Any) where `$Circle::pi` and
  `Circle::<$pi>` answered 3.14, and so did a computed key, `.AT-KEY`,
  `.pairs`, `.values` and a Stash held in a variable: `.WHO` copied the
  variable's cell holder, which reads as nothing, into the Stash. The Stash
  holds the variable's container now, as Rakudo's does, so
  `Circle::{'$pi'} = 3` assigns to `$Circle::pi`. Compiled, `Circle::.keys`
  was (): the native bareword lookup made a type object named `Circle::`
  where the interpreter answers the package's stash.
- **A module's importer takes its variables, constants and enums by tag**
  (aff5032b; issue #202). Only a routine's `is export(:tag)` was read: a
  plain `use` brought in `our $tv is export(:vt)` and `constant TK is
  export(:ct)`, `use M :vt` brought in M's DEFAULT variables, constants and
  enum members too, and `need M` its constants and enum members. A constant
  or enum a `unit module` declared without exporting it reached the
  importer all the same (`use NC; say ha`). Every declaration keeps its
  tags now (AST cache v51), a load imports what the module exported by
  tag, and `require M <C $x>` by name. An exported `our` variable is its
  package's one cell, which the importer is bound to. `import G` brings in
  G's exported variables and constants, and a `sub EXPORT` handing over a
  Stash's variable binds its container (roast's gh2979.t). The orbits and
  fourier showcases used four constants their libraries never exported;
  those are `is export` now, and both showcases run under Rakudo too.
- **`(return 5) or say 'x'` returns 5 without running the `say`** (48b76d56;
  found fixing issue #195), as in Rakudo. The cooperative `return` counted
  a left operand as on its way to the end of the statement, handed `or` an
  Any, and `or` went on to its right side.
- **`f($i) or return "no"` returns "no"** (a348489b; found fixing issue
  #195), as in Rakudo. A `return` written as an operand ended before a
  keyword by reading the next token's TEXT, a string literal's contents
  included, so "no", "or", "and", "xor", "if", "my" and `q/or/` went: the
  routine returned Nil and the string became a statement of its own. So did
  `return not 1`. The statement form was right.
- **`--exe` runs a LEAVE however its block is left** (fc262224; found fixing
  issue #195), as interpreted and in Rakudo. A block's LEAVE ran after its
  last statement only, so a `next`, `last`, `redo`, `return` or exception
  that left early skipped it: `for ^3 -> $i { LEAVE { … }; next if $i ==
  1; … }` ran two of three. It is a scope guard now, and a death in it
  propagates, so a routine's `LEAVE { die "x" }` dies as interpreted. A
  LEAVE that reads its block's variables beside a CATCH bundles the
  program; its C++ did not compile before.
- **A string that is not an ISO 8601 date or timestamp is
  X::Temporal::InvalidFormat** (0f19ab0e; issue #208), as in Rakudo.
  `DateTime.new('')` was 0000-01-01T00:00:00Z, so a missing timestamp read
  from a file (`%h<at> // ''`) gave an age of about 2026 years. The string
  constructor refused only a string with no `-` after its first character
  and read the rest with sscanf: `2020-01-01 `, `2020-1-1`, `999-01-01`,
  `2020-01-01T10:00`, `2020-01-01 10:00:00`, a bare `T`, `UTC`, `.5,5` and a
  13-digit fraction were dates, and `Date.new` took a whole timestamp and
  kept its day. A string has Rakudo's grammar's shape now before it is read,
  in `Date.new`, `DateTime.new`, `.Date`, `.DateTime` and `Date()`; the
  exception's `.format` for a DateTime is Rakudo's text, not "an ISO 8601
  timestamp" alone.
- **A junction invocant threads before a junction argument** (f483f0e6;
  issue #192), as in Rakudo. `so @paths.all.starts-with('/etc/' |
  '/var/lib/')` was False where every path starts with one of the two
  prefixes: the argument threaded first, so the answer was an any of alls,
  any(all(True, False), all(False, True)). The invocant threads first now,
  whatever the two junctions' kinds, and the arguments inside each call.
  Among the arguments an all/none junction threads before an any/one, as a
  sub's already did: `'abc'.substr(0|1, 1&2)` was any(all(a, ab), all(b,
  bc)), and is all(any(a, b), any(ab, bc)).
- **An alias of a typed container's element keeps the container's type**
  (17e33a45; issue #184), as in Rakudo. `my Int @a = 1, 2, 3; my $e :=
  @a[1]; $e = 'foo'` stored the Str, and `$e.VAR.of` died "No such method
  'of'". A type here lives with the variable's name, which an alias does not
  share; each alias of an element now carries the container's guard, so a
  store through it refuses what the element type refuses, converts for a
  coercion type, and resets the element on Nil (which it stored as Nil):
  `:=` and `\x` binds, `is rw` and sigilless parameters, `for` over the
  array, its views and `%h.values`, `given`, and `.map`'s and `.grep`'s `$_`,
  for arrays, hashes and attributes' elements. `$x := @a.AT-POS(1)` and
  `%h.AT-KEY("k")` alias the element, where they bound a copy, and
  `@a[0].VAR` answers the element type, its default and the container's
  name (`@a`; it was `element`). An untyped container's aliases cost what
  they did; a typed one's are checked now: 300k `:=` binds and stores 135 →
  192 ms, a `for` writing 1M elements 169 → 199 ms.
- **An alias of a typed scalar keeps its variable's type** (5f87f445), as in
  Rakudo, which names the variable: `my Int $a; my $b := $a; $b = 'x'`
  stored the Str, as did a store through `my \s = $a`, an `is rw`, `is raw`,
  sigilless or named `is rw` parameter given `$a`, `-> $x is rw`, `for $a`,
  `for $a <-> $v`, `for $a, $c` and `given`/`with $a`; Nil through one stored
  Nil, and `$b.VAR.of` was (Mu). The alias of a typed variable is a Proxy
  over its cell carrying the variable's type, smiley, coercion and default
  now, made once per variable and kept in its scope; `.VAR` through it, and
  the variable's own `.VAR.of` under `is default`, a smiley or a coercion,
  answer as Rakudo's. An untyped variable's aliases cost what they did
  (within 3%); a typed one's are checked: 300k `f($a)` into an `is rw`
  parameter 94 → 130 ms, `:=` and store 70 → 115 ms.
- **A module keeps its own `is` trait after `use NativeLibs`** (0e44c5a6;
  issue #211). A module that declared `multi trait_mod:<is>(Routine,
  :$zapped!)` and then imported NativeLibs lost `is zapped` without a word:
  the `&trait_mod:<is>` NativeLibs' EXPORT sub hands back was bound over the
  module's own group. A multi an EXPORT sub returns joins a same-named group
  the importing scope declared now, as an exported `multi` already did.
- **A CArray subscript is evaluated once** (357cb4bb; issue #187): `$c[$i++]`
  read the element after the one asked for and left `$i` at 2, interpreted
  and compiled. t/regression/carray-index-once.raku agrees with Rakudo
  2026.09.
- **`"<$k>"` in a regex matches the text `<abc>`** (6eddeeb5; issue #188).
  Inside a double-quoted atom the angles around a variable were read as
  the `<$var>` assertion, so `/ "<$tag>" (.*?) "</$tag>" /` found nothing.
  t/regression/regex-quoted-angle-var.raku agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **`for` over a value in no container walks its `.list`** (c50eb3b8; issue
  #146): a typed `Channel $in` parameter, a call answering a Channel or a
  Supply, a Match, an object of a class with its own `list`. Each ran once,
  with the value itself, so `sub drain(Channel $in) { for $in -> $v { … }
  }` saw the Channel and not its values. A `$` variable is still one item,
  and so is a typed parameter holding something Iterable. The module cache
  is format v53 (VarExpr.bareParam). t/regression/for-over-listing-value.raku
  agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **A `return` passing through a block or routine runs its UNDO** (09018c67;
  issue #77, FCO's item 6). `sub wrap(&b) { KEEP …; UNDO …; b() }; wrap {
  return 42 }` ran wrap's KEEP. Rakudo runs KEEP only in the routine the
  return leaves, and UNDO in every `if`, `for`, bare or pointy block, sub
  and method it unwinds, undoing their `let`s and still checking their
  POSTs; so does Raku++ now. Red's transactions committed where they should
  have rolled back. t/regression/return-through-block-undo.raku agrees with
  Rakudo 2026.09.
- **An object hash copied into another keeps its keys as the objects they
  were** (9695e120; issue #77, FCO's item 3). `my %copy{K} = %original`,
  typed or not, in a declaration or a later assignment, answered "(K)", a
  Str, for each key: the copy took the entries but not the table of the
  objects they were subscripted with. A plain hash still stringifies them.
  Red keys query branches by AST objects. t/regression/object-hash-copy-keeps-keys.raku
  agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **A role's own multi candidates join those of a role it does**
  (1d5e98c0; issue #77, FCO's item 5). Through `role SQLMapping does
  BaseMapping` a class found SQLMapping's group and stopped there, so
  BaseMapping's `('int' --> 'integer')` lost 'int' to `(Str $value)`, and
  any literal or `where` candidate one role in to a plain type one role
  out. Red's SQL type aliases came back untranslated, and SQLite refused an
  auto-incrementing key typed `int`. t/regression/role-in-role-multi-candidates-join.raku
  agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **A named `@`/`%` parameter refuses a type object that is no
  Positional/Associative** (c423e9db; issue #77). `:to(Str)` reached Red's
  `inflate(Str $value, :@to!)` and died binding it, where Rakudo takes
  `inflate(Any $value, Any :$to)`; `:to(Array)` still binds. t/regression/named-positional-param-refuses-type-object.raku
  agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **`.^find_method` on a built-in type answers no for a name it does not
  have** (3f0baa68; issue #77, FCO's item 4). Some forty built-in types —
  Set, Bag, Mix and their mutable forms, Range, Pair, Seq, Rat, Complex,
  Buf, Blob, Version, Duration, Instant, IO::Path, Uni, and the roles Cool,
  Real, Numeric, Stringy and Iterable — answered a method object for any
  name, and Red called the "red-type-db-methods" it found on a Set column.
  `.can` shares the new sentinels, so `Set.can('elems')` and
  `Version.can('parts')` answer yes. t/regression/builtin-type-find-method-answers-no.raku
  agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **An X::TypeCheck built by hand renders its message** (52426690; issue
  #77). `X::TypeCheck::Assignment.new(symbol => '$x', got => 42, expected =>
  Str).message` was undefined, and so was every hand-built member of the
  family; they now read as Rakudo's `method message`s do (Assignment,
  Binding, Binding::Parameter, Return, Splice, Argument and the base
  class). Red builds the Assignment one for a column refusing a value.
  t/regression/typecheck-exception-built-by-hand.raku agrees with Rakudo
  2026.09.
- **A method called on a Whatever value is called on it** (34168c0e; issue
  #77). `my $w = *; $w.grep({ True })` answered a Sub: every method on a
  Whatever VALUE curried, behind the evaluator that curries a written
  `*.grep(…)` already. Its list methods now see `(*,)` and `.ACCEPTS` is
  True, as in Rakudo; Red walks an AST whose nodes may hold a `*`.
  t/regression/whatever-value-method-does-not-curry.raku agrees with Rakudo
  2026.09, and as `--exe`.
- **A module does not see the operators the main program imports or
  declares** (8a6c9d9a; issue #77). The program's top scope is global_,
  every module's hung from it, and so `use Red;` in a test put
  Red::Operators' `eqv` in front of Red's Case optimizer, which imports no
  operators: its `where { [eqv] |%when.values, $else }` answered an AST,
  and `.grep(…).map(*.id)` sent SQLite `CASE WHEN  THEN "bla".id END`.
  Module scopes now hang from a scope that holds a hole for each operator
  the program defines, so a module finds its own import or the built-in;
  the program keeps its own. Red's suite: 40 of 126 files pass, 50 with
  this, none lost, and no file meets the empty WHEN. Roast's spectest list
  passes as in v5.5.0. t/regression/module-sees-not-the-programs-operators.raku
  agrees with Rakudo 2026.09, and as `--exe`.
- **`.can` on a Failure answers Failure's own methods and Any's**
  (ad7f05dc; issue #77). The probe that answers `.can` for a built-in value
  calls the method, and a Failure throws what it holds at any call, which
  read as "found": `$failure.^can('anything')` was True. With no
  `$*RED-DB`, Red's `try $*RED-DB.^can("table-name-formatter")` took its
  branch and died "Dynamic variable $*RED-DB not found" there, in eight
  test files. t/regression/failure-can-is-failures-own.raku agrees with
  Rakudo 2026.09.
- **A dynamic variable no caller declares is GLOBAL's before it is an
  error** (e6777420; issue #77). `%*RED-DEFAULT-DRIVERS` reads the
  `%GLOBAL::RED-DEFAULT-DRIVERS` Red's `red-defaults` writes, as Rakudo's
  lookup does (S02), so a test that sets its database that way no longer
  dies "$*RED-DB wasn't defined". Only a `GLOBAL::` write or a top-level
  `our` counts, not a top-level `my`; a caller's own `my $*x` still wins.
  Red's suite: 50 of 126 files pass before these two, 58 with them, none
  lost. t/regression/dynamic-falls-back-to-global.raku agrees with Rakudo
  2026.09.
- **A Buf is written in place** (18ef8f73; issue #186). In a binary built with
  `--exe`, a natively compiled module routine's `$b.write-uint16(…)` and
  `$b[2] = 3` on a Buf it was passed left the caller's Buf as it was, and in
  any compiled code `$b[1] = 5` turned the Buf into an Array (`[(Any) 5]`).
  Interpreted, `my $c = $b; $c.write-uint16(…)` left `$b` as it was. A write
  within a Buf lands in the storage every holder of it shares now,
  interpreted and compiled. t/regression/buf-write-in-place.raku agrees
  with Rakudo 2026.09, and t/aot/cases/buf-args prints Rakudo's output in
  all three of its modes.
- **A sunk `if`, `given` or CATCH sinks its block's value, and so does a tap
  or a whenever** (640accf3; issue #60). A lazy value a call handed back — a
  gather, a map whose block `return`s — never ran under `f() if $c`, at the
  end of an `if`/`else`/`with`/`given` block, in a matched `when` or
  `default` (in a given, a loop or a CATCH), or as a tap's or whenever's
  block; a Failure there was dropped instead of detonating. Each is sunk now,
  as in Rakudo, and a value that is kept (`my $x = do given …`) is kept
  unrun. t/regression/sunk-construct-sinks-its-value.raku agrees with Rakudo
  2026.09.
- **A class's second parent seeds its attributes** (2b82edd7; issue #60). In
  `class X is A is B` every default of B's came back undefined, and in
  `class F does R is A`, where the role took the first parent slot, every
  default of A's did; methods, `.^mro` and named arguments were right, so
  the object looked whole until a default was read. A second parent's `is
  required` was not checked either. Every parent seeds now, in the order
  `.^mro` reports (a diamond's base once), and every parent is checked.
  FunctionalParsers' MermaidJS actions, `does … is Raku::AST`, had none of
  their actions; FunctionalParsers 0.1.11 now passes all 18 of its test
  files. t/regression/second-parent-attribute-defaults.raku agrees with
  Rakudo 2026.09.
- **A slice bound to a `$` or sigilless name is the List of those
  elements** (820388ce; found with issue #215). `my \x = @a[0, 2]` bound 30
  and `my \w = @a[0..1]` bound 10: the subscript was read as one index, a
  list's length or a Range's first element. `my \x = %h<a b>` bound Any and
  made an entry under the key "a b". A list, a Range, `*` or `@keys` binds
  the List of those elements' containers now, as `my @s := @a[0, 2]` did:
  a write through it reaches the array or hash, a write to them shows
  through it, a refill leaves it the elements it had, and a typed container
  still checks what it takes. A place past the end reads Any without making
  it. `--exe` bundles a program that binds an element or a slice and writes
  the array, where a compiled `my \x = @a[1]` missed the write too.
  t/regression/bound-slice-is-a-list.raku agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **`--exe`: an unpassed `:@a` or `:%h` is an empty container of its type**
  (a1bb0e7b; issue #191). Every natively compiled routine bound it to Any:
  `sub MAIN(Str :@mode)` given no --mode had Any, and `@mode.push` died. It
  is `Array[Str].new()`, `[]` or `{}` now, as interpreted and in Rakudo.
  t/regression/named-container-param-absent-native.raku, which t/run.raku
  compiles natively, agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **Arithmetic on a Match numifies the text it matched** (7f28cba0; issue
  #189). A Match operand took the double path: `0 + $0` of ｢42｣ was 42e0,
  and compiled code, which writes `+$0` as `0 + $0`, failed a `--> Int`
  return with it. It takes a Str's exact arm now, an Int, Rat or Num by the
  literal. t/regression/match-numifies-exact-native.raku, which t/run.raku
  compiles natively, agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **A map or grep a routine returned is read whole by the built-in subs
  and operators** (c5fd7f1d). Since c0691dff made such a map lazy,
  `flat(f(), $x)`, `slip`, `cache`, `zip`, `cross`, `any`, `first`, infix
  `X` and `[\+]` saw it empty: Cro's ConnectionConditional ran none of its
  transforms. Every built-in sub now drains a finite lazy argument, as the
  methods already did, and so do Z, X and the reduce; a returned map's
  `.head` still runs one block.
  t/regression/returned-map-read-by-builtins.raku agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **A module loaded from the parse cache keeps the flags the parser set on
  its calls** (d4ed6cac; issue #60). With `--precomp-modules=on`, every
  load after the first ran a returned map's every block where `.head` reads
  one, called one candidate for `.+m`, curried on through `((*.flip))`, and
  threw the Failure `so f()` tests under `use fatal`: the AST cache wrote
  none of those flags (AST cache v55). `rakupp install FunctionalParsers`
  failed its t/06 that way, once 00-sanity had cached the module. The
  install trace now records the parse-cache setting and where it came from.
  t/regression/precomp-method-call-flags.raku, three runs against one cache,
  agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **A module's name stays the module beside a type inside it whose name
  ends with it** (480f6850). The tails of a class's qualified name are
  short aliases, and `Cro::RPC::JSON.^name` answered X::Cro::RPC::JSON, the
  role that dist declares. t/regression/module-name-not-taken-by-nested-type.raku
  agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **`sprintf('%s', $e)` prints an exception's message** (594d4aac), as `~$e`
  and `$e.Str` do, when its class writes only `message`: `%s` asked an
  object for its Str only when the class writes `Str`, and printed
  `X::FieldNotFound<4365607088>`. Template::Mustache logs every warning that
  way. t/regression/sprintf-s-exception-message.raku agrees with Rakudo
  2026.09.
- **An element bound to another reads as its value everywhere**
  (594d4aac). After `%specs<x> := %data<tests>`, prefix `+` and `-`, the zen
  slices `<>` and `[]`, and `@(…)` read the binding's Proxy record, so `+`
  of four tests was 3 and `for %specs<x><>` walked the record; they now read
  through it as `~` does, and through a user's Proxy (its FETCH) the same
  way. Template::Mustache's spec runner planned 3 tests for each file of 11.
  t/regression/bound-element-reads-through-proxy.raku agrees with Rakudo
  2026.09.
- **`.AST.EVAL` runs the declarations a program wrote** (acf27e4b): the view
  built them without what they say beyond their name, and rendered valid
  Raku meaning something else. `has $.x is rw` came back as `has $.x`, whose
  accessor does not assign; `sub f($a is rw)` as `sub f($a)`; `class C is Int
  does R` as `class C`; `our sub f` as `sub f`, unreachable from its package;
  `if $x -> $y { … }` (and `elsif`, `else`, `with`, `given`) with no binder;
  `for @a -> Int $i` and `for @a -> ($p, $q)` with no signature at all; `no
  strict` as `use strict`; `use lib "x"` with no path; `$x.=abs` as `$x.abs`,
  which leaves `$x` alone. Each now renders as Rakudo 2026.09 writes it, and
  in a localization too. A `<->` block renders `-> $x is rw`, the same block,
  as the parse records no more than that. `sub f(Int)` no longer renders
  `sub f(Int )`. `no` with a module, a `.=` through `>>.`, `.?`, `!` or `.^`,
  and a sub-signature with `-->` are refused by name, as before for anything
  without a view. t/regression/rakuast-view-declarations.raku runs each shape
  both as `EVAL $src` and as `$src.AST.EVAL` and agrees with Rakudo 2026.09's
  rendering of each. The round-trip harness, which asks only that the text
  parse again, had passed all of them, and counted examples/dirstat/dirstat.raku and
  examples/factorize.raku as round-tripping while a pointy block in each lost
  its destructuring binder.
- **`.AST.EVAL` keeps the parentheses a program writes** (586c5160):
  `Q[(1 + 2) * 3].AST.EVAL` was 7, where Rakudo answers 9. The view built a
  Circumfix::Parentheses only for a list, so `(-$y) ** 2` was -9, `($t ?? 10
  !! 20) + 1` was 10, `(1 .. 4).sum` a Range and `(1 + 2; 3 + 4)` 7; every
  parenthesized expression now renders and runs as written. The parse
  records the parentheses on every expression for this (in spare bytes, so no
  node grows); the AST cache format moves to version 56, and a cached module
  is parsed once more after an upgrade. With them, `%h<a>` no longer renders
  as `%h<"a">`, a lookup of the key with its quotes, nor `%h<a b>` as `%h<["a",
  "b"]>`, and a subscript keeps its adverbs: `%h{"a"}:delete` deleted
  nothing, `@a[0]:exists` read the element, `%h<>:k` came back as `%h<*>`.
  tools/rakuast-roundtrip.raku reports `reparse 0`: showcase/eclipse and
  showcase/fourier rendered `(0 max 5) min 3` without its parentheses, which
  does not parse. t/regression/rakuast-view-parentheses.raku agrees with
  Rakudo 2026.09's rendering of 17 shapes and runs 13 of them both ways.
- **A `my token` held in a variable matches in `<$var>`** (89d80faa).
  `my $p = &NAME` binds the routine the declaration made, not a Regex
  value, and `<$p>`, a bare `$p` in a regex and `<{ &NAME }>` read its
  `.Str` as the pattern: `/<$p>/` matched nothing and said nothing, where
