  `<&NAME>` and `~~ $p` matched. Each now matches the declared source as
  its kind does: a token does not backtrack, a rule's whitespace is
  significant. URL::Find chooses its tokens that way (`my $base = $ascii ??
  &baseascii !! &base`), found no URL at all and failed its suite, so
  Data::ExampleDatasets, which needs it, would not install, and
  Mathematica::Serializer's examples/Serializer-profiling.raku, run from
  its GitHub URL, could not start. An install of Data::ExampleDatasets into
  an empty store now runs and passes all 29 suites, and the script reads
  the same 1,745 rows and writes the same WL and WXF text as Rakudo
  2026.09. t/regression/regex-routine-in-a-variable.raku agrees with Rakudo
  2026.09.
- **`--exe` reads a placeholder through its bare name** (9326ca7e). `$^c`
  declares the block's `$c`, which the body may name without the twigil
  after its first mention, and the C++ backend gave the two spellings two
  locals: `{ print $^c; say $c }` under an outer `my $c = 100` printed 100
  where Rakudo prints 42, and `sub f { $^x ~ $x }` or the same block without
  the outer `$c` did not compile, so `--exe` failed with no fallback. The
  same at v5.5.0; roast/S32-io/IO-Socket-INET.t reached it once 762ce0e2
  compiled its `:D` variables. A placeholder is now named by its bare name,
  as the interpreter binds it, and a block that declares that name again
  bundles. `tools/cpp-build-check.raku` goes from 59 accepted programs that
  do not compile to 57, none newly failing;
  t/regression/codegen-sub-placeholders.raku carries the cases, with
  Rakudo 2026.09's output.

### Fixed: crashes

- **Two candidates of one role that differ only by a named both survive
  composition** (4df715fc; issue #77). Red::AST::Infix's `new($l, $r,
  :$cast!)` and `new($l, $r)` share the positional key composition dedupes
  by, so a class adding its own `multi method new` dropped the plain one
  and `Red::AST::Concat.new($left, $right)` died "Default constructor for
  'Concat' only takes named arguments"; a role composed by copy dropped it
  too, and a second role with any `new` made the pair a conflict.
  t/regression/role-multi-siblings-differing-by-named.raku agrees with
  Rakudo 2026.09.
- **A return type check sees through a bound attribute's Proxy**
  (5ebfc05f; issue #77, FCO's item 2). `method model(--> Mu:U) { $!model :=
  $type; $!model }` died "expected Mu:U but got Proxy (BoundType)"; a
  smiley or a built-in return type now judges what the cell holds, and the
  container still goes back for `is rw`. Red resolves its lazy
  relationship models this way. t/regression/return-check-sees-through-bound-attribute.raku
  agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **`Lock.condition` is a Lock::ConditionVariable** (57a9a792; issue #77).
  It was a kind named LockCondition, and `Lock::ConditionVariable` named
  nothing, so Red's Transaction::Guard died declaring `has
  Lock::ConditionVariable $!available`. t/regression/lock-condition-variable-type.raku
  agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **A bare block's implicit `$_` parameter answers `.named_names`**
  (3c62ef44; issue #77, FCO's item 1), and the other fields every Parameter
  has; `{ .say }.signature.params[0].named_names` died NotFound. Red reads
  a transaction callback's parameters this way. t/regression/implicit-topic-parameter-introspects.raku
  agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **A worker thread's teardown no longer races the cycle collector**
  (e30e2827). A worker gave its count back at the end of its body, before its
  closure, ExecContext and frame pools were let go, and a collection on the
  main thread could walk containers that teardown was freeing: Roast's
  S07-hyperrace/basics.t segfaulted about one run in six. 300 runs, none.
- **`-O` compiles a `--> Int` sub of no parameters that is an int kernel**
  (e30e2827); its kernel guard named an `__a` its direct overload has not got.
- **Compiled `$failure.handled = True` works** (42f79e98); it died "Target is
  not assignable".
- **Workers auto-extending one array by index no longer realloc its buffer
  at once** (90bc6e23). Four workers storing `@r[$w * 10_000 + $_]` into an
  empty array died 8/8 in parallel mode (SIGABRT, SIGBUS, SIGSEGV); the
  growth now takes push's stripe, and t/stress/ub-indexed-grow.raku stages it.
  2/40 still crash: see Left open.
- **Compound assignment to a shared container no longer crashes**
  (12af490d). `$s ~= "a"` from two threads appended into one buffer at once,
  a double free on every run at 3,000 appends each. The other `OP=` arms
  computed from the slot and stored back unstriped: a Rat or a big Int
  `+=`, `,=`, `~= $obj`, a typed or subset variable, `Z~=`. Every arm now
  computes from the slot or a copy taken under its stripe and stores under
  it, and the generated accessor's fast reads take the stripe too. Of 48
  shapes, each run by two threads on one container, 33 crashed before and
  none do now. t/stress/ub-opassign.raku.
- **`push @a, …`, `pop @a` and `shift @a` from two threads no longer crash**
  (12af490d). The sub forms changed the array in place without the stripe
  the methods take: 3 runs in 6 crashed. t/stress/ub-array-push.raku has a
  sub-form phase, which crashed every run before.
- **Threads reading one shared hash no longer corrupt it** (b05e989b; issue
  #193). A plain Hash's Pairs hold its own containers, and the first Pair a
  hash handed out moved its entries into the generation the Pairs co-own, so
  `.pairs` was a write: eight `start` blocks taking it on one shared hash read
  it as `{}`, read its values as Nil, or died (SIGSEGV), and `run` copying
  %*ENV while another thread did `%*ENV.grep(…)` read it mid-move. That is
  `iz4 status`'s shape, which died about one run in a hundred naming a sub's
  own parameter. The entries stay where they are now until the hash lets them
  go, and the generation is made once, under a lock. A sub's lean call lane
  is published release/acquire too, so a thread no longer takes it with the
  slot layout another thread's first call is still building.
  t/race/shared-hash-pairs.raku: 36 runs in 50 failed at 5.2.1, none in 200
  now; t/stress/shared-hash-pairs.raku runs ThreadSanitizer-clean.
- **`%h{"k$i"} = $i` in a loop no longer crashes a GCC 15 build** (079427c0;
  issue #175). The loop kernel compiler read an interpolated string part's
  type before computing it, an order C++ leaves open: GCC 15.3 at `-O3`
  took it the wrong way round, and the kernel read an Int as a string
  (SIGSEGV, every run). The Clang builds never crashed.
- **Two threads refilling one array no longer abort the process**
  (c9560e0d; found with issue #215). Eight `start` workers each running `@a
  = …` on one array died every run, SIGTRAP or SIGABRT with nothing on
  stderr: the in-place refill move-assigned into the array's storage, and
  the second thread freed the buffer the first had freed. The refill takes
  the storage's stripe now. t/race/array-refill-shared.raku: 50 runs in 50
  died before, none in 200 now, in parallel mode and under RAKUPP_GIL=1.
- **`--exe` builds for another macOS deployment target beside a cached
  header** (d1c84b6b; issue #199). The precompiled header's name left out
  the target, which clang also takes from MACOSX_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET, so a
  build for 13.0 after one for 11.0 failed "PCH file was compiled for the
  target …". The name holds the compiler's predefined macros now, so each
  target gets a header of its own; t/run.raku builds for 11.0 and 13.0 with
  one cache on macOS.
- **A utf8-c8 string holding a stray continuation byte answers positions on
  every call** (94172cb6). The empty table cached to mean "no grapheme
  table" was handed back as a table from the second call on: `.index` from a
  position missed, `.substr` saw -1 characters, and `.rindex` died of
  SIGSEGV. t/regression/utf8-c8-stray-byte-positions.raku agrees with Rakudo
  2026.09.
- **`--exe` and the other compile modes embed a module the program finds
  through its own `use lib`** (392873ab). The module graph was collected
  from the command line's search path alone, so such a module was left on
  disk and loaded at run time, interpreted, and a binary moved without it
  died. Issue #189's program was built that way. t/run.raku builds two such
  programs and runs them with the module's directory gone.
- **Two threads no longer replace one `$/` at once** (a92d9cfd). A subset's
  `where /…/` checked off the main thread set the `$/` of the scope the
  subset was declared in, which every thread shares, and a grammar's inline
  block saved and restored `$/` past its routine: the old value was freed
  twice. Cro::HTTP checks such a subset for each request it builds, and its
  session test, whose two clients run at once, crashed in about one run in
  four. t/regression/match-var-shared-scope-across-threads.raku agrees with
  Rakudo 2026.09.
- **Four more races under Cro's WebSocket and session tests** (0d089427),
  found with ThreadSanitizer: an exception type made on the fly went into
  the class table that other threads read without a lock (a lookup lost in
  the rehash made `DeepCompareRec.new` die "No such method 'new'"); a tap
  record gained a key while another thread read it; and a supply's
  completion flags and the cycle-break counter were plain fields several
  threads changed, so two of them could both fire a supply's `done`.

### Fixed: what FatRoast found

FatRoast's fattened Roast (strings-v1.mixed, names-v1.mixed, ops-v1; Roast
da425eb92) through tools/run-fatroast.raku, rakupp against Rakudo 2026.09
run the same way. Every fattened file Rakudo passes now passes here; fully
passing, before → after (Rakudo): strings-v1 537 → 539 (507), names-v1 933 →
951 (933), ops-v1 428 → 428 (418; infinity.t gained, one S17 file lost to
the 10 s limit under -j load in each run). What is left fails on Rakudo too:
the v1 transforms broke it, and their v2 (the last paragraph) does not.

- **A variable named beyond ASCII interpolates into a pattern and a
  replacement** (9d48b3b5): `/$é/`, `/@𝐯/`, `/"$ч"/`, `<$甲>`, `/$*ж/`,
  `s/q/$ч/`, `%ü<k>` and `@é[1]` in a replacement. Each scanner read `$é` as
  `$` and a literal `é`.
- **A twigil takes a name in any script** (9d48b3b5): `has $.ч` made no
  accessor, `$!ч` was "Two terms in a row", `$*ч` not assignable, `$^ч` and
  `$:ч` routines.
- **A type named beyond ASCII is a type to the parser's guesses** (9d48b3b5):
  `Éx:D` was the call `Éx(:D)`; `Ро[Int]`, `Éx .new`, `new Еx:` and
  `handles Бэк` likewise.
- **`万` and the other CJK ideographs with a numeric value are letters**
  (9d48b3b5), as in Rakudo: `my class 万 {}` was "Undefined routine 'class'" and
  `sub 万` the number 10000.
- **A name that starts with a quote word and goes on beyond ASCII is that
  name** (9d48b3b5): `class Q̲x` made every later `Q { … }` a type name, and
  `Q̲x.^name` a Q quote; `?&е` was the infix `?&`.
- **`.samecase`, `s:ii` and `:ignoremark` work by grapheme in any script**
  (9d48b3b5): a combining mark took a pattern position, `s:ii` was ASCII-only,
  `m:m/ w̲x /` never matched and `:i:m` folded ASCII only.
- **EVAL refuses an undeclared variable before an undeclared routine**
  (9d48b3b5), as Rakudo does: `EVAL '{ my $v = 1 }; nope $v'` is X::Undeclared.
- **`my Int $x = ∞`'s exception carries Inf** (9d48b3b5), and says so; `.value`
  was Any.
- **`/:my $x = 1; a/` compiles** (9d48b3b5); a leading `:my` was taken for an
  adverb and the `;` refused.
- **`--highlight` reads the Unicode quotes, hyper operators, word lists
  after a listop and in `<<…>>`/`«…»`, the Whatever `*`, ranges, shifts,
  `q(…)` calls, a routine named `s`, `:sym<…>` token names and radix
  numbers** (184c423c). FatRoast places its edits by these spans: `“…”`,
  `‘…’` and `｢…｣` were no strings, `is <a b>` no word list, `>>+<<` began a
  `< … >` word list that swapped strings and code to the end of the line,
  and a `"…"` ended at a quote inside its `{…}` block. Fattened again with
  this build, the v1 files `rakupp -c` refuses while their plain form
  compiles go 61 → 55, 49 → 48 and 8 → 7; FatRoast's v2 transforms bring
  each to 0.
- **`val('NaN+Inf\i')`, `val('Inf\i')` and `val('NaN\i')` are ComplexStr**
  (72e0c5fb), as in Rakudo; they were Str, refused for carrying no digit. And
  a word-list word reads Inf and NaN as val() does: `<0+NaNi>` was a Complex
  where Rakudo keeps a Str.
- **A Complex and a plain Str order as strings** (72e0c5fb): `"abc" cmp
  <0+0i>` was Same; and `1 before <1+0i>` follows its own `cmp`.
- **`IntStr.new` and the other allomorph constructors check what they bind**
  (72e0c5fb): `IntStr.new("x", "y")` made an IntStr of two Strs, where Rakudo
  dies.
- **`.indices` reads a Cool number as its Str** (72e0c5fb): `422.indices(2)`
  was "No such method". Roast's S32-str/indices.t died there and had been
  counted passing; see the next entry.
- **run-roast counts a run that printed no plan as no pass** (316e5855): a file
  that died before its `done-testing`, every test so far passing, was
  PASS. Raku++'s S32-str/indices.t was a false `[PASS] 18/18` in every run of
  Roast, and Rakudo's fattened S32-str/val.t `[PASS] 93/93`.

FatRoast's v2 transforms (strings-v2, names-v2, ops-v2, made in the FatRoast
repo beside these fixes) leave alone what v1 broke on every engine. Fully
passing under the corrected harness, Raku++ / Rakudo 2026.09, v1 → v2:
strings 538 / 507 of 1413 → 675 / 648 of 1414; names 951 / 933 of 1181 →
1022 / 1010 of 1166; ops 429 / 418 of 446 → 422 / 420 of 422. Plain Roast:
1419 / 1396 of 1419. In none of the seven does Rakudo pass a file Raku++
does not.

### Behaves like Rakudo

- **A Jupyter cell that printed to stdout shows no value** (3c80bc24), the
  rule the rakupp prompt has followed since issue #66 and Rakudo's REPL
  follows: `say 42` shows 42, not 42 and then True. The bytes decide, so
  `print ""` still shows its value and stderr does not count. A typed
  value (`#% html` and the like) is shown regardless, because the magic
  asked for it, and a printing `%% always` entry is the cell printing.
  tools/jupyter-smoke.raku, 62 checks, pins each case.
- **An undeclared routine in the main program is a compile-time report with
  its line** (c7a12943): `say length("Riga")` printed a run-time "Undefined
  routine 'length'" at line 0, and now prints Rakudo's ===SORRY!===
  "Undeclared routine: length used at line 1. Did you mean 'elems', 'chars',
  'codes'?", every such name at once, with Rakudo's fixed hints (`local`,
  `foreach`, `quit`, a phaser in lower case, …). `len` is no longer "Did you
  mean 'leg'?".
- **A source read live but finite is not lazy** (12af490d). `Channel.list`,
  a handle's `.lines` (standard input included), a process's output and a
  `Seq.from-loop` with a condition answered `.is-lazy` True, and an Array
  assigned from one stayed lazy; Rakudo says False and reads them to the
  end. A `for` still walks them one item at a time, and `lazy` still
  declares one lazy. t/regression/live-finite-sources-not-lazy.raku agrees
  with Rakudo 2026.09.

- **A module that exports nothing has no EXPORT package** (5d31d4a2; issue
  #202). Every loaded module named `Mod::EXPORT`, so `Circle::.keys` was
  ($pi EXPORT) where Rakudo says ($pi). Only a unit that declares something
  `is export` (a routine, variable, constant, type, enum or subset) or an
  `EXPORT::` package of its own has one now; a subset's `is export` is
  recorded for it (AST cache v50).
  t/regression/issue-202-stash-our-variable-lookup.raku agrees with Rakudo
  2026.09.
- **EXPORT holds every exported symbol, under each of its tags** (aff5032b;
  issue #202). `Mod::EXPORT::DEFAULT` and `::ALL` listed only routines, a
  tagged routine sat under DEFAULT, and no tag package of its own existed.
  A symbol is in each tag it names (DEFAULT when none) and ALL now, the tag
  packages are listed in `Mod::EXPORT`, and a variable's entry is its
  container. A package declared in the program, a class, and every package
  around an exporting one have an EXPORT, as Rakudo installs a symbol in
  the EXPORT of each package it is declared within.
- **A package's Stash lists its subsets and modules, and `Foo::<$x>` takes
  every adverb** (aff5032b; issue #202). `Foo::<$x>:p` was the value and a
  Pair `p => True`; `:k`, `:v`, `:kv`, `:!p`, a slice and `:delete` work as
  on `Foo::{…}`, and `:delete` removes the symbol from the package
  (`M::<$x>:delete` was a parse error).
  t/regression/export-tags-and-package-stashes.raku agrees with Rakudo
  2026.09.
- **A file required by its path is that file, and its `sub MAIN` runs**
  (cb5b360a). `require "/abs/Build.pm6"` loaded nothing and said nothing:
  the path was looked up as a module name. `require "path"` and `require
  Stub:file(path)` now load the file named (an absolute path as it stands, a
  relative one under each search-path directory) and run its `sub MAIN` on
  @*ARGS after its mainline, once; a `use` of it still does not, and a
  missing absolute path is X::NotFoundInRepository. `rakupp install` runs a
  `Build.*` hook with zef's own command, which requires it by path, so a hook
  that is only a `sub MAIN` script runs (Timezones::ZoneInfo's died with
  "Undeclared name 'Build'").
- **`:4m, :5s` is two pairs** (cb5b360a): after the digits of a `:4m` pair
  the lexer opened an `m,…,` match (`rx,` too), "Malformed radix number".
- **`samewith` works in a routine that is not a multi** (cb5b360a), calling
  the routine it is written in again with the new arguments, as Rakudo
  does; it died with X::NoDispatcher.
  Timezones::ZoneInfo 0.5.0 now builds, passes its suite and installs. The
  three t/regression files (require-path-loads-file-runs-main,
  colonpair-digits-regex-key, samewith-only-routine) agree with Rakudo
  2026.09.
- **A module may extend the core DateTime in place, and `rakupp install
  DateTime::Timezones` installs** (d5184116). `CORE::DateTime` is the
  setting's DateTime, as a term and as a parameter type, whatever the scope
  has made of the short name; `class DateTime is DateTime` in a module is a
  subclass of the core one (only `class A is A`, with nothing outside, is
  refused); `.wrap` of a core method's object
  (`DateTime.^find_method('in-timezone').wrap(…)`) wraps it for every
  DateTime, where it was accepted and ignored, and `callsame` reaches the
  core method; a sub called with a method call's invocant first (a
  `.^add_fallback` method, `$d.&up`) takes the invocant's container, so an
  `is rw` parameter assigns the caller's variable and a multi picks its
  `is rw` candidate only for one (`3.&m`, `my \d = DateTime.new(…)` take the
  other). A class deriving DateTime keeps its class through `±` a Duration
  (`$dt - $day` was a Num), and through `.later`, `.in-timezone`, `.clone`
  and kin; a difference is a Duration, `$dt + 60` an Instant, and
  `DateTime but R` keeps its role. DateTime::Timezones 0.4.2 installs with
  its suite green, and a program using it prints what Rakudo prints from the
  same store. t/regression/core-class-extended-in-place.raku agrees with
  Rakudo 2026.09.
- **What was found beside it** (50aebf54), each the same on v5.5.0 before.
  An engine object kept in a hash (a DateTime, Date, Promise, Channel, Lock,
  …) is ONE element: `my @a = DateTime.new(0)` held eight Pairs, a Lock
  none, and `.elems` was 8; a Set, Bag or Mix in a `$` parameter or loop
  variable is one item. A DateTime against a plain number is its Instant:
  `$d + 60` is Instant:70 (it moved the date), `$d * 2` 20 (it was 0), `$d
  == 10` True; a Duration still moves it. `sub f(CORE::Int $x)` checks its
  argument, at compile time too, and its `.signature` is `:(Int $x)`
  (Param.typeIsSetting, AST cache v52). `&?ROUTINE` is the routine a block is
  written in, after it returns and from a `start` block as well (it named
  whichever routine ran the block), and `samewith` calls that one. `my \x =
  @a.first`, `$x := @a.tail`, `for @a.head(2) { $_ = 7 }` and
  `@a.grep(…).first` reach the elements' own containers. A top-level `class
  DateTime is CORE::DateTime`, the core class's own name outside a package,
  is refused as X::NYI where it crashed. The five t/regression files
  (engine-objects-list-as-one, datetime-number-is-instant,
  core-qualified-parameter-type, routine-is-lexical,
  array-ends-are-containers) agree with Rakudo 2026.09.

- **A capitalized sub takes a block as its argument** (67a2fe76; issue
  #198). `sub Foo(&bar) { bar }; Foo { say "bar" }` died "Calling Foo()
  will never work with declared signature (&bar)" where `foo { … }` worked:
  every capitalized name before a `{` was read as a type with a block after
  it, so Foo was called with nothing and the block ran on its own. A name
  the unit declares a routine of, above or below the call, or a `use`d
  module does, and no type has, is now the listop Rakudo reads, in an
  interpolated `"{ … }"` and an EVAL too. `when Int { }` and `if $x ~~ Pt
  { }` keep the type. t/regression/issue-198-titlecase-sub-block-arg.raku
  agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **A listop before a block in a statement condition takes it, as Rakudo's
  xblock does** (4af53b07). In `if`, `unless`, `elsif`, `with`, `without`,
  `while`, `until`, `repeat while`, `for`, `given` and `when` the first
  block after a condition's listop was always the statement's: `if foo { 1
  } { say "yes" }` called foo with nothing, and `if bar { say "t" }`, `for
  lines { .say }` and `for foo -> $x { }` ran where Rakudo refuses them.
  `whenever` read its supply as a plain expression, so `whenever tick { … }`
  handed tick the block and died at run time. The listop now takes the
  block or pointy, the statement's own comes after it, and with none the
  program is refused at compile time by Rakudo's group:
  X::Syntax::BlockGobbled ("Function 'bar' needs parens to avoid gobbling
  block", with "or perhaps it's a class" only for a type-like name), naming
  the innermost listop, and X::Syntax::Missing. A term, a type (a native
  one too) and a statement prefix keep the block for the statement (`if
  now`, `if rand`, `if i`, `for buf8 -> \bt`, `for uint64 -> \T`, `for
  gather { … } { }`), and so does a class a `use`d module exports, which
  67a2fe76 had counted among its subs, harmlessly until now
  (Statistics::Distributions's `when $_ ~~ Generic { }`).
  t/regression/xblock-listop-in-condition.raku agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **A `%` parameter takes an Associative and nothing else** (3ddcd58b; issue
  #207). `sub g(:%env) { say %env<LC_ALL> }; g(:env(LC_ALL => 'C', LANG =>
  'C'))` printed C where Rakudo dies at the call: a List of Pairs is no
  hash, and Rakudo builds none from one, so a test file written that way
  passed here and died partway there. A named `%` parameter made a Hash of
  whatever came (an Int, Nil, a Range, a Match, an object that is no
  Associative), a positional one refused only a definite scalar, a default
  was never checked, and `for`, `given` and `with … -> %h` bound by name
  only, which left a List in `%h`. Each now refuses, `is copy` too, with
  Rakudo's message: the type alone, the "You have to pass an explicitly
  typed hash" hint for a List, an Array, a Range or code, word-wrapped where
  Rakudo wraps it. What does bind is itself: a Pair stays a Pair (`for
  %h.pairs -> %p`, as in Rakudo), and neither an Associative object passed
  by name nor an Associative type object (Pair, Bag, a class that does
  Associative) is made a Hash any more. A loop with a `%` parameter now
  binds each element through bindParams, as a typed or `is copy` one does:
  `for @h -> %h { $s += %h<a> }` over 200,000 hashes, interpreted, best of
  3 against a build of 170bb19a, 0.022 → 0.048 s (`-> $h`: 0.021).
  t/regression/issue-207-hash-param-takes-associative.raku agrees with
  Rakudo 2026.09.
- **A block's `}` ends a statement only at the end of its line, in a file
  too** (2b9494ae; issue #206). `given $x { when 'a' { 'A' } when 'b' { 'B' }
  }` on one line ran here where Rakudo refuses to compile it, so a module
  that passed its whole suite here failed to load there. An EVAL already
  refused it; a file skipped the check, and a class, role, grammar or module
  body had none at all (`class A { method a { 1 } method b { 2 } }`). Each
  is now refused at compile time as X::Syntax::Confused, in words of our own
  rather than Rakudo's "Strange text after block (missing semicolon or
  comma?)": `Missing ';' before 'when': a block's '}' ends a statement only
  at the end of a line`, with a `^` under the excerpt where the next
  statement starts. An infix after a package declaration still parses
  (Roast's `class {} − class {}`). Six of this repository's files were
  written so, and Rakudo refused each: showcase/python/python.raku, two
  t/regression files and three t/cnp and t/jit cases have their `;` now. A
  `-c` scan of 3,736 files (Roast, the modules installed for both engines,
  Rakudo's core setting, this repository) finds no other file refused.
  t/regression/issue-206-text-after-block.raku agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **NativeLibs loads quietly beside Cro** (0e44c5a6; issue #211). A Cro
  app using DBIish printed "Module NativeLibs EXPORT failed: No such method
  'dispatcher' for invocant of type 'Any'" once per `use NativeLibs`:
  its EXPORT looks for the `is native` candidate among
  `&trait_mod:<is>.candidates`, which listed only Cro::HTTP::Router's `is
  query`/`is auth` multis once they were in scope. A user `trait_mod:<is>`
  group lists the engine's own trait candidates ahead of its own now, as a
  Rakudo multi joins CORE's group, each with that group as its
  .dispatcher. t/regression/issue-211-trait-is-candidates.raku agrees with
  Rakudo 2026.09.
- **`rakupp install AttrX::Lazy` installs** (8c871a25). A metaclass
  `compose` that asks `.^method_table`, `.^method_names` or `.^methods`
  before its `callsame` sees the methods the class declared and no
  attribute accessors, as in Rakudo, which makes those in the base compose.
  AttrX::Lazy's `is lazy` checks the table there to tell a user's `method
  bar` from a lazy `$.bar`, so its own suite died on its first class with
  "A method 'bar' already exists"; AttrX::Lazy 0.0.3 now passes its three
  test files and installs. t/regression/how-compose-sees-declared-methods.raku
  agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **A LEAVE that dies while a method unwinds leaves its caller's scope in
  place** (099a2055; issue #205). The caller's CATCH ran in the method's scope
  and died with X::Undeclared naming the CATCH's own variable; it handles
  the LEAVE's error now, as a sub's caller already did.
  t/regression/method-leave-dies-unwinding.raku agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **`rakupp install https://github.com/antononcube/Raku-Woxi` installs**
  (642a320a). A `try` or `quietly` hands its block's value on in its
  container, as `do` does, so `try { $result = await $proc; CATCH { … } }`
  no longer sinks a failed Proc after the try. And a CATCH or CONTROL
  written last is a block's last statement, as it already was a
  routine's: what precedes it runs in sink context inside the block, into
  that CATCH, and the block answers Nil. `try { await $killed; CATCH {
  default { } } }` threw the killed child's X::Proc::Unsuccessful from
  outside the try, and `(1, 2).map({ $_ * 2; CATCH { … } })` answered
  (2 4) where Rakudo answers (Nil Nil). Woxi 0.0.1 now passes its 14 tests
  and installs. t/regression/try-block-sink-and-trailing-catch.raku agrees
  with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **`<42>.parse-base(16)` is 66, and a number matches on its Str**
  (62a3d9a5; issue #212). An allomorph (IntStr, RatStr, NumStr) had no
  `.parse-base`, and `.match` with a literal needle answered a Str only, so
  `42.match("4")` died, and so did a MAIN argument that looked like a
  number. Cool.match is `self.Str.match` now, as in Rakudo, and a needle
  that is no Regex is matched as its Str.
  t/regression/allomorph-str-methods.raku agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **`IO::Path.chown` and `chown`** (17a71667; issue #213). An id left out keeps
  the file's own, the method answers True, and a missing file or a refused
  chown(2) is a Failure carrying X::IO::Chown with Rakudo's message; the
  sub answers the paths it changed. The sandbox counts both as writes.
  t/regression/io-path-chown.raku agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **A module's own `sub q` stays its own** (46c4ef60; issue #194). A module's
  non-exported `sub q`, `sub s` or `sub tr` made `q{…}`, `s///` or `tr///`
  a call in every file that used it, and so did one a module further down.
  Only a sub the import can bring in shadows a quote word now: one marked
  `is export`, or any of them when the module has an EXPORT sub.
  t/regression/module-quote-word-sub-scope.raku agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **`proto f(|)` keeps its signature beside a declared `infix:<(|)>`**
  (cbbeeb07; issue #60). With the operator declared anywhere in the file, or
  imported, `(|)` after a routine declarator or glued to a name lexed as that
  operator, so the proto took no arguments and every call died "will never
  work with signature of the proto ()"; `sub g(&)` beside `infix:<(&)>` broke
  the same way. A declared `(…)` spelling yields where the built-in set
  operators do now. FunctionalParsers 0.1.11 loads again: 5 of its 18 test
  files pass, the rest on faults of their own.
  t/regression/paren-signature-beside-declared-set-op.raku agrees with Rakudo
  2026.09.
- **A code constant exported under a tag comes in by that tag** (55e91563;
  issue #60). `constant &sp is export(:shortcuts) = &drop-spaces` had no tags
  on record, so it was taken for a plain `is export`: `use Mod :shortcuts`
  left it out ("Undefined routine 'sp'") and a bare `use Mod` let it in. A
  code constant or `our &var` is held to its own tags now, as a sub is.
  FunctionalParsers 0.1.11 passes 13 of its 18 test files with this, up
  from 5. t/regression/tagged-code-constant-export.raku agrees with Rakudo
  2026.09.
- **A map or grep a routine or closure returns is lazy** (c0691dff; issue
  #60). `sub f { @a.map(…) }; f().head` ran the block over every element;
  it runs it once now, a Seq kept from `f()` runs as it is read, and a sunk
  one still runs where it stands. `.is-lazy` says False for a map or grep
  over a finite list, as Rakudo's does, `.append`/`.prepend` read such a
  Seq to its end, and `.sum`/`.min`/`.max` fold it in place. perf-guard
  within noise; a returned map's `.head` is 89% faster and `[+]` over one
  ~10% slower. A map written where it is stored, and `for` over a map,
  stay eager (TODO.md). FunctionalParsers 0.1.11 passes 14 of its 18 test
  files. t/regression/returned-map-is-lazy.raku agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **`&{ … }` is a term** (bf64a45d; issue #60), the block itself, as `&( … )`
  is its contents: `my &f = &{ $_ * 2 }` was "unexpected operator in term
  position", and after a listop the `&` was the infix junction (`say &{ 42
  }()` said an empty line). Braces that are a hash stay one (`&{ a => 1 }`),
  and after a term `&` is still a junction. FunctionalParsers 0.1.11 passes
  15 of its 18 test files. t/regression/amp-brace-term-is-the-block.raku
  agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **A backslash in an operator's name escapes an angle bracket** (12015e48;
  issue #60). `sub infix:<(\<&)>` declares `(<&)` and `infix:<(&\>)>`
  declares `(&>)`, as in Rakudo, where `1 (<&) 2` was a word-list parse error
  and `&infix:<(\<&)>` an undefined routine; `\\` is `\`, and any other
  backslash stays (`infix:<\o/>`).
  t/regression/escaped-operator-name.raku agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **An imported operator lexes whole, as the file's own declaration does**
  (25781bde; issue #60). The lexer learned which spellings to read whole from
  the file's own declarations only, so a spelling the built-in rules split
  stayed split when a module exported it: `1 (<&) 2` was a word-list parse
  error, `(+&)`, `⨁⨁` and `√√` were "unexpected operator in term position",
  `1 +++ 2` read as `1++ + 2`, and a circumfix `[[ ]]` silently made the
  array `[6]`. A `use` that brings such a spelling in lexes the rest of the
  unit again knowing it, once, as an imported `sub tr` already did; a module
  that exports none costs nothing. That re-lex keeps an L10N rewrite now,
  where `use L10N::XX` before a module exporting `sub tr` left the
  keywords after it untranslated, and a slang's re-lex keeps the imported
  quote words and operators. FunctionalParsers' t/01 and t/12 parse now, and
  fail on the operators' traits, the next entry.
  t/regression/imported-operator-spellings.raku agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **An imported operator keeps its precedence and associativity traits**
  (f417ab7e; issue #60). The importing file took a module's operators by name
  only, so each bound at the default additive precedence, left-associative:
  an exported `is assoc<right>` nested left, and `is equiv(&infix:<&&>)`
  under `is equiv(&infix:<||>)` made one long left-nested chain. `is
  tighter`, `is looser`, `is equiv` and `is assoc` are read off the module
  now, by the reader the file's own declarations use. With it, declared in
  the file or imported, `is equiv(&[**])` binds as `**` (it stayed
  additive), and a `sub` that gives a built-in spelling traits, such as
  `sub infix:<(&)> is equiv(&infix:<&&>)`, binds as its traits say instead
  of as the set operator; a `multi` candidate leaves the built-in alone.
  FunctionalParsers 0.1.11 passes 17 of its 18 test files, up from 15.
  t/regression/imported-operator-traits.raku agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **An enum's type object called from a variable or a container coerces as
  its name does** (f3fb83e3; issue #216). `MyEnum(2)` was CCC, but the same
  type object taken out of a Hash, an Array element, a Scalar, a parameter
  or an attribute and called, `$r<x>(2)` or `$t(2)`, died "No such method
  'CALL-ME' for invocant of type 'MyEnum'". It answers the member now, or
  the X::Enum::NoValue Failure the call by name gives. Held in a Scalar, the
  type object is the type to everything else that asks too: `$t.raku` is
  MyEnum (it was the pair list behind it), `$t ~~ Int` is True, `.^mro`
  starts with MyEnum, and `my Int $i = $t` no longer dies.
  t/regression/enum-type-called-from-a-container.raku agrees with Rakudo
  2026.09.
- **An enum's type object is one undefined item, not the list of its pairs**
  (5e611fa3; issue #216). Nested in a list, a hash or a pair it rendered as
  `[AAA => 0 BBB => 1 …]`, and `say %(x => MyEnum)` with it; interpolated,
  joined or concatenated it was "AAA\t0 BBB\t1 …"; `.elems` was 4, `my @a =
  MyEnum` took four Pairs and `for MyEnum` ran four times; it was True, `+MyEnum`
  was 4, and `.pop` took a member out of the enum for good. It gists as
  `(MyEnum)` and `.raku`s as `MyEnum` wherever it is, is "" in string context
  with the uninitialized warning, is one element, False, refuses to be a
  number (X::Constructor::BadType, or X::Numeric::Uninitialized under an
  infix) and refuses `.push` and `.pop`, as in Rakudo; `.keys`, `.pairs`,
  `.pick` and the rest of Enumeration still read the members. A declared
  enum's or subset's name before `~` is a term now, as a class's was: `MyEnum
  ~ "x"` was the coercion `MyEnum(~"x")`, the first member.
  t/regression/enum-type-object-is-one-item.raku agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **A kept `@a.head(n)` or `@a.tail(n)` holds the array's elements**
  (67c35490; issue #215). `my \h = @a.head(2); @a[0] = 42` left `h` as
  (1, 2), and so did `my $h = @a.head(2)` read before the write: `.head`
  copied the elements when it ran. Assigned or bound to a variable, a name or one
  element, it holds the array's positions now, as Rakudo's holds its
  Scalars: a write to the array shows through it, a write through it
  (`$h[0] = 5`, `for h { $_++ }`) reaches the array, and a typed array's
  element still checks what it takes. `@a = …` makes new containers in
  Rakudo, so a refill leaves the Seq the elements the array had, and two
  Seqs over the array keep sharing them. A `.head(n)` nothing keeps (summed,
  passed on, assigned to an `@` array) copies as before. `--exe` bundles a
  program that keeps one and writes the array.
  t/regression/array-head-keeps-element-containers.raku agrees with Rakudo
  2026.09.
- **A constant's initializer or a BEGIN block that calls a routine declared
  further down is "Undeclared routine"** (f303f0c0; issue #196). Such a call
  ran, with the routine rakupp had already declared, and read whatever it
  read that was not set yet as Any; Rakudo refuses the file before running
  any of it. The call check finds it now, as far as Rakudo looks: what the
  initializer names outside a nested block, run or not, and what the body of
  a routine above it that it surely calls names. A `-c` scan of 3,489 files
  refuses none for it.
  t/regression/issue-196-compile-time-call-declared-later.raku agrees with
  Rakudo 2026.09.
- **Compiled prefix `+` of a string or Match that is no number is a
  Failure** (43341539), as interpreted and in Rakudo, where it died "Cannot
  convert string to number"; the interpreter's `+` of such a Match is that
  Failure too, where it was Any.
  t/regression/prefix-plus-failure-native.raku, which t/run.raku compiles
  natively, agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **A grammar's HOW is a Metamodel::GrammarHOW** (78c4f478), which is a
  ClassHOW, as in Rakudo: `G.HOW ~~ Metamodel::GrammarHOW` was False and its
  `.^name` said ClassHOW. Found checking issue #214.
  t/regression/grammar-how-is-grammarhow.raku agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **A binary carries its constants and what its BEGIN blocks computed, as a
  precompiled Rakudo module does** (946d29e4; issue #190). `--exe`, `--aot`
  and `--bundle` ran a constant's initializer and the BEGIN blocks when the
  binary started, so a constant that `slurp`s a file read it on the machine
  running the binary, and died where it was missing. The build runs the
  program's compile-time part now — BEGIN, CHECK, `use`, declarations,
  top-level constants, no mainline — and the binary carries each constant's
  value, each BEGIN value, and what the top-level BEGIN blocks left in the
  program's variables, written as Raku; none of that code runs at start. A
  value that is not plain data reading back `eqv` through its `.raku` (an
  object, a closure, a lazy list) is computed at start as before, and the
  build names it; so is a constant that reads the program's own variables or
  sits in a routine, and a BEGIN that reaches past them (EVAL, a package's
  stash) keeps them all running.
  docs/guide/CLI.md says what a build computes.
  t/regression/issue-190-binary-embeds-compile-time.raku checks all three
  modes with the files gone.
- **A supply whose tapper dies on an emit no longer quits or closes**
  (e9455bfa). The error comes out of `emit`, where a CATCH in the whenever
  body still gets it, and goes back to whoever delivered the value: a
  Supplier's `.emit`, the caller of `.tap`, or nobody for a Promise; the
  supply's next values still arrive, as in Rakudo, whose block CATCH cannot
  see what the tapper throws. IO::Socket::Async::SSL's listener lost every
  connection after one whose tap block died, which stopped `rakupp install
  cro` at its client-server test. A react tapping a supply now dies of its
  own whenever body's death itself, and that whenever's QUIT no longer sees
  it. t/regression/supply-tapper-death-is-not-a-quit.raku agrees with
  Rakudo 2026.09.
- **`whenever $obj` taps the Supply of a user class with a Supply method
  of its own** (be510e3a), as in Rakudo; the body ran once with the object
  as its value. IO::Socket::Async::SSL's upgrade test reads a line with
  `whenever $conn.head` and got the socket. With e9455bfa its suite is
  green under `rakupp install`.
  t/regression/whenever-coerces-user-class-supply.raku agrees with Rakudo
  2026.09.
- **An enum a module declares outside any package is `our`** (5801c286):
  the program reaches its type and its members by their qualified names,
  where `use C; C::Idx::NotIndexed` was "Could not find symbol", and `enum
  G::Idx` inside `unit module G` is G's own Idx, not G::G::Idx, as in
  Rakudo. HTTP::HPACK's encoder test, which stopped `rakupp install cro`,
  passes. t/regression/module-enum-is-our.raku agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **A class, role or grammar a `use`d module declares is a type while the
  importing file is parsed** (7aa4256c): `for Cro::Iri -> $p { … }` was
  "Function 'Cro::Iri' needs parens to avoid gobbling block", which kept
  Cro::Core's uri test from compiling. A name nothing declares is still that
  error, as in Rakudo. t/regression/module-type-before-pointy-block.raku
  agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **A BUILD's attributive parameter that nobody passed stores its default
  in the attribute** (c4df7b96), through the attribute's type check, as in
  Rakudo: `has Str $.s` with `submethod BUILD(:$!s)` refuses the Any, and an
  attribute's own default is not applied after it. Cro::HTTP::Cookie.new
  lived without a name or a value, which stopped `rakupp install cro` at
  Cro::HTTP. t/regression/build-attributive-param-unpassed.raku agrees with
  Rakudo 2026.09.
- **An enum a module declares outside any package publishes its members'
  bare names to the program** (67b676cf), named or anonymous, as in Rakudo,
  and a `my enum` publishes nothing. Cro::HTTP2's `PROTOCOL_ERROR` and
  `data` were undeclared outside their modules, failing five of Cro::HTTP's
  test files. t/regression/module-enum-is-our.raku agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **A routine `is export` declared in a module's BEGIN block reaches the
  importer** (a81b6f91), joining a same-named multi there, and
  **`Metamodel::ClassHOW` is the setting's** even with a dist's own
  `…::Metamodel::ClassHOW` loaded. Cro::RPC::JSON declares its `is json-rpc`
  traits that way, and they were silently no traits at all.
  t/regression/module-trait-exported-from-begin.raku agrees with Rakudo
  2026.09.
- **`nqp::defined` exists under `use nqp`** (bd8936e7); Cro::RPC::JSON's
  metaclass roles died "Undefined routine". t/regression/lizmat-nqp-ops.raku
  agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **A Pair does not bind a named sub-signature that wants a positional, and
  `Hash:D(List:D(Pair:D))` is a nested coercion** (0938078e), not a type with
  a sub-signature: `is json-rpc(:async)` died "expected Str but got Pair".
  t/regression/named-subsig-pair-and-nested-coercion-smiley.raku agrees with
  Rakudo 2026.09.
- **Multi dispatch ranks three more pairs as Rakudo does** (858a037e):
  `:ws(:web-socket($websocket))` takes no `:websocket`; an `&block`
  parameter is a Callable, narrower than `Any:D $obj`; and a smiley weighs
  only once every type has tied, so `($j, @x)` beats `(Any:D $j, Any $x)` for
  a Positional type object. Each sent a Cro::RPC::JSON call to the wrong
  candidate. Roast's dispatch directories pass the same 188 files.
  t/regression/multi-callable-alias-and-smiley-ranking.raku agrees with
  Rakudo 2026.09.
- **`|@a` passes an Array's list and hash elements as items** (21c37405), so
  a `*@` slurpy keeps them whole: `f(|@a)` for `@a = 1, [2, 3]` is `[1, [2,
  3]]`. t/regression/slip-array-elements-stay-items.raku agrees with Rakudo
  2026.09.
- **A role's own metaobject keeps a mixin and hears of each class that does
  the role** (0b29ef00): `$*PACKAGE.HOW` in a role body is one
  ParametricRoleHOW per role, traits on the role declaration and
  `.^candidates[0].HOW` reach it, and a `specialize_with`, `add_method` or
  `incorporate_multi_candidates` that a mixin supplies is called as a class
  composes, as Rakudo does. A trait that dies in a class body no longer
  leaves the package prefix behind. Cro::RPC::JSON's trait and auth tests
  pass. t/regression/role-metaclass-mixin-hooks.raku agrees with Rakudo
  2026.09.
- **A role mixed into a routine keeps the attributes its methods write, and
  a subclass's dispatcher called directly reaches its parents' candidates**
  (9b3fbb8a): `meth does R; meth.set-name(…)` and `$obj.&m()` with `&m =
  Kid.^find_method('foo')`.
  t/regression/routine-mixin-attribute-and-direct-dispatcher.raku agrees
  with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **Dispatch asks a nested coercion's whole source chain** (de0d04e5): a
  bare `is json-rpc-actor` goes to `Bool:D :$json-rpc-actor!`, not to
  `Hash:D(List:D(Pair:D)) :$json-rpc-actor!`.
- **The core `trait_mod:<is>` candidates include `:$test-assertion!`**
  (3e15da85), so Test::Assertion adds none of its own and its suite
  passes. t/regression/core-trait-candidates-test-assertion.raku agrees with
  Rakudo 2026.09.
- **A typed `@` parameter's type names its class's own element type, and an
  Array held in a Scalar binds to `@` as itself** (2df02ed1):
  Positional[K::In], not Positional[In], and `Int @x` takes `@args[0]`
  holding an Array[Int]. t/regression/typed-positional-param-element-and-item.raku
  agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **`.on-close` wraps a supply without running it, and a CLOSE phaser sees
  its body's lexicals from the first `whenever`** (2adfaa34): on-close over
  a channel-fed supply hung, which hung every request Cro::HTTP::Test made.
  Cro::HTTP::Test's seven test files pass.
  t/regression/supply-on-close-and-close-phaser-scope.raku agrees with Rakudo
  2026.09.
- **A `whenever $promise` that settles later runs on a worker after `keep`
  returns, and in turn with its supply's other whenevers** (1676fc8a), as
  Rakudo's `.then` does: run inline, Cro::RPC::JSON's actor ended its
  supply from `whenever $close` before its input's end arrived.
  t/regression/promise-whenever-fires-later-and-in-turn.raku agrees with
  Rakudo 2026.09.
- **A react's `whenever $promise` body waits while another of the react's
  bodies runs** (116a638e), as Rakudo runs them one at a time. Fired later
  on a worker of its own, it ran at once beside the body that kept the
  promise, so `$v.keep(2); log 'after keep'` could log second and the
  promise's `done` could end the react before the keeper finished;
  promise-whenever-fires-later-and-in-turn.raku lost that race now and then,
  and its supply case, which raced the keeping thread's next line on a
  loaded box, holds its lock across `keep` (3c68ff57). A promise already
  kept when its whenever was registered ran its body inline, in the middle
  of the react's own body; it now runs after it.
  t/regression/react-promise-body-in-turn.raku agrees with Rakudo 2026.09,
  interpreted, under RAKUPP_GIL=1 and compiled.
- **A supply block's `whenever $promise` bodies run in the order their
  promises are kept** (7a419af4), as Rakudo's pool queue cues their
  `.then`s. Each fired on a pooled worker of its own, and the workers raced
  to the supply: ten kept in a row came out in another order in 19 of 20
  runs, where Rakudo 2026.09 keeps the order in 20 of 20, and
  supply-tapper-death-is-not-a-quit.raku's two, a TLS listener's shape, in
  6 of 200 runs with eight copies at once. The keep hands each delivery its
  turn, and a body's worker waits only until the one before it is queued or
  running. t/regression/supply-promise-whenevers-in-keep-order.raku agrees
  with Rakudo 2026.09, interpreted, under RAKUPP_GIL=1 and compiled.
- **`done` in a react whenever's LAST ends the react** (5b70d2e1) when the
  source finishes on another thread; Cro's WebSocket client waited out its
  timeout after the server closed.
  t/regression/react-last-done-from-another-thread.raku agrees with Rakudo
  2026.09.
- **A react's whenever over a Supplier runs its body as the react's**
  (9cac10e4), not as the supply block that emitted into the Supplier on that
  thread: a `whenever` in the body joined that supply block, so the react
  ended without waiting for it, and a `done` there ended the supply block.
  Cro::RPC::JSON's WebSocket test registers its close that way.
  t/regression/react-body-fed-from-inside-a-supply-block.raku agrees with
  Rakudo 2026.09.
- **An emit into a supply block busy on another thread waits for it**
  (f3ffb5c3, 1979aacc), as Rakudo's emit waits on the supply's lock, and
  the value is handled before emit returns: Cro::WebSocket's handler test
  counted its replies before the last one had been made. The wait lasts as
  long as the holder's body, up to a second; f3ffb5c3's gave up after
  25 ms, which GitHub's macOS runner, loaded, overran on a 3 ms body. Two
  supply blocks on two threads emitting into each other, which deadlocks
  Rakudo, are seen as a circle and queue at once (1979aacc).
  t/regression/emit-waits-for-busy-supply-on-another-thread.raku agrees with
  Rakudo 2026.09; t/regression/supplies-delivering-into-each-other.raku has
  the circle.
- **IO::Socket::Async's `write` and `print` queue their bytes and return
  at once** (c067446c), with a Promise kept once the bytes are out, as
  Rakudo's event loop does. Sent on the caller's thread, a write blocked
  until the peer read, so a client that wrote more than the socket buffers
  hold before it tapped the echo hung for ever. A `close` goes out after
  the writes made before it. t/regression/async-socket-write-queued.raku
  agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.
- **A grammar's actions fire as each subrule completes** (594d4aac), not once
  the parse is over, so the rest of the parse sees what they did: a
  `<?{ $<tag>.made … }>` right after the call reads the action's `.made`,
  and a dynamic variable an action sets is what the next token matches.
  Template::Mustache needs both, to keep the whitespace around a tag that
  shares its line and to switch delimiters on `{{=<% %>=}}`. Each node is
  still built and fired once: the parent's action, a code block's `$/` and
  the finished tree get the Match built when it completed. A parse that
  fails keeps what fired before it failed, and an action that dies ends the
  parse. No action fires inside an LTM probe, as in Rakudo. The JSON grammar
  in tools/bench/grammar-json parses as fast as before (five runs, best: api
  94 → 94 ms, deep 136 → 135, numbers 55 → 55, strings 61 → 59).
  t/regression/grammar-actions-fire-during-parse.raku agrees with Rakudo
  2026.09.
- **A `token` commits to a `regex` it calls** (594d4aac), and to a proto of
  them: when what follows the call fails, the token fails, where it used to
  pull a longer match out of the regex. Template::Mustache's linetag
  stretched a section tag over `{{#a}}{{line}}` and named the section
  "a}}{{line". A `regex` calling a `regex` still backtracks into it.
  t/regression/token-commits-to-regex-subrule.raku agrees with Rakudo
  2026.09.
- **`q+…+`, `qq*…*`, `Q=…=` and `qw^…^` are quotes** (594d4aac): the q family
  takes the punctuation delimiters a bare `m` takes, tight against the
  keyword and closing on the same line, except `; , .` (Left open).
  Template::Mustache's spec tests write their lambdas as `q+{{planet}}+`.
  A `|q` capture parameter is a term, so `q;` after it ends the statement.
  t/regression/q-bare-punctuation-delimiters.raku agrees with Rakudo 2026.09.

  With these five, Template::Mustache 1.2.7's suite passes, all thirteen
  files: `rakupp install` refused it, and with it Pod::To::HTML,
  Raku::Pod::Render, Pod::To::Markdown and App::Assixt, which need it (not
  re-run yet).
- **A Pod block gists as Rakudo's tree of block classes** (5d4de331): its
  class with its config, name, level, caption, type and term, then each of
  its contents two spaces deeper, as in `Pod::FormattingCode{:type("B")}`
  over `bold`, and nested in a list too (`$=pod.gist`). It was the text of
  its contents run together. A declarator block still gists as its text, as
  Rakudo's does. A table with a header row carries `:header-row(0)` in its
  config, as Rakudo's does. Pod::Load 0.7.2's suite looks for
  `Pod::FormattingCode{:type("K")}` in the gist and failed; it passes now,
  and Pod::To::HTML, which needs it, comes next (not re-run yet).
  t/regression/pod-block-gist-is-rakudos.raku agrees with Rakudo 2026.09
  byte for byte.
- **`.DEPARSE` ends an `enum` or `subset` declaration with `;`** (21cd03cd), so
  `.AST.EVAL` of a program with a statement after one runs. Both rendered as
  if they were block statements, with no `;`, and the next statement was a
  second term: `subset Pos of Int where * > 0` then `my Pos $p = 5;` died
  with "Two terms in a row across lines". L10N::BG, L10N::LV, L10N::RU and
  L10N::UK 0.0.2 compile each fragment of their suites with
  `.AST("RU").EVAL`, failed at the subset case, and `rakupp install` refused
  all four; BG and RU were reported, and all four install now. A
  `my subset` and a `my enum` keep their `my`, and `enum C (a => 1)` no
  longer comes back as `enum C ((a => 1))`.
  t/regression/rakuast-declaration-terminator.raku agrees with Rakudo
  2026.08 and 2026.09.
- **`rakupp install Text::CSV` installs, and JSON::Unmarshal with it**
  (42618157). Text::CSV 0.022's suite stopped on seven things, one test
  file after another, and none of them about CSV. `die` of `class D does
  Iterable is Exception` threw an X::AdHoc carrying D: with the role written
  first, Exception was a second parent, which `die` never looked at.
  `open $f, :enc(Str)` died "Unknown string encoding ''"; an undefined
  encoding is the default one. `@p.VAR.name` on a parameter answered "@p"
  (since 1e36359f) where Rakudo names what was bound: the caller's "@x", or
  "element" when nothing, an anonymous array, a default, a copy or a slurpy
  was; Text::CSV chooses arrays or hashes by it. An `is copy` parameter with
  a default stayed read-only when its argument came from a slurpy.
  `@a[1, 4..6, 8..Inf]` padded the endless range with ten thousand (Any)s;
  it is a sub-slice that stops at the array's end. `Supplier::Preserving.new`
  is a Supplier::Preserving to `.^name` and `~~`, not only a Supplier. `use
  Text::CSV; use Slang::Tuxic;` read the file without the slang, because
  Text::CSV's own `use` of it was scanned first. And `.new(a => f())`, `f`
  returning a `.map`, left `has @.a` empty: the constructor copied only what
  the Seq had made so far, which was nothing. That one stopped
  JSON::Unmarshal 0.18's `is unmarshalled-by(&code)` on an array attribute,
  a test dependency the installer had been skipping. An install into an
  empty store now runs and passes all fifteen suites, Text::CSV's 33 files
  and JSON::Unmarshal's 11 among them. Eight files in t/regression hold the
  fixes; seven agree with Rakudo 2026.09, and the slang one uses a fixture
  that does not load there.
- **`class A::B` and `use A::B` declare the package A as well** (3072c3a5).
  Each package a qualified name passes through is a stub made by
  PackageHOW that holds the next part, as in Rakudo: `::('A')` is A,
  `A.WHO<B>` reaches A::B, and a later `class A`, `my class A` or `module
  A` replaces the stub. A bare `A` was "Undeclared name 'A'" and `::('A')`
  a Failure, and naming A before a later `package A {}` was refused as an
  illegal post-declaration. The core namespaces (`X`, `IO`, `Metamodel`, …)
  are left as they were. t/regression/implied-package-of-qualified-name.raku
  agrees with Rakudo 2026.09 in 16 checks, under `--exe`, `--exe -O` and
  the parse cache too.

### Left open

- From issue #77's fixes (9695e120 … aa309270): a role candidate's
  `callsame` still reaches the inner role's candidate it overrides; a
  class's own candidate overrides a role's that differs only by a required
  named; `.^find_method` on Promise, Channel, Supply, Lock, Semaphore,
  Thread and the code types still answers yes for any name, and `.can` on a
  role no; an infix on a Whatever value still curries; a subset's unknown
  trait is ignored, and a runtime subset on a taken name answers it with a
  suffix; a cell bound in a method and returned `is raw` is not the
  attribute's; and a sub the program imports is still visible to a module
  that never imported it (8a6c9d9a hid only operators). TODO-FOLLOW-UPS.md
  has each.
- `=for para` and `=begin para` make a Pod::Block::Para, where Rakudo makes a
  Pod::Block::Named named "para" around one, so their gist differs; and a
  Pod block's `.Str` and `.raku` are still its text, where Rakudo's are its
  class with an address (`Pod::Heading<…>`) and a `.new(…)` (5d4de331). TODO-FOLLOW-UPS.md has
  both.

- `.AST` still drops `is export`, `is default`, `handles` and a `use`'s
  import list (`<foo>`, `:tag`), each into valid Raku meaning something else
  (acf27e4b), and a `start` block renders the `$!` and `$/` its parse
  declares, one more pair each time the text goes round (586c5160; both on
  23e143f2 too). `1 or 0 xor 1` runs, where Rakudo refuses it as
  non-associative (3431c375; on 23e143f2 too). TODO-FOLLOW-UPS.md has each.

- A `token` the packrat memo answers fires its action once, where Rakudo,
  which has no memo, fires it on every call: `<a> 'x' || <a> 'y'` on "12y"
  fires `a` once (594d4aac). `q;…;`, `q,…,` and `q.….` are still not
  quotes, and `~X::AdHoc.new(:payload<boom>)` is the empty string with a
  warning. TODO-FOLLOW-UPS.md has all three.
- A tapper dying on a value from `Supply.interval` or a Channel still quits
  the supply, where Rakudo's scheduler prints it and ends the process: code
  cued on rakupp's scheduler that dies is silent (e9455bfa). A supply that
  quits also tells its tapper before running CLOSE, the other way round
  from Rakudo. TODO-FOLLOW-UPS.md has both.
- A binary still computes at start a constant in an embedded module, in a
  routine or in a class body, and `constant T = now`, since rakupp's
  `Instant.raku` is not exact (946d29e4). TODO-FOLLOW-UPS.md has both.
- A constant's initializer still calls a routine declared below it where
  Rakudo calls an outer one or the builtin, or finds nothing yet inside a
  `try` (f303f0c0). TODO-FOLLOW-UPS.md has it.
- EVAL seeds the caller's operators at the default precedence, so their
  traits are lost inside it, and at equal precedence the left operator's
  associativity decides where Rakudo asks the incoming one; with three
  smaller faults found beside them, TODO.md has each (25781bde, f417ab7e).
- A `for` loop's, `given`'s or `.map`'s alias of a typed array element
  refuses at its write-back, after the rest of the body; `.hyper.map`, `for
  @a.kv -> $k, $v is rw` and `.first` still store anything; and `my Int:D
  @d; @d[0] = Nil` is still taken (17e33a45). A typed scalar's container
  handed out by an expression still takes anything — `($a, my $z) = 'la',
  2`, `($a, 2)[0] = 'l'`, a Pair's value, an `is rw` routine's tail — and a
  `where` clause is not checked through an alias (5f87f445).
  TODO-FOLLOW-UPS.md has each.
- Issue #211's other two reports are open: DBIish's in-memory SQLite
  failing in the app's `init-db`, and Cro's "Parameter type
  SessionStore::Session not allowed on a request unpack parameter", neither
  reproduced without the reporter's code. A module still sees what the
  program imported before it (Cro's traits inside NativeLibs), and a
  signature smartmatch ignores the return type (`:(Int --> Nil) ~~ :(Int)`
  is True, Rakudo False). TODO-FOLLOW-UPS.md has each (0e44c5a6).
- In a metaclass `compose` before its `callsame`, `.^can` and
  `.^find_method` still find a public attribute's accessor, where Rakudo
  finds none yet (8c871a25). TODO-FOLLOW-UPS.md has it.
- `$in.VAR.^name` of a typed `$` parameter says Scalar where Rakudo names
  the type, and `loop(…)` calling a `&loop` parameter is refused as a
  malformed loop (c50eb3b8). TODO-FOLLOW-UPS.md has both.
- A `let` in a routine whose LEAVE dies while it unwinds is not undone,
  where Rakudo restores it (099a2055). TODO-FOLLOW-UPS.md has it.
- A write past a Buf's end grows a buffer of its own, which a second holder
  and a compiled caller do not see, and `$b[1]++` on a Buf element dies with
  X::OutOfRange (18ef8f73). TODO-FOLLOW-UPS.md has both.
- A phaser written last (LEAVE, KEEP, ENTER, FIRST…) still leaves the
  statement before it as the block's or routine's value: `sub f { 42; LEAVE
  { } }` answers 42, Rakudo Nil (642a320a). TODO-FOLLOW-UPS.md has it.
- A Complex still has no `.Version`, `.uniparse` or `.parse-names`, which
  Rakudo's Cool gives it (62a3d9a5).

- `--exe` checks no parameter's sigil, `%` included: a compiled `sub
  p(%h)` given `(a => 1, b => 2)` holds the List, as it did before
  3ddcd58b, and an `@` or `&` parameter takes an Int. `-> @a` in a `for` or a
  `given`, a named `:&c`, `Int %h` and `%h is copy` over a Map still bind
  what Rakudo refuses or copies, and a `for` bound through bindParams slows
  the loop after it. TODO-FOLLOW-UPS.md has each (3ddcd58b).
- Two terms in a row are still refused only in an EVAL (`say 1 1` in a file
  prints 1); a package declaration that starts a statement takes no infix
  after it (`class A {} === A` is refused, Rakudo says True); and a parse
  error in `-e` code or under `-c` shows no excerpt, so no `^`.
  TODO-FOLLOW-UPS.md has the last two, TODO.md's decisions the first
  (2b9494ae).

- `CATCH { when X::Y {} }` with X::Y undeclared is refused only in an EVAL
  (as on daf9c03c), and an enum's type object counts its pairs: `.say for
  Col` says each one, and `Col.elems` and a member's `.elems` are 2 where
  Rakudo says 1. TODO-FOLLOW-UPS.md has both (4af53b07).
- Two types of one name are still one here (50aebf54): a top-level class of
  a core class's own name that inherits it is refused as X::NYI (Rakudo
  accepts it; in a package it works), a top-level class of a core name that
  does not inherit replaces the core type, and two `my class A` in two
  blocks are one class. Types are known by their names throughout.
- `%h.values.head` hands back a value, where Rakudo's is the hash entry's
  container (an Array's `.first`/`.head`/`.tail` do now); a Block's `.gist`
  is `sub { ... }` where Rakudo writes `-> { #`(Block|…) ... }`.

- A store into an array element can still land in a buffer a sibling worker's
  growth has just moved, in a program that races the two (90bc6e23): an
  in-bounds store racing a push crashes about a quarter of runs, and
  ub-indexed-grow's parallel leg is on t/stress's `%known-flaky`. Two fixes
  were prototyped and measured in docs/dev/experiments/STALE-ELEMENT-POINTER.md.
- Issues 2, 4 and 14 still bundle the mainline; 10 (a `use`d module's sub
  costs ~6× compiled) and 21 (no array-element loop lane) are unchanged.
- Found and open, the same on v5.5.0: `sub f(int $x) { $x * 2**62 }`
  answers a BigInt where Rakudo wraps; a `--> num` routine returning a Str
  hands it back where Rakudo dies; a `fail` in a block written at the
  mainline returns from the routine that runs it (Rakudo dies); and
  t/jit/run.raku's tier-up cases calls, elements, inline, methods and
  natives enter no kernel.
- t/exe/fuzz.raku's 20 `*-shadow` programs, which failed at parse time on
  v5.5.0, were not valid Raku (Rakudo refuses them too); the generator now
  writes valid ones, and all 232 programs agree.
- A Failure that builds a Range (`my $r = $f .. 2; @a[$r]`) is still 2
  before any subscript sees it, and one that has thrown is not marked
  handled, so using it again rethrows its exception where Rakudo dies with
  another (85d56001).
- A native element reached through `map` (`@n.map({ $_ = 1.5 })`) or a
  `.pairs` value takes the value as it comes, and so does a native `str`
  variable (`my str $s; $s = 5`). `for @n[0 .. 1]` aliases the elements
  where Rakudo's Range slice of a native array is a copy. `$_ += 1` at
  int64's max through an alias wraps, as a native variable does in Rakudo,
  where Rakudo's element refuses it as too wide. A native element reads as
  a boxed Int: `@n[0] + @n[0]` at 2**62 does not wrap, and `f(@n[0])` picks
  an `Int` candidate over an `int` one (273863ac). Rakudo's literal-index
  `(@u[0] = 2**64 - 1)` answers -1; Raku++ answers 2**64 - 1, as Rakudo does
  under a computed key (960e8aed).
- A `--> int` call's result is a boxed Int to the native arithmetic around it,
  as a native element is: `$s = $s + f($x)` into a `my int $s` at int64's max,
  and `my int $t = @n[0] + @n[1]`, die "Cannot unbox 64 bit wide bigint" where
  Rakudo wraps, interpreted and compiled alike. `@n[0, 1]` on a native array
  is a native array where Rakudo's is a List.
- Reading a top-level loop's header variable where its C++ declaration is
  not in scope fails the `--exe` build with a C++ error (`v_si` undeclared):
  a routine (`sub show { say $i }` beside `loop (my int $i = 0; …)`), or a
  top-level CATCH. The same before these changes (8c570a98).
- Found with issue #202, the same on b85a84ec: a class or subset a module
  declares is reachable from its importer by its short name, exported or
  not and whatever tags were asked for (`use NC; say Hid.^name` answers
  NC::Hid where Rakudo refuses the name). `Mod::EXPORT.^name` is
  `Mod::EXPORT` where Rakudo's is `EXPORT`, and `UNIT::EXPORT` is empty. A
  module that fills `EXPORT::DEFAULT::{…}` in a BEGIN without declaring the
  package writes a global EXPORT::DEFAULT, where Rakudo resolves the name
  inside the module's package. Two packages of one file exporting the same
  name are not X::Export::NameClash.
- Found with issue #216, the same before 5e611fa3: `|MyEnum` still slips
  an enum's four pairs where Rakudo slips the one type object, and `.Hash`,
  `.Map`, `.min`, `.max`, `.rotor` and the Cool string methods (`.chars`,
  `.uc`) on it answer from the pairs or the empty string where Rakudo
  refuses or answers `{}`. `(Int, 1).Str` and a user type before `~`
  (`Foo ~ "x"`) give no uninitialized warning, for any type object.
- Found with issue #215, the same before 67c35490: a kept head over an
  array that `.shift` or `.pop` shortens before it is first read keeps its
  elements, where Rakudo's lazy head reads the shorter array; `.skip`,
  `.reverse`, `.map` and a copy of a kept head still copy the values; and
  `my $x := @a[0]` and `my \x = @a.head` still read their position after
  `@a = …`. TODO-FOLLOW-UPS.md has each.
- A read on another thread while `@a = …` refills the array can still read
  Nil (11 runs in 200 of eight threads reading and refilling) or die: the
  read takes the element's stripe, not the storage's (c9560e0d).
  TODO-FOLLOW-UPS.md has it.
- Found with 42618157, the same before it: inside a nested slice a typed
  array's hole reads Any where Rakudo reads its type (`my Str @t = <a b>;
  @t[0, 1..3]` is `("a", ("b", Str, Str))` there), finite ranges included.
- Found with 89d80faa, the same before it: an anonymous `rule { a b }`
  matches "ab" and not "a   b", the other way round from Rakudo; a named
  `my rule` keeps its whitespace significant.
- Found with 6617859d, the same before it: the interpreter's `$u -= 5` on a
  `my UInt` gives -2, its `op=` check running only when the program declares
  a subset. `loop (my int $k …)` inside a top-level `try { }` fails to
  compile under `-O`: the header's `my` is never declared. (The `where`
  cases found beside them are fixed in d23c2ed8.)
- Found with 0d9ea1ca, the same before it: a list assignment checks none of its
  targets' declarations, interpreted. `my Int $a = 1; ($a, my $b) = $str,
  2` stores the Str, and a `where`, a `:D` and a `:U` are skipped the same
  way, as in `my (Int:D $a, $b) = $int, 2`; Rakudo dies on each. (The parse
  cache's `:D` and `:U`, found with d23c2ed8, are fixed in 0d9ea1ca.)
- Found with 762ce0e2, the same before it, in the interpreter and so in a
  routine that still runs there: a variable's `where` is asked before its
  type, so `my Int $x where * < 10 = 5; $x = "a"` dies X::Str::Numeric where
  Rakudo says X::TypeCheck::Assignment; a Nil resets a `where` variable to
  Any, where Rakudo's is the `<anon>` type; `=` into a variable of
  `subset Pos of Int:D where * > 0` takes the Int type object, which Rakudo
  and compiled code refuse; and `.=` checks neither a variable's type nor
  its `where` (`my Int $x = 5; $x .= Str` holds "5"), where compiled code
  checks both. And under `--exe`, `temp $x = 6` dies
  "Undefined routine 'temp'".
- Found with 116a638e, the same before it: a react's other bodies still run
  beside one another, where Rakudo runs one at a time. Two timers' bodies
  overlap (`whenever Promise.in(0.1) { sleep 0.4; say "c end" }` beside a
  `whenever Promise.in(0.2) { done }` ends the react before "c end"), and so
  do a Supplier's emits from other threads. Waiting for a turn there is not
  safe yet: a Supplier runs a tap's body on the emitting thread holding the
  supplier's mutex, which a body of the same react emitting into it would
  wait for. (A `whenever $promise` body takes its turn since 116a638e.)
- Found with 3072c3a5, the same before it: `GLOBAL::.keys` lists none of
  the program's packages, where Rakudo lists them; a second `module A {}`
  is no redeclaration; `.new` on a package says "No such method 'new'",
  where Rakudo says "You cannot create an instance of this type"; and in
  the Python binding, `dir()` of a package reached as an Object
  (`dir(raku.main.Outer)`) lists Python's own attributes, not the
  package's. TODO-FOLLOW-UPS.md has each.
- Found with 9326ca7e, the same before it, in the interpreter and so in
  compiled code: a placeholder is writable, where Rakudo's is read-only.
  `{ say $^c; $c = 5; say $c }(42)` and the same with `my $c = 5` print
  42 and 5; Rakudo prints 42 and dies "Cannot assign to a readonly variable
  or a value".
- Roast's `S17-promise/nonblocking-await.t` still crashes now and then, after
  its sixth test, where a hundred workers' awaits break at once: one of the
  three Roast runs at the tag. Run alone, one copy at a time beside twelve
  busy cores, it crashed 1 time in 25 on the tag, 2 in 25 on 3a9c1b6f and 5
  in 40 on v5.5.0 (SIGSEGV, SIGABRT or SIGTRAP), so it is not this release's.
  TODO.md has the likely cause: the dying workers each mix into their
  exception and write the interpreter's class table with no lock
  ([ROAST.md](docs/status/ROAST.md#the-one-file-that-flaps)).

## v5.5.0 (2026-10-22) — reference cycles freed, `rakupp --auto`, Alpine and i386, and decimal literals on the fast tiers

Roast: **all 1,425 files** of `spectest.data` and **all 219,207** of its tests
with skip and todo left out (220,845 of 220,845 counting them as passes), in
three runs of three, on Roast `da425eb92` — the same Roast and the same file
list as v5.4.1, archived as v5.5.0.list. The other gates: the compiler agrees
with the interpreter on every `-O` kernel; slim binaries behave identically
on all 1,144 corpus programs that finish (two need a network or a server and
were not judged); the installed-binary gate and t/aot pass, t/aot once its
expectations followed 48223469 (below); the adopters' suites are where v5.4.1 leaves them on this machine (iz4's
t/07-install fails 5 of 32 under both, an install check this machine fails). Not run: the GCC and
MinGW toolchain checks (not on this machine), Raku Koans, the module battery
and the conformance sweep. The local suite passes all 1,744 checks. The module battery, the
documentation examples, the `-O` harness and the ecosystem sweep were not
re-run; their figures in the README and `docs/status/` are v5.4.x's or older,
as labelled.

**Measured on a different machine.** The machine of record, an Apple M3, is
out of reach for now, so this release's benchmarks and gates ran on an Apple
M4. The M4 takes 0.75–1.03× of the M3's time depending on the kernel, so its
milliseconds are not comparable with earlier releases'; ratios are. The same
sitting re-timed two builds the M3 had timed, to tie the two machines
together ([BENCHMARKS.md](docs/status/BENCHMARKS.md#two-machines)), and the
dashboard draws the M4's readings as lines of their own.

### Faster

Against a local v5.4.0 build in the same sitting
([BENCHMARKS.md](docs/status/BENCHMARKS.md#v540-to-v550)): `arrayops` −29%
interpreted and −30% compiled (30.1 → 21.5 ms), `rats` −16% compiled, and
`textsplit` −10%, `sortby` −9%, `sortnums` −9% and `objects` −8% interpreted.
The other rows moved by 3% or less, but for `regex`, which is slower (below).
Figures in the items are instructions retired, base → after, unless they say
otherwise.

- **Float code written with decimal literals runs on the fast tiers**
  (a508cc93). A decimal literal is a Rat, and a Rat beside a Num kept the loop
  kernel, the typed subtree and the fast shape off, so code written the way
  most people write it ran ~60× slower than the same line with `e0` literals.
  `$x = $x * 1.0000001 + 0.000000001`: 3,418 → 57 an iteration (`e0`
  literals: 58); under `--cnp` 6,410 → 57, under `--jit` 3,451 → 58.
  `$x = $x * 1.5` 2,209 → 42.
- **Standard input is read a block at a time** (dc76aa01). Every byte of
  `$*IN` was a virtual `getc` call. 32 MB, 400k ASCII lines: `$*IN.lines` and
  `lines()` 40.0G → 2.90G, `-n` 44.1G → 7.0G, a `$*IN.get` loop 45.5G →
  8.5G, `$*IN.slurp` 57.4G → 0.19G. 27 MB of Latin, Greek, Cyrillic and CJK:
  `$*IN.lines` 43.4G → 11.9G.
- **`try`, `do` and `quietly` run their block in place** (6279175b, 205f5e97)
  instead of building a closure and calling it: `try { $t += $_ }` 6,482 →
  4,915 an iteration, a `try` that catches a `die` −42.7%, `do { so $t }`
  4,837 → 3,266, `quietly { so $t }` 4,706 → 3,136. A body that needs the call
  (`state`, a phaser, CATCH, `when`, `temp`, `@_`, a placeholder) keeps it.
- **A closure is 88 bytes, and closures of one block share their code**
  (f7d4dc67, 6d67f0cd). A Callable was 704 bytes; everything about the code now
  lives in a block every closure of it shares. 100k closures kept: peak 125.6 →
  61.6 MB; a closure made per iteration −3.3%, then −5.0%.
- **Scopes come from the slab, and a body with no `state` gets no state frame**
  (dca29704): `.map({ $_ * 2 })` 3,485 → 3,174 an element, `.grep({ … })`
  −7.3%, `try { }` 7,411 → 6,482, each from one malloc a call to none.
- **A block's placeholders live in its pad** (584f140a): a sort by
  `{ $^a.abs <=> $^b.abs || $^a <=> $^b }` over 100k −12%,
  `.map({ $^x + $^y })` −24% in wall time.
- **`cmp`, `Less` and `== Less` take the short way** (b35718ba): `$o = Less`
  −48.8%, `$o = $x cmp $y` −41.9%, two 100k sorts with `cmp` comparators
  −54.7%; `(@a[3] cmp @a[5]) == More` 200k times ~77 → ~35 ms (Rakudo 2026.09
  ~32).
- **An `our` costs about what a `my` does** (432a3398, 62b8949f):
  `for ^400_000 { our $n += 1 }` 3.35G → 0.93G.
- **Int `div`, `mod`, `%%`, `**`, `gcd`, `lcm`, `min` and `max` on the hot
  path** (a508cc93, 4c78cce6): with the kernels off `div` 2,745 → 1,144 and
  `**` 3,949 → 1,131 an iteration; the loop and sub kernels take `gcd`
  (2,382 → 81) and `lcm` (2,403 → 89). **`1/3` is a small Rat built once**
  (a508cc93): `$r += 1/3` 7,836 → 3,562 with the kernels off.
- **Big-Int gcd is Lehmer's algorithm** (f33a78be), and every Rat and FatRat
  reduces by one: `(3**30_000 + 1) gcd 2**47_500` 0.91 s → 0.8 ms (Rakudo 7
  ms); `FatRat.new(3**300_000 + 1, 2**475_000)` ran for minutes and takes 49 ms.
- **Binding to an array element costs a third to a half less** (e46e839b,
  bb3cc0af, cce315ef): `my $n := @a[3]` 200k times 137 → 89 ms (Rakudo 11),
  and BIND-POS binds the variable's container: a heap in BinaryHeap 2.0's
  shape, 2^16 pushes and pops, 47.7G → 36.5G, peak RSS 200 → 100 MB.
- **Native arrays stay machine words** (6021c2fa, 44ed66bc, ff3fcc5b): a
  2000×2000 `my int @m` filled and summed by `@m[$i; $j]` takes 317 → 34 MB
  and 1.83 → 0.68 s; a 1M-element `my int @a` no longer unpacks for `.elems`,
  `for`, `.sum`, `+=` or `++` (`@a[$i] += 1` 0.16 → 0.04 s, 86 → 9.5 MB).
- **`.sum`, `.min` and `.max` read a plain Int array in place, and
  `STMT for ^N` walks its range** (02fb7c6a): `.max` over 1M Ints, 20 calls,
  0.43 → 0.05 s; `f($_) for ^5_000_000` peaked at 410 MB and now at the block
  form's 9 MB. **`my @d = <fresh list>` takes the list's buffer over**
  (4a1edb88): `my @d = @a »*« @b` over 2M Nums 46 → 13 ms.
- **Small hashes and Int-keyed Pairs allocate less** (c37ce984, 9b3ed082): a
  hash of up to four entries builds no probe index (objects.raku 428,683 →
  228,677 mallocs), and `(^100_000).pairs` −44.7%, 62.9 → 30.6 MB.
- **A bound `&infix:<op>` no longer slows the built-in operators it does not
  spell** (e9841fec): a plain integer loop ran 23.8 ms alone and 93–96 ms
  beside `role R[&infix:<tops>] { }`, BinaryHeap 2.0.3's role parameter.
- **Reaching the per-thread context costs one TLV lookup per function on
  macOS** (f9f0cac1): BinaryHeap's benchmarks −3.9% instructions / −5.1%
  cycles, tools/bench with the kernels off −4.3% / −8.2%; the binary is
  327 KB smaller.
- **Quadratics made linear** (69d1df4d, 9550aecc, cce315ef): a `<?after …>`
  calling a `my token` tried every start position (80 s at 12,000 characters;
  Rakudo 0.11 s) and is bounded by the token's width; `rindex` 200 calls on
  800,000 characters 0.23 → 0.0002 s; `substr-eq` 4.8 → 0.035 s; `m:c($pos)`
  and `:pos` over ASCII text, a 5,000-token tokenizer 29.9G → 0.48G (over
  non-ASCII text still quadratic).
- **`.contains`, `.comb` and `.split` with a regex use the pattern cache**
  (cce315ef): `.split(/\s+/)` 100k times 4.4G → 2.3G.
- **Num and bitwise operators on the fast path, and labelled loops without
  exceptions** (48223469): interpreted, `+&`/`+>` 311 → 3.5 ns an op, three
  labelled `next` 209,490 → 1,962 ns. **Entering a `--cnp` kernel builds no
  frame and hashes no names** (783bc53a): 4,382 → 1,794 instructions an entry.
- **A loop kernel's variable may hold a Bool** (a8c61b84): the flag loop
  `$found = True if $i == 500_000` 1,010 → 47 an iteration.
- **A large program checks and loads in less time and memory** (777f23cf,
  b7ce11f0, 28989c10, 8a027add): `-c` on a generated 150k-line program 826 →
  632 ms and 1,100 → 854 MB; a VarExpr is 400 → 136 bytes; `$*DISTRO` on macOS
  reads the kernel instead of spawning `sw_vers` (`-e '$*DISTRO.is-win'` 40 →
  under 10 ms).

### Slower

Against the same v5.4.0 build, three interleaved rounds of perf-guard on the
M4 put the call path 4–9% behind, with no overlap between the two builds'
rounds: `junctionwide` +8.6%, `subcall` +7.3%, `strpass` +7.2%, `regexloop`
+5.7%, `privmeth` +4.5%, `method` +4.0%. Four of them are over the gate's 5%
tolerance, and they are **accepted for this release, not fixed**. The costs
the commits admitted when they landed do not add up to it — `subcall` +1.7%
for every routine's own `$_` (cce315ef), `method` +0.5% (f7d4dc67), the method
kernels +0.7–0.9% (dca29704), the cycle collector's `objects` +1.3–1.5%
(7f40dd87), `$x andthen $_ = $_ + 0` +17.5% now that it writes through
(4af50b85) — so the rest is not found yet
([TODO.md](docs/dev/plans/TODO.md)). `strscan` is 10.8% faster, and
`loadtime`, `objnew`, `substrloop` and `sharedappend` 5–7%.

`regex` is 6% slower interpreted and 7% compiled. With the loop raised
tenfold, so that startup no longer counts, both modes lose the same 4.0% a
match, so the cost is in the regex runtime they share; it arrived in steps of
about 1% along `main`, not in one commit, and the rest is about 0.1 ms more
startup ([TODO.md](docs/dev/plans/TODO.md)).

The perf-guard baseline is still the one recorded on the M3 for v5.2.0.
Absolute times do not carry between machines, and the M4 refused a record at
load 2.7 (three kernels' runs spread more than the tolerance), so it was not
re-recorded; the comparison above is an A/B on one machine instead.

### New

- **Objects that refer to each other are freed** (#151; 7f40dd87, 16c15bf0).
  Reference counting stays; a backup cycle collector beside it finds groups
  whose counts only explain each other and breaks them, at scope exits and
  compiled loop iterations, on the main thread while no worker is live. The
  #151 loop freed 0 of 320,000 objects and grew 238 → 1,882 MB; it now frees
  319,078 and grows 10 → 11 MB (Rakudo 2026.09 frees 319,252). Every call of a
  routine that declares a `my sub` or keeps a block in a variable leaked its
